# Workbench development record — entries 7.119 onwards

Earlier entries: [7.101–7.118](7.101-7.118.md). [Index](../frontend_implementation_status.md).

### 7.119 Feasibility-only benchmark follow-up: fixed bias, completed calibration and full final-tree suite (2026-09-30, Astra, user calibration decisions)

The user fixed scoring to the upstream pass rule, all objective metrics descriptive, and bias currents at the
benchmark starting values for every arm. The importer and comparison driver enforce those decisions; all Probes
remain, and `best_feasible` reports every metric only for a feasible design. The deviation and each fixed current
are in `artifacts/local/llm-sizing/plan-benchmark.json`. The workbench supplies native `mf`/`mult` count feature
columns consistently to validation, the learner and the archive; no current support or optimizer-package edit was
made. All ten input domains were checked, and a free ACBC SAC smoke completed five candidates / three updates.

**G9:** 80 full trials (ten circuits, five random and three pattern seeds, 60 candidates each), 4,800 search launches:
4,711 OK, 88 nonconverged and one backend error. Zero passing designs in either baseline; no circuit meets the
unchanged selection rule. Every best-feasible field is null. The harness independently re-simulates every GATOR pass
using both original upstream decks, compares all metrics at relative 1e-9 without an absolute floor and requires the
same verdict; any disagreement stops all workers. No pass occurred, so no positive recheck ran and positive parity
remains unqualified. The original 205-design parity sweep also had zero passes. Offline tests exercise both positive
checks and immediate stop on disagreement. No targets or rewards were relaxed; deterministic pattern seed labels
are explicitly not independent trajectories.

Interrupted attempts remain separate from the 80 full trials: 25 later attempts contain 785 candidate records,
782 confirmed launches and 24 uncertain launch allowances; the first interrupted batch has 19 recorded launches
and an unknown in-flight count. Nine additional free smoke candidates are separate. A host/session interruption
removed /tmp scratch and benchmark dependencies; the exact public commit, evaluator hashes and existing ngspice-47
were restored, while all frozen imports stayed byte-identical. Retained JSON evidence survives; lost raw scratch
and uncertain costs are disclosed. Completed trials were reused; interrupted trials received full same-seed
replacements, with their prior costs retained. No evaluator disagreement was bypassed.

**Read-only current finding:** a custom `current` FeatureSchema column at reference 1e-6 A is accepted by PyG and by
SAC as a static device feature, preserving `dc` writeback and the old default schema. A tunable `current/A` group
still fails the hard-coded unit gate in `models/inputs.py`; the schema alone cannot add current actions. No such
implementation was built. Proof: `current-feature-schema-readonly.json` in the report directory.

**G10 follow-up:** tests intercept the actual schema files sent through both Codex entry points before any process
launch. Both sizing variants pass strict object checks. The unchanged Agent-tab schema has 16 structural issues
and one explicit expected-failure test; the required closed shapes, required properties and nullable absence
handling are listed in `docs/llm-sizing.md` and `codex-strict-schema-audit.json`. The historical unauthorized,
HTTP-400-rejected Codex request still makes G10 fail. No new real model request, bridge or Spectre call was made;
the live agent-audit hash is unchanged. Paid slices remain unapproved.

**Review 7.118:** F2–F4 are fixed: recovery with transition archives off caps warm-start admissions to restored replay;
failure context is bounded/redacted and license text withheld, including restored history; malformed hint requests
return HTTP 422. The suite caught a ContinueBody field accidentally displaced while adding HintBody; corrected
before the successful full rerun. F1 (ulp drift evading exact duplicate checks on continuous rows) remains open
under the no-package-change constraint, so G6 is partial. The optimizer digest stays `27a36b2b74ec29f69bf916a8d6079b2cca78dea07fd9887d939211fe070d2f49`.

**G8:** full final-tree suite, 1,581 passed / one expected Agent-schema failure / zero failures, 202.64 s, six workers.
All 311 source/test/build-config file hashes match the suite-start manifest. Focused follow-up: 86 passed / one
expected failure. G1's repeated seven-table comparison has zero differences. Existing frontend evidence remains;
this follow-up changed no frontend code, did not rebuild the live bundle or restart 8765, and made no commit.
Reports: `artifacts/local/llm-sizing/implementation-report.md`, `acceptance-summary.json`, `calibration/summary.json`.
The 78 kB open log was rolled over into `7.101-7.118.md` with its bytes unchanged and its old name kept as a pointer;
this file is the new open record, reflected in the index and CLAUDE.md.

### 7.120 Audit finding F1 fixed: the LLM duplicate check compares sizes to a tolerance (2026-09-30, session "RL reward design", user: "fix the four", then chose "After Astra finishes" for the landing)

**State found.** Of the four findings of 7.118, F2–F4 were already fixed in the tree by Astra (7.119). Re-checked
here: 24 of the audit's 25 tests passed on the tree of 15:19, the one failure was F1, which 7.119 left open under its
no-package-change constraint (G6 partial). This entry closes it.

**F1.** `src/analog_optimizer/search/llm.py`: the exact-float key (`design_key`, a set of tuples) is replaced by
`same_design(a, b)`: same group names and every size within `rel_tol=1e-9` (`abs_tol=0`); `LlmProposer.seen` is a
list of size sets. Seven places in that one file (9 lines out, 10 in); nothing else in the package. It covers the four sources as before: evaluated designs
(history and `observe`), queued ones, accepted ones still in flight, and earlier candidates of the same reply. Why a
comparison and not a rounded key: `legalize(encode(v))` drifts by a few ulp on a group without a grid step, and a
rounded key still misses whenever the drift straddles a rounding boundary; the comparison cannot, and it costs at most
evaluations × groups per candidate (early exit on the first differing group) next to a model call of many seconds.
1e-9 is six orders above the drift and far below any grid step or physical meaning. `docs/llm-sizing.md` states the
rule in place of the deferral note.

**Tests.** New `tests/workbench/test_llm_audit_fixes.py` (20): 1,000 evaluated MOCK designs repeated exactly as the
prompt prints them are all refused (the 295 of them that drift were accepted before); the baseline design, a queued one,
an observed one and a same-reply one are each refused while a design 1e-6 away is accepted and a row with other group
names never matches; end to end, exact copies stay refused after a resume and no design is simulated twice. With the
old file swapped in, exactly these three fail. The other 17 state properties the audit verified and no test held:
the encode round trip (exact on a grid, within 1e-12 without one) and its refusals, every malformed reply shape, the
notes rule, hints and notes as JSON data behind the rules, the cost ceiling with the remaining budget handed to the
CLI, one call record per request, a receipt whose evaluation never committed, the run-review digest of a plain SAC run.

**Verification.** Built in a scratch copy of the tree (15:19) while Astra's calibration ran: suite 1,577 passed /
24 skipped (no evidence folder there) / 1 xfailed; G1 (seeded MOCK SAC, committed tree vs fixed copy) 0 differences
over the seven tables. Landed at 17:29 with a guarded script (`artifacts/local/reviews/llm-audit-f1/apply_f1.py`:
refuses any package state but `27a36b2b…`), after the calibration and Astra's finalize had ended and the tree had
been untouched since 15:47, no run in flight. The tree's code, tests, frontend sources and examples are
byte-identical to the tested copy. Full suite on the tree: **1,601 passed, 1 xfailed** (7.119's 1,581 + these 20). A
first run at low priority (`nice 19`) had one failure in a wall-clock overlap assertion of
`test_native_preparation.py`, which imports nothing of the search package and passed 10 of 10 alone and in the second
run. Evidence: `artifacts/local/reviews/llm-audit-f1/` (patch, script, three suite logs, the G1 comparison).

**Optimizer digest** `27a36b2b74ec29f69bf916a8d6079b2cca78dea07fd9887d939211fe070d2f49` →
`100945fb780852ea3776a2395f83474bd208ed2f1ee5a27aff7cdf28b0d2651b`. What that leaves behind: Astra's evidence in
`artifacts/local/llm-sizing/` describes the tree before this entry and was not edited - the acceptance summary (G6

A checkpoint or AI-search save written at `27a36b2b…` refuses exact resume; none of record exists (the output root has

### 7.121 Audit of the fixed-bias calibration (7.119) and the final LLM-sizing tree (2026-09-30, session "LLM+RL solutions and sizing support", user: "audit")

**Verdict.** The code is sound and the tree is green. The calibration's zero passes is real, but it comes mostly from
one spec, and partly from my own advice to hold the bias currents fixed. Report:
`artifacts/local/reviews/fable-audit-llm-calibration.md`. Evidence beside it: a read-only analysis script over the 80
`result.json` files and the upstream YAMLs, and the suite log.

**Verified.**
- GATOR scores exactly upstream's pass rule (`AmpEnv._get_info` `all_targets_satisfied`, commit `10093e3`): nine hard
  specs with the same thresholds, and the other six metrics inactive.
- The four fixes of 7.118 are in the tree.
- The digest `100945fb…` is the same in the tree and the snapshot, and no code or test file changed after 17:30.
- Full suite on a snapshot: 1,577 passed, 24 skipped (they need `artifacts/`), 1 xfailed, 0 failed.

**Findings.**
- C1: the settling-time target (at most 1 us) was met by 0 % of designs in six circuits, at most 1.9 % in three, and
  33.8 % in `amp_raffc`. `amp_az` had 59 designs that met eight of the nine specs, with settling the only miss every
  time (its fastest design settled in 15 us).
- C2: the bias currents held at the benchmark's starting values make settling unreachable in four circuits:
  `amp_tcfc` 1.25 uA, `amp_clia` 0.63 uA, `amp_nmcnr` 4 uA and `amp_dacfc` 5 uA, each out of up to 30 uA. A tunable
  current needs one kind/unit entry in `models/inputs.py` plus a workbench component-list kind at 1 uA. It is best
  bundled into the uncommitted package step.
- C3: the three pattern-search runs per circuit were identical, so 1,200 of the 4,800 simulations were repeats.
- C4: the median design took 162 s under the parallel load (about 20 s alone); the calibration took about 288
  design-hours.
- C5 (known from 7.120): Astra's evidence still names digest `27a36b2b…`. It must be refreshed before the commit,
  which needs hunk picking.

**Not done.** No code change and no commit. The lower 7.118 findings (F5-F8) and the Agent tab's Codex-schema gap
are unchanged.

### 7.122 Comparison protocol: longer equal budgets, constraint progress and every-pass verification (2026-09-30, Astra, user's four benchmark revisions)

The old requirement that pattern search pass within 60 evaluations is removed, together with selection on short
random-success counts. B3 domain/import/parity failures still exclude a circuit. All ten qualified amplifiers stay
eligible; the first three alphabetically (ACBC, AFFC, AZ) form the planned cohort and ACBC is the pilot. The completed
80 runs/4,800 candidates remain byte-for-byte evidence under the old protocol. A separate progress reanalysis records
all candidate/per-spec violations and source hashes without any new benchmark simulation.

The brief and plan use a provisional 2,000-candidate budget for every arm, including resets and failures, with
17/29/43 seeds and no first-feasible early stop. The driver requires the shared plan, validates its candidate budget,
rejects competing wall/launch limits and serializes arms with an output-root lock. Per-run outputs show uncapped
constraint trajectories, the best worst-spec violation from one valid design, baseline improvement and reached
checkpoints; invalid designs cannot improve the incumbent, and unreached checkpoints stay null. Group summaries
retain unsuccessful/early-ending runs in success denominators and report budget completion, without pooling unlike
circuits, providers/models, budgets, steering or fallback states.

A benchmark-only engine observer runs after durable evaluation receipt and before retention/next dispatch. Every
pass (including reset/repeat) is checked in both original upstream decks at 1e-9 relative/zero absolute tolerance
and exact verdict agreement. A mismatch, false-negative verdict or unresolved check persists a STOP marker, ends
the run and blocks later arms. Benchmark-marked runs refuse an engine without the verifier. Verification results
never enter learning or AI feedback; their launches/time are separate and included in total cost. No actual positive
parity has yet been observed. The Agent-schema known gap and historical G10 incident remain.

**Validation:** final full suite 1615 passed, 1 expected failure, 0 skips/failures/errors; source manifest matches
all 314 files. The earlier suite's 4 ngspice PATH skips are preserved and resolved in this final run.
34 focused protocol/calibration tests passed. Existing UI contracts are unchanged. No optimizer edit by this
revision; peer F1 digest `100945fb780852ea3776a2395f83474bd208ed2f1ee5a27aff7cdf28b0d2651b` is reflected in refreshed acceptance evidence. Implementation commits
`4634200` / `7c7a0ff` were made separately; this revision makes no commit, live rebuild/restart, new benchmark run,
paid call, bridge or Spectre call. The ignored pasted suggestions did not change the fixed-current policy.
Report: `artifacts/local/llm-sizing/protocol-v2/report.md`. LLM-only's larger proposed call ceiling is explicitly
unapproved (at least 500 full k=4 batches per 2,000-evaluation run; proposed cap 550 including repairs).

**Commit.** `d8b8c4f` (10-02 21:46, pushed), with every other finished change in the tree, on the user's
"commit everything and push" in the session of record 7.139.

### 7.123 Verify the committed duplicate fix and deploy the sizing UI (2026-09-30, Codex, user's four remaining sizing tasks)

The current tree already contained F1's tolerant duplicate comparison in optimizer commit `4634200`, with the
four-mode workbench in `7c7a0ff`. No optimizer or application source was changed by this follow-up. Its regression
coverage includes continuous round-trip drift, history/queue/observed/same-reply repeats, distinct designs and resume.
The later comparison-protocol revision and other sessions' pending changes were preserved without committing them.

**Validation.** The first full suite had 1,614 passes, one expected Agent-tab schema failure and one failure in
`test_hung_lane_keeps_peer_transition_and_reports_unknown_job`: the non-hung peer missed its 0.6-second deadline
while browser/build work also ran. The complete runtime-recovery module passed all 13 tests alone; the full suite
rerun, without those concurrent jobs, passed **1,615 tests, one expected failure, zero skips or unexpected failures**.
Both original and rerun logs are retained. Three scratch browser scripts (`llm_sizing`, `run_control`,
`run_control_live`) passed **123 checks**, no failures or retries. `npm run build` passed both TypeScript and Vite,
writing first to a staging directory. The deployment checked all 377 recorded source hashes before switching.

**Deployment.** No live run or backend child request was active. Port 8765's backend stopped gracefully and was
restarted using the same command, working directory and inherited environment; remembered settings and allowed
roots matched afterwards. The prior `frontend/dist` was backed up; old hashed assets were retained for existing
browser tabs, and the new index was published last. Served HTML matched the staged build and its assets returned
HTTP 200. A fresh read-only browser rendered exactly SAC, LLM warm start → SAC, LLM only and SAC with LLM rescue,
with no JavaScript errors. Its model/simulator/settings-write requests were blocked by interception.

**Remaining evidence.** No model response or sizing-effectiveness claim is added. Local CLI help confirms the
required flags; the prepared section-7a proposal is at most two Fable 5.1 calls (proposed $2 notional ceiling) and
two Astra calls, both high effort, no simulations, and is explicitly unapproved. Codex cost is unavailable and
its call count is the enforceable limit. The separate comparison remains unrun with its provisional common
2,000-candidate budget; calibration evidence is unchanged. New paid calls, benchmark candidates, bridge calls and
Spectre calls: **zero**. No commit or push was made in this follow-up.

Evidence: `artifacts/local/llm-sizing/deployment-20260930/` (`deployment.json`, source manifest, full-suite logs/JUnit,
browser logs, `live-ui-result.json`, `live-ui.png`, previous bundle, CLI-help check and unapproved proposal).

**Commit.** `d8b8c4f` (10-02 21:46, pushed), with every other finished change in the tree, on the user's
"commit everything and push" in the session of record 7.139.

### 7.124 Specs: a compact tick-box column, the objective inside the Constraint list, "Judged at" only with training corners (2026-09-30, same session as 7.113; user: "keep probe and spec table separate. Make the first column tickbox column more compact. isnt objective column conflict with previous constraint settings? shouldnt the objective also be merged into constraint? isnt the judged at column conflict with reward and conditions settings?")

Probes and Specs stay two tables (the user's choice). Page only: the saved configuration keeps its fields and format.

1. **Tick-box column.** The row-selection box inherited the 84 px minimum width of every editor input; `th.sel` /
   `td.sel` now size the column to the box (20 px measured). All editor tabs.
2. **The objective is part of the constraint list.** Limit and objective never meant opposite things - the limit
   decides pass or fail, the objective ranks the designs that pass (and adds a reward bonus only when "Objective bonus
   weight" is above 0) - but two selects allowed pairs that can never work (`>=` with minimize: the backend's
   `objective_without_limit`). The Specs "Constraint" cell is now one list of the eight pairs that can: `≥ lower`,
   `≥ lower, then higher is better`, `≤ upper`, `≤ upper, then lower is better`, `interval` alone or with either
   objective, `target ± tolerance`. One choice writes `constraint` + `direction_after_feasible`, one Undo step. A stored
   pair the list does not offer is shown as it is, and validation names it. The Objective column is gone; the
   Constraint header's help says what the second half means. "At most one objective" (`multiple_preferences`) is
   unchanged. Found while verifying: the unchanged limit sent with the objective made the scale follow as if the limit
   had been edited, freezing an empty (backend-sized) scale to the suggestion (0.95 in the fixture). The scale now
   follows only a field whose value changes.
3. **"Judged at" only with training corners.** `nominal_only` chooses nominal or every planned corner for one spec; the
   reward settings decide how violations across those conditions combine, so the two do not conflict, but the choice
   acts only when Training conditions is not "Nominal only" (`run.conditions.policy != off`; only the real OTA7 task
   has corners). Without corners the column is hidden, values kept; it returns when corners are planned.

`docs/workbench.md` (the reward-v2 paragraph) says so.

**Verification.** Clean copy of HEAD `ad90a45` + these files: `tsc --noEmit` clean, Vite build.
`spec_equations_check.mjs` **25/25** (7 new: tick-box width, no Objective column, the offered pairs, one choice saves
both fields and leaves an empty scale empty, one Undo takes both back, Judged at hidden without corners and shown with
them); against the build before the scale fix the empty-scale check fails (0.95). Its "no writing request" check now
counts the connection test the page makes once on opening (7.80) as refused and harmless, like the output-folder
look-up: on a backend with a connection profile (the live one) it failed on that. `task_rows_check.mjs` hermetic
**42/42**. Astra's `reward_v2_frontend_check.mjs`: its Specs lines follow the new controls. Run against a COPY of
`20260926-qualification-05/stage-b` (report and configuration paths pointed at the copy - the page's folder check writes
a probe file into its output folder) with a copy of that qualification's bridge profile: **23 checks pass**, all Specs
lines included; it stops at "actual checkpoint initially resume compatible", which fails without the page too - asked
directly with the untouched configuration, the backend reports the 09-26 checkpoint not resumable (reward v2.1 added
`nominal_gate_band` + `objective_shape`, spec input schema v2, optimizer digest 3473ed9c -> 100945fb, SAC settings);
the check needs a checkpoint from the current optimizer to run end to end. The evidence's stage-b listing (1,771
entries, sizes and times) is identical before and after. The page's connection test on opening reached the VM twice
through the scratch backend (read-only; tunnel, daemon and session OK; nothing started).

Live: the page reaches 8765 only after a `frontend/dist` build; backend unchanged, no restart. No commit.

Committed on the user's "commit and push" as `54153dd`, together with 7.125 (pushed: 6e95547..54153dd). Live since
the 23:12 build (see 7.125).

### 7.125 Frontend audit and its fixes: an honest real-run question, the Agent tab's message box, Import JSON, Save task with the Virtuoso window closed, stale answers, fewer requests (2026-09-30, same session as 7.113; user: "audit the frontend to see anything to optimize", then chose all four groups and "One step per cell edit")

**Audit.** Five read-only reviewers (left column; right column and graph; agent, connection and pop-ups; app core and
run panels; editors, styles and build), every finding checked by me in the code, and the page measured in headless
Edge on a scratch backend (`measure.mjs`, `visual.mjs`, `bundle.cjs`: request counts, React components rendered per
commit through a DevTools-hook stub, storage writes, script time). Report with file:line and fix sizes:
`artifacts/local/reviews/frontend-audit-2026-09-30.md` (+ folder with screenshots and the scripts). The page itself is
cheap - idle 0 requests, a keystroke 3-5 ms of script, main JS 140 kB gzip - so memoization and bundle splitting were not
proposed. Six serious findings, three of them seen in the browser first: the Agent tab's message box at 0 px while an
AI run's thread is shown; a 20-row import confirmation 3,404 px tall with Apply out of reach; importing
`configs/gpdk045_conditions.json` (it has a `schema_version`) blanked the page on Apply.

**Fixed (26 source files, +464/-210 lines, many of them comments; 6 check scripts).**
1. Serious: `launchWithConsent` asks a real-authorization refusal in real words built from the backend's report, never
   the page's MOCK text (D1); the SizingThread gets a grid row of its own (`:has()` in styles.css, C1); Import JSON takes
   a whole configuration only for `schema_version` `gator-workbench-config-v1` with its sections, rows need their ID,
   the confirmation scrolls with Apply / Cancel first (E1, E2); the first-save TaskSave renders with the column, not in
   the Virtuoso window's portal (A1); the header lets go of its run after two misses in a row, with one re-read after
   the first (D2).
2. Stale displays and lost input: the saved-model facts stay while the same model is re-checked, only the verdict waits
   (record 7.96's rule; B1); AI number boxes show the default as a placeholder and never store null (B2); one shared
   Agent-settings read that the Agent tab's save updates (api.ts `agentSettingsShared`, B3/C4); SizingThread keeps its
   thread across status blips, retries a failed read, numbers calls by `call_number`, "Add hint" has a busy state and
   the hint text lives in AgentTab (C3, C5); graph head clamped (B4) and destroyed on unmount (B10); corner policy
   switch keeps the corners (B9); output-panel problem cleared per root, Browse answers guarded (B11); default model
   guarded (B5); import-cell race and selects locked while reading (A2); one gate message (A3); rows untouched when they
   match the starter read at the choice, ngspice labels from the save answer (A5/C8); Check now answer tied to its rows
   (A6); the copy question in the Virtuoso window (A7); Load task guarded (A10); TaskSave name reset (A11); Matched
   devices answer cleared after Apply, honest launcher tooltip (A12); approvals close only their own proposal and say
   when edits were replaced (C9); the selection list scrolls once (C6); connection busy state per connection, the
   on-open test through `forChosen` (C7); "Copied" only when copied (C12); an empty-diff import opens no box (C13);
   Run status "Reading the run..." / the read error (D6); the run list error is said (D7); Continue busy (D9); Refresh
   keeps a table until its new answer (D10); the unsaved change is written on `pagehide` unless another tab stored
   meanwhile, and "fresh browser" is decided at mount (D14); banners: plain sentence first, code under "Technical
   details", `no_answer` instead of `schema: Failed to fetch` (D15); the strip left of "Runs..." shows the live state
   (D5); tick boxes of rows that are gone go (E3); issues drawn only on the row they were found for (`validation.sent`,
   E6); "(not set)" for a missing required choice (E7); exact numbers in diffs (E8); Task file / ngspice window CSS
   (E5); row blockers unboxed (E9); one `table { font: inherit }` for every pop-up (E10).
3. Requests: SizingThread reads only while visible and polls only live AI runs (30 requests per 30 s of a SAC run
   before, 0 after); connection list and Agent settings read once (`connectionProfilesShared`); no task reload per
   Labels keystroke, loads within one task settle 0.4 s; the Virtuoso schematic read waits 0.4 s after the first
   (A4, A8); no model check in scratch (B7); saved-model list follows the run list only (B6); a finished run in the
   tabs is not polled at 1.5 s while the header pauses another (D12, one line of 7.115's hunk - its session agreed);
   Logs reads a resting run once (D13); no task reload after a refused Start (taskEpoch); PDK list read once after
   Load, sorted once per PDK (A13, A9); the run list is built only while its window is open.
4. Cleanups: AgentTab uses `usePopupWindow` (+`close()`, C11); dead `manifest.search_settings` condition (B12), dead
   'Objective' label of 7.124 (E12), duplicate `refreshTasks`, unused `Column.numeric`, the unused PDK `refresh` prop.
5. Undo (user choice): changes typed into one field less than 1.5 s apart are one step (`typingField`, store.ts); leaving
   the field ends it.

Not done, said so: D11 (duplicate reads at poll-interval changes - a fix would delay noticing a run that came alive; one
6 ms read each); memoization (D3, E11, B8, C10 - measured 2-5 ms); "Copy path" as a button (the user's own choice);
opening "Network structure" keeps reading on each opening (a press; the per-edit re-reads are gone with B1); a run
still in state `created` when a page opens is not adopted (rare).

### 7.126 The optimization review's package changes landed: corners two at a time, every 5th reset simulated, Maestro results not parsed (2026-09-30, same session as 7.115; user: "review and see anything to optimize", chose the three package changes and "Build now in a copy", then "Land now" and "Mine only")

**What a real run does differently** (MOCK runs are unchanged; the details are in workbench.md, "How a real run spends
its simulations"):
- A candidate's corner simulations run two at a time after its nominal one when the circuit is measured from Spectre's
  own results (today `real:gpdk045_ota7`). Rows, stops and launch accounting match one-at-a-time. Maestro tasks keep one
  at a time.
- A lane simulates the unchanged starting design at its first reset, every 5th reset and after any failure. The other
  resets reuse the last simulated one and cost nothing (event `episode_reset_reused`).
- A Maestro task's Spectre writes its own binary results; the bridge no longer parses a text copy nobody reads.

  Astra's package was committed (4634200) and with no Astra job running.

### 7.127 The form writer types a transistor's length before its width, so GPDK045's well-proximity values follow the final length; MScR ring3 mended (2026-09-30, same session as 7.115; found by the ring3 check of 7.126, the cause found by the session "MScR Virtuoso circuit library"; user: "Re-save the task")

**The fault.** GPDK045's width callback `gpdk045_mosCB('w)` computes the well-proximity values `sca`, `scb` and `scc`
from the length the transistor has at that moment, and the length callback never recomputes them. `cdf_write.write_skill`
typed each instance's fields in the order of the task's rules: width, then length. So:
- every candidate of a task that moves both a transistor's width and its length was simulated with the values of the
  previous candidate's length (every MScR task except `mscr_ota7t`, which writes m and l; the effect on results is
  small);
- a restoration put the widths and lengths back but left the values of the last candidate's length. The adapter compares
  the live cell with the task's record when it connects, so the next run refused to start ("Restoration mismatch:
  MN1.sca"), as on `real:mscr_ring3` in 7.126.

My first reading in 7.126 (the recorded values were stale) was wrong. The MScR session showed on throwaway `nmos1v`
cells that typing the length first gives exactly the recorded values.

**The fix.** `write_skill` sorts each instance's fields so that a length comes first. The sort is stable, so held values
(the finger count GPDK045 folds when a wide width is typed) still come after what they correct. A first attempt, running
every callback once more after the rounds, re-folded the finger count in the fake Virtuoso's fold test and was dropped.
- New test `test_the_width_callback_sees_the_final_length_in_a_candidate_and_in_the_restoration`: a fake with GPDK045's
  dependency. It fails on the old writer (72.22222 where 54.16667 belongs) and passes now.
- Second part (the MScR session's follow-up): only the width, finger width and finger count callbacks recompute the
  values, so a task that writes lengths and holds the widths (`mscr_ota7t`: m and l; `mscr_ldo_basic`'s pass device)
  ran every candidate with the build's values (about 0.4 mV of Vth between 45 and 60 nm on ring3's NMOS, by that
  session's estimate). A round that types a length now types the width again at its own value (`lengthTyped`); a
  width-only write is unchanged, so the fold test still passes. New test
  `test_a_written_length_types_the_held_width_again_so_the_values_follow_it` (fails without it); the file has 28
  passed. A 10-evaluation `mscr_ota7t` run with it completed, and its cell equals the record in every field afterwards.
- Full suite on the live tree (every session's uncommitted work, 7.126 and this fix included): 1,633 passed, 1
  expected failure. One timing test of `test_runtime_recovery.py` failed at load 60 and passed 3 times out of 3
  alone.

**ring3 mended:**
- 23:31: Save task (rev 3), the user's choice made on my wrong reading, recorded the wrong values. An old-code run of the
  7.126 check then moved them again.
- 23:40: the fixed writer, in two transactions under the session lock (every transistor width one grid step up, then
  back to the rev-2 record): the cell equals rev 2 in every field (`sca` 72.26287 and 56.99756 again). Script:
  `artifacts/local/reviews/mend_ring3_2026-09-30.py`.
- 23:41: Save task again (rev 4). Its readback is byte-identical to rev 2's.
- 23:43: a 10-evaluation run with another seed completed; the restoration check passed, and afterwards the cell equals
  rev 4 in every field.
- Backups: `artifacts/local/reviews/mscr_ring3-before-resave-20260930T233026` (rev 2) and
  `mscr_ring3-rev3-before-resave-20260930T234122` (rev 3).

**Not done yet:**
- Committed in `e2584fa`.
- The other five MScR tasks have not run. Their records hold the values the build computed, which the fixed writer keeps.

### 7.130 Frontend audit follow-up: autosave recovery, encoder races, bounded reads and narrow windows (2026-10-01, Codex, user: "go on")

The verified audit findings are fixed. Normal sandbox execution still failed before starting a command; approved shell
execution could read and edit the whole workbench, so this was not an optimizer-only model restriction. No app settings
were changed. Existing shared edits, including App's status-poll intervals, were preserved.

- `store.ts` only clears the dirty flag after localStorage succeeds. Failed saves remain unsaved, including for the
  pagehide retry; the header offers Retry save and Export JSON for the current in-memory configuration. Successful
  saving clears the warning. Pagehide still avoids replacing another tab's stored configuration.
- `Right.tsx` ties encoder settings and preset replies to the selection, revision and expected network/advanced values.
  A newer choice, edit or Undo wins. Preference writes and resets are ordered so an older save cannot undo a reset.
- `api.ts` and `store.ts` bound JSON GETs and hook reads at 30 seconds. Owned fetches receive cancellation; Refresh
  replaces an outstanding hook read. Late callbacks that ignore abort are discarded, polling stays single-flight,
  and interval changes retain the result and current request. Shared cached GETs have their own deadline. Writes and
  streaming agent calls are not automatically retried. Failed validation blocks starting; log, output-folder and
  model-structure read errors offer a retry.
- App memoizes its validation signature; Editors skips unchanged inputs and groups validation issues once by row.
  An unrelated bottom-tab switch now performs zero whole-configuration serializations (one in the audit reproduction).
- At <=1,100 px the editors get a full-width row; at <=700 px the side panels stack around them. The page scrolls,
  tables scroll horizontally inside their panel, and Agent areas stack with useful heights. At 640 px the editor
  measured 613 px (zero before), the message box 580 px; at 960 px, 933 px and 541 px. Neither page overflowed
  horizontally. The 640 px screenshot was visually inspected.
- Agent tests explicitly seed their provider, restore settings, and run in the separate serial writer lane. The runner
  waits for Windows to reach its scratch backends: WSL curl answered before Windows localhost forwarding on the first
  attempt, stopping four scripts before their assertions. The typing test now verifies actual cancellation of the
  superseded validation fetch; the lifecycle fixture still deliberately delivers late replies that ignore cancellation.

Verification: TypeScript clean. Broad browser pass: 13 scripts, 579 assertions, zero failures/flaky reruns, logs in
`C:/Users/38094/AppData/Local/Temp/gator-checks-2026-09-30T23-13-40-559Z`. After final read-error UI and added Undo/
cancellation cases: reliability 20, request_lifecycle 25, output_panel 61, files_tab 18, corner_check 37 = 161 assertions,
all passed; logs/screenshots in `C:/Users/38094/AppData/Local/Temp/gator-checks-2026-09-30T23-16-37-777Z`. Across the two
passes, 15 distinct scripts passed. `git diff --check` clean. New regression: `frontend/scripts/reliability_check.mjs`;
extended controlled React StrictMode fixture: `frontend/scripts/fixtures/request_lifecycle.tsx`.

Temporary builds, backend settings and browser profiles only. No optimizer/backend source changes by this work,
real simulation, paid agent call, commit or live frontend deployment.

**Commit.** `f3fe825` (10-01 03:25, pushed), by the session "Parameters, specs, and probes linking in Virtuoso" on the
user's "commit and push codex's 7.130". It holds this entry's hunks only (19 files, +403/-93), on a083a11 from a
private index. The index's 7.129 row moved from after "| 7.1 |" to its place (agreed with that record's session).

That tree was tested in a clean copy: tsc clean; the browser suite 35 scripts, 1,268 checks passed, none flaky.
Two earlier full runs on older bases each had one check of the serial writer lane fail once, and both passed 3 of 3
runs alone:
- export_check: its CSV export gave no answer within the wait.
- run_control_live: after an abort the header's "Start" flipped back to "Aborting...", which looks like an older status
  answer landing after a newer one. Not investigated further.

With this commit, 7.131's extra Logs read (R5) takes effect.

### 7.131 Review after the frontend audit: a saved task is kept between the page's loads, the repository path resolved once (2026-10-01, same session as 7.113; user: "review and see anything to optimize", then chose "Keep the loaded task" and, for the frontend findings, "After the other agent finishes")

The review (report `artifacts/local/reviews/review-2026-10-01-after-audit.md`) had two parts: two adversarial reviewers on
this session's commit 54153dd, every finding re-checked in the code (part A: 8 confirmed, 3 plausible; they wait for the
other agent's frontend work, 7.130, and are fixed on top of it), and the backend time of what the page asks while a real
task is edited (part B, this entry).

**Why the page was slow with a saved task.** Every edit validates the configuration, and the task load, Matched devices and
the saved-model check load the task again. Loading a saved task (`real:<name>`, saved from a Virtuoso cell) opened 43 files
(17 distinct: every saved task's `profile.json` three times, the connection file three times, the task's own files) and
rebuilt its form writer: 0.63-0.96 s per request on the 9p-mounted D: drive, about 85 % of it file work. The saved-model
check (again 0.25 s after every edit when Mode loads a model) loaded the task twice. `repo_root()` resolved its own path
at every call: 38 calls to read the task list.

**What changed (backend only, no optimizer file).**
- `tasks.load_task_view`: the page's loads keep a saved task while nothing it is built from has changed - the name, size
  and modification time of the folder of saved tasks, of the task's folder and every file in it, of the connection file
  and of the conditions file (about 10 stat calls; the traced load reads nothing else that is data). A new revision from
  Save task, a recorded baseline, a deleted or added task, an edited connection: read again. The link and the model file
  of the connection are checked at every call, as before. Each caller gets its own copy of the circuit facts and notes;
  the graph is shared, read only (`compile_task` copies it before it sets variables). At most 16 are kept. Every other

### 7.134 The header's flip-back after Abort: the run lock's liveness test saw another test as a worker (2026-10-01, same session as 7.113; user: "investigate the header flip-back race")

**What was seen.** In one full browser run of 7.130's tree (`run_control_live`, 02:17), the header showed "Start" after
an Abort and then "aborting - waiting for the simulation in flight" again, while the backend's own answer said
"stopped, no worker".

**Cause.** `RunLock.held()` is behind "worker alive" in run status, behind a corner check's state and its stop, and
behind "bridge busy". It tested the lock by taking it EXCLUSIVELY for a moment. A second test at that moment found the
lock taken and answered "held". The second test can be the page's poll, the check's own status read, another tab or a
script. The answer was `worker_alive: true` for a run whose worker had exited, with its state ("stopped") and heartbeat
unchanged. The header orders answers by heartbeat, which cannot tell these apart, and `phaseOf` reads "stopped + worker
alive" as "aborting". The same moment could also refuse a Start or a Resume: the run lock's exclusive acquire failed
while a test held it, and the run was refused as "already running". Evidence:
- 8 threads testing a lock nobody holds: 39.6 % (Linux file system) and 43.5 % (D: drive) of the answers said "held".
- Browser probe in a scratch copy (status reads tagged by the path that asked, repeated Start->Abort cycles, other
  status reads alongside): the page's read sent 24 ms after the one that showed "Start" came back "stopped, worker
  alive" with the same heartbeat. "Aborting..." came back, then "Start" 28 ms later.
- Same probe with 8 test threads in the scratch backend: 4 flip-backs in 9 cycles with the old code, 0 in 12 with the
  fix.
- Ruled out first: a reply overtaking a newer one. With status replies delayed 0-0.3 s at random there were 0 flips in
  12 cycles.
- The hermetic run_control_check cannot show it, because its fixture gives every reply a new heartbeat.

**Fix** (`src/gator_workbench/engine.py`, `runs.py`; no optimizer file):
- `RunLock.held` tests with a SHARED lock. Tests share it with each other, while a worker's exclusive lock still refuses
  it. With 8 threads: 0 false answers out of 12,000, on both file systems.
- `RunLock.acquire(patience=0.2)` waits up to 0.2 s for a test's moment to pass before it says another worker owns the
  run. A worker holds the lock for its whole life, so a real second worker is still refused.
  `_register_in_experiment` keeps `patience=0`, because its own loop already waits up to 10 s.
- Left as it was: the optimizer's Virtuoso session (`virtuoso_bridge.py`) takes `session.lock` exclusively with no
  wait. A "bridge busy" test at that moment can still refuse a real run's session, as before. Fixing it is a package
  change (a new digest) and was not made.
- Seen, not changed: when App's status-poll interval changes it reads at once (7.130's useRequest). That gave three
  reads within 60 ms after "Start", which made a clash more likely.

**Tests.** New `tests/workbench/test_wb_run_lock.py` (5 tests):
- a test in progress is not a worker;
- a worker is seen, and so is its end;
- concurrent tests never see a worker;
- acquire waits out a test but still refuses a worker;
- a stopped run read during another read has no worker.

Against HEAD's old test, 4 of the 5 fail. Results:
- Workbench suite on the working tree: 928 passed, 1 failed. The failure was
  `test_wb_connection::test_consecutive_infrastructure_failures_pause_safely_instead_of_burning_budget`, under a load
  of 25-47 from other sessions. It passed alone (3/3), on HEAD + this fix (7/7) and on the working tree at lower load
  (5/5). This fix did not cause it. "Project optimization review" found the cause in its record 7.135: under load, the
  engine recorded a candidate that the run's own stop caught before its first launch as an infrastructure failure
  (16 of 60). 7.135 fixes it, and the test is deterministic since.
- HEAD + this fix in clean copies: pytest 1,545 passed, 1 skipped, 1 xfailed (218 s); browser suite 35 scripts, 1,268
  checks passed, none flaky.

The live 8765 keeps the old test until it is restarted. Workers of new runs need nothing.

### 7.135 Review round 5's open items: agent CLIs stop with the backend, a run paused for infrastructure failures can be resumed, a proposal is applied once, the random test failures explained (2026-10-01, session "Project optimization review"; user: "go on")

The items 7.117 left open, built and tested in a scratch copy, then written into the working tree only where the files
were still the ones tested (`engine.py` and `runs.py` 3-way merged onto 7.134's edits, identical hunks). Workbench and
tests only: the optimizer digest stays `46327861…`.

**Agent CLIs outlived the backend.** Measured first, with a real `serve` and a fake `claude` that hangs:
- a request answered in one piece kept its CLI running after any number of Ctrl+C (the live answer was stopped on the
  second Ctrl+C by its own stream);
- `kill` (SIGTERM, the way sessions stop 8765) left the backend waiting for an open agent request for ever, streamed or
  not, and a forced kill then left the CLI running on its own.

Now `serve` waits at most 5 s for open requests (`cli.SHUTDOWN_GRACE_SECONDS`), the app's shutdown cancels every CLI
it started (`ProcessRegistry.stop_all`, through `cancel`, in a FastAPI lifespan), and `serve` does it again after a
second Ctrl+C, which skips the app's shutdown. All eight cases measured again: the backend stops within the grace and
no CLI is left. `_read_live` also closes the CLI's pipes (the ResourceWarnings of 7.117).

**A run paused for infrastructure failures could never continue.** Resume restored the consecutive count at the limit,
so `_check_limits` paused the run again before the first dispatch, nothing evaluated, at every Resume (reproduced: 8
evaluations, "4 consecutive infrastructure failures"). The count now starts over on resume: the pause asks for the
connection gate to pass again, and a resume passes it.

**A candidate the run's own stop caught before its first launch was counted as a failure of the link.** One dispatched
just before a pause or stop sees the stop file before its nominal launch; the environment raises "Candidate stopped
before nominal evaluation" and the engine recorded `execution_error` / `infrastructure_unclassified`, charged and
counted (one more infrastructure failure, consecutive failure +1). It is now `cancelled` (failure type `cancelled`),
like a forced cancel, still charged. This was the random failure of
`test_consecutive_infrastructure_failures_pause_safely_instead_of_burning_budget` (16 of 60 runs under load; 7.134
saw it too): the test now allows a cancelled last candidate.

**Smaller fixes.**
- Two presses of Apply (a double click, a second tab) could both apply one proposal: approve, apply and reject now check
  and change it under the store's lock.
- The ngspice deck was written in place, so a failed write left half a deck: a temporary file, published atomically.
- The agent tools' relay honoured `http_proxy`, so a proxy would have seen the one-time token: no proxy now.
- A resume whose consistency-check write failed kept the run lock until a backend restart (every later Resume "already
  running"): released now.

**Random test failures.**
- `test_other_tasks_load_as_before` asserted an empty task cache that it never emptied: it fails whenever
  `test_wb_profiles` ran earlier in the same worker (reproduced every time in that order). It empties the cache first.
- `test_hung_lane_keeps_peer_transition_and_reports_unknown_job` gave both lanes the 0.6 s deadline, so the peer's step
  could miss it under load; only the hung lane has it now.
- `test_the_no_simulation_check_is_gated_and_reports_the_step_that_failed` (failed once on 09-30) did not fail in 104
  module orders, 120 runs under load or 5 full suites with the adapter's process lock traced (the only way to fail
  before the folder exists; no foreign holder seen). It now prints the check's outcome if it fails again.
- Seen 3 times in about 17 parallel full runs, not explained: one pytest worker fails to import a test module during
  collection (`TypeError` in `test_reward_v2_real_data.py`, then `test_graph_sac.py`) and xdist reports "Different
  tests were collected". Never in 6 collection-only runs. Keep the whole output if it comes back.

**Cleanups.** The two broad `pytest.raises(Exception)` name their error (`UnpicklingError`, `BackendNotConnected`). The
frontend `tsconfig` includes `scripts/fixtures`: `request_lifecycle.tsx` had gone stale (2 type errors since 7.130's
props), fixed with the `null`s it was rendering anyway. `task_rows_check` reads the saved configuration with the
harness's `trackSaves`/`saved` instead of a fixed second. `reward_v21_check` keeps its waits: its author's fixture
(`/tmp/gator-reward-v21-browser`) is gone, so it cannot be run. Found already done: the ModelChooser reads (frontend
audit), soft-spec "missing = failed" (named by validation), the MScR limits (7.132). Still open: the working copy
restoring from a later import (needs the Virtuoso-flow session).

**Proof.** 12 new tests in `tests/workbench/test_wb_round5_followup.py`, each failing on the old code for the reason it
names. Full suite on a copy of the working tree: 1,539 passed before, 1,551 after (the 12 new; no other outcome
changed). `tsc` clean with the fixtures. Browser checks on a build of the copy: task_rows 42, request_lifecycle 25,

### 7.136 Several designs at once on Spectre: 2 lanes 1.8× faster, 4 lanes 2.3×, the one Virtuoso preparation process the limit (2026-10-01, same session as 7.115; user: "go on", then chose "ota7t at 1, 2, 4", then for the diagnosis "dont use fake simulator use spectre")

**Why.** The user's concern is the wall-clock time of runs. Their saved "Concurrent Spectre jobs" is 1, although a real
task allows 4, and nobody had timed more than one lane on a real task.

**Runs on Spectre.** All on `real:mscr_ota7t` (rev 1), 20 evaluations per run, the starter's defaults otherwise, seed
20261004. CLI runs used a scratch settings folder holding copies of the task files. Each run left the cell equal to its
record in every field. 100 simulations in all.

| run | at once | per evaluation | faster | lanes starting | whole CLI run |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 03:55 | 1 | 1.99 s | 1× | 2.1 s | 60.1 s |
| 03:56 | 2 | 1.10 s | 1.8× | 4.3 s | 41.4 s |
| 03:57 | 4 | 1.59 s | 1.25× | 9.0 s | 58.9 s |
| 13:50 | 4, with the stack sampler | 0.87 s | 2.3× | 8.7 s | 44.4 s |
| 13:51 | 4, one CPU thread per process | 1.10 s | 1.8× | 29.7 s | 78.8 s |

The whole CLI run also holds about 15 s before the preparation process starts: Python and torch start-up, the
connection check, the cell check, and the setup read, which alone takes 6 s.

**Where the time goes** (13:50 run; a scratch `sitecustomize` sampled every thread of every process every 50 ms):
- **The one Virtuoso preparation process** writes and netlists every candidate, one at a time. During the evaluations
  it was busy 90% of the time, about 0.6 s per candidate:
  - five reads of the whole cell, 0.23 s;
  - the netlist export, 0.22 s;
  - two window checks, 0.06 s;
  - the write, 0.06 s;
  - journal files, 0.03 s.
- **The lanes** spent 47-61% of their time waiting for that process, 28-36% in Spectre, and 4-20% waiting for the
  engine.
- **The engine** was idle 92% of the time.

So about 0.6 s per evaluation is the floor on this cell, whatever the lane count. The reads cost more on a larger cell
(`mscr_vco_lin`, 34 instances: about 0.4 s per read, from 7.133's export manifests). Lanes open one after another,
about 2 s each.

**Not explained.** The 03:57 run stalled for about 12 s once the policy took over. Its results waited 1.5-4.7 s after
their jobs ended, and every lane sat idle until +32 s. The repeat at 13:50 did not stall:
- Learner updates took 0.06 s each.
- No checkpoint fell inside the run.
- The machine had memory to spare.

The afternoon runs used the live tree, which then held the uncommitted engine edits of 7.134 and 7.135; neither touches
dispatch. Before the user's "dont use fake simulator use spectre", a run with the test stand-in and 1-s fake jobs had
scaled to 3.6× at 4 lanes without stalling. That is why I suspected the real path. All the figures above are from
Spectre runs.

**One CPU thread per process** (`OMP_NUM_THREADS=1` and `MKL_NUM_THREADS=1` for the whole run, the main suspect for
the stall) did not help: 1.10 s per evaluation and a 21 s longer start.

**Found on the way.** A saved task's `profile.json` stores its working-copy folder (`state_dir`) as an absolute path
into the live `configs/local/workbench/working_copies`. Two consequences:
- A run from a scratch settings folder still writes its setup-read logs there. 7.133's five runs left five such
  folders (request, answer and bridge log of a read; `last_written_setup.json` untouched). These runs pointed the copies
  at the scratch folder instead.
- A moved repository would write to, or fail on, the old path.

**Open, the user's choice:**
- Set "Concurrent Spectre jobs" to 2-4 for real runs. It is the user's setting, so it was not changed.
- Fewer cell reads per candidate. Reading the cell again right after the write's own readback, and again before the
  export, adds nothing. Dropping those two reads and one window check saves about 0.12 s of the 0.6 s on `mscr_ota7t`,
  and about 0.85 s per evaluation on `mscr_vco_lin`. That code is in the optimizer package (`virtuoso_bridge.py`,
  `native_preparation.py`), so it means a digest step. Done in 7.137, on the user's choice.
- Open the lanes in parallel, about 2 s per extra lane at the start. This is workbench code (`lanes.open_lanes`). Done
  in 7.137.
- Store `state_dir` relative to the settings folder. This belongs to the Virtuoso-flow code (`working_copy.py`,
  `profiles.py`). Done in 7.137 by reading it under the settings folder in use.

Documented in `docs/workbench.md` ("Several designs at once", in "How a real run spends its simulations"). No code
change. Committed in `8043c07`.

### 7.137 A run's lanes open side by side; the native preparation reads the cell three times per candidate, not five; a task's working-copy folder is read under the settings folder in use (2026-10-01, same session as 7.115; user: "go on", then "Yes, remove them" and "Yes, one run")

Three of 7.136's open items. The lanes and the working-copy folder are workbench code. The fewer reads are optimizer
code, done on the user's choice "Yes, remove them" after they were told it is a digest step.

**Lanes open side by side.** `lanes.open_lanes` used to open a run's lanes one after another. A real lane starts a
worker process and builds its environment, about 2 s, so a 4-lane run waited 8.7 s before its first evaluation (7.136).
Now:
- The lanes open in parallel, one thread each.
- They come back in index order.
- If one fails to open, every lane that did open is closed and the failure of the lowest index is raised, as before.

Nothing else in a lane's start is shared: a native lane's `connect` only asks the preparation process for its baseline
and binds the bridge environment in its own process. New tests in `tests/workbench/test_wb_lanes_open.py`:
- the lanes' builds overlap and keep their order;
- a lane that fails closes the others;
- zero and one lane;
- a 2-lane run on worker processes, the path real runs take, around the scripted test backend.

**Three reads per candidate, not five** (`backends/virtuoso_bridge.py`, `backends/native_preparation.py`). The
preparation process used to repeat two checks a moment after making them, with nothing happening in between:
- `read_parameters()`, right after `set_parameters`' own readback;
- the read and window check at the start of `generate_netlist`.

Now the service takes the write's readback (`written_parameters()`) and exports with
`generate_netlist(..., just_written=True)`, which uses that readback as its snapshot. What stays:
- the cell is read before each write, after it and after the export;
- the export is compared with the readback;
- an edit made meanwhile is still refused.

Other callers of `generate_netlist` are unchanged. New tests in `tests/test_native_preparation.py`: three reads and one
window check per candidate, and a cell edited during the export refused. Optimizer digest `46327861…` ->
`66ce02ab91eda506b52a2a727746ff370b7517383eaf3680d507a647caf54797` (`docs/decisions.md`): a run saved under an earlier
digest no longer resumes exactly, while `fine_tune` and `frozen` still load. Peers were told; none has a job pinned to
`46327861…`. Astra's work in the Codex app is not known to them.

**The working-copy folder** (7.136's finding): `working_copy.state_path(target)` reads a stored absolute `state_dir`
whose last three parts are `working_copies/<a>/<b>` as `<settings folder in use>/working_copies/<a>/<b>`. Any other path
is used as stored. It serves:
- the setup read and write folders (`_request_dir`);
- `write_setup_on`'s `last_written_setup.json`;
- `last_written`.

The stored path is unchanged, with no migration, so older code (the 8765 server until it restarts) still finds the same
files. For the user's tasks under the live settings folder the resolved path is the stored one. Test:
`test_a_task_saved_under_another_settings_folder_keeps_its_copy_records_in_this_one` (`test_wb_working_copy.py`).

**Checked on Spectre** (the user's "Yes, one run"): one run with both changes, `real:mscr_ota7t`, 4 at once, 20
evaluations, the same settings as 7.136. The cell equalled its record afterwards, and the run's manifest names digest
`66ce02ab…`.

| | 7.136 (13:50) | now |
|---|---|---|
| per evaluation | 0.87 s | 0.70 s (2.8× one at a time) |
| lanes starting | 8.7 s | 2.6 s |
| whole CLI run | 44.4 s | 32.8 s |
| preparation per candidate: cell reads | 0.23 s | 0.14 s |
| preparation per candidate: window checks | 0.06 s | 0.03 s |

The preparation process is still busy 92% of the time, so it is still the limit. The export varied between the two runs
(0.22 s, then 0.27 s per candidate), and each figure is one run, so part of the 0.17 s gain per evaluation is
run-to-run spread.

**Correction to 7.136.** Dropping the two repeated cell reads and one window check saves about 0.12 s per candidate on
`mscr_ota7t` (not 0.17 s) and about 0.85 s on `mscr_vco_lin` (not 1.2 s). The first figures counted three reads.

**Proof.** Full suite on a clean copy of HEAD `87f4dd8` plus my seven files (both package files included), with the
evidence folders linked in: 1,564 passed, 1 skipped, 1 xfailed (62 s). Before the package change: 1,550 on HEAD
`4652209` plus the workbench files, and 1,576 on the live tree with the other sessions' uncommitted work.

Documented in `docs/workbench.md` ("Several designs at once"; after "Until 2026-09-23 the button Make a working copy")
and `docs/decisions.md` (summary line and entry). Committed as `1c308a5` (the optimizer part, digest `34bc85f4…` =
HEAD `6b61967`'s `41983cc7…` plus this step's four package files) and `8043c07` (the workbench part and the docs),
tested before the branch moved, in clean copies with the evidence folders linked in: the optimizer tree 1,571 passed
and the whole 1,580 passed, 1 skipped and 1 xfailed each. The Spectre trials of 7.137-7.143 ran under the working-tree
digests of their day, before 7.144's behaviour-identical cleanup.

### 7.138 What is left of the Virtuoso preparation time, and the ways to cut it (2026-10-01, same session as 7.115; user: "check possible solutions" for the netlist export)

An investigation, no code change. All numbers come from 7.137's Spectre check run (its stack samples), the decks
kept by the MScR runs, and one read of the simulator host.

**Where the 0.53 s per candidate goes** (`mscr_ota7t`, 4 lanes, preparation process busy 92%):

| step | per candidate |
|---|---|
| netlist export, SKILL on the host | 0.25 s |
| netlist export, download of the package | 0.02 s |
| three whole-cell reads | 0.14 s |
| form write (`schCheck`, `dbSave`) | 0.07 s |
| window check | 0.03 s |

The bridge's export (`schematic_export_netlist_skill`) runs `simulator('spectre)`, `design(...)`, `ddsRefresh()` and
`createNetlist(?recreateAll t)` on every call. So it sets OCEAN up again, refreshes the libraries and re-netlists every
cell, the unchanged testbench included. The form writer saves the cell after each write.

**Decks of different candidates differ only in values.** 14 distinct kept decks of `mscr_ota7t`, `mscr_vco_lin`,
`mscr_ldo1_rgcn` and `mscr_ring3` were compared after joining the netlister's continuation lines. They have the same
statements, with the same parameter names in the same order, and differ only in the values of:
- the written sizes (`w`, `l`, `m`, `r`);
- the values the PDK callbacks derive from them (`as`, `ad`, `ps`, `pd`, `nrd`, `nrs`, `sca`, `scb`, `scc`).

Identical designs gave byte-identical decks across runs. `validate_export` already holds every netlisted value to one
of three sources: the readback (written), the form's current value (derived), or the record (everything else). It
passed on every native export so far.

**Simulator host:** 8 cores (i7-13700KF), 12 GB of memory, idle between runs. Four lanes use half of the cores.

**Ways to cut it:**

| | what | saves per candidate | cost and risk |
|---|---|---|---|
| A | Netlist from the readback: build the export from a template (the session's first native export), filling each value slot from the after-write readback. Keep a native export every Nth candidate, and stop if it differs from the built one | about 0.32 s on N-1 of N candidates (export and the read after it); average preparation about 0.27 s with N = 5 | optimizer code, digest step. Changes the "native export for every candidate" rule into "verified build, natively checked every Nth", which the backend guidance allows only once verified to match. Up to N-1 candidates could run on a wrong netlist if the netlister did something the three-source rule misses (it has not so far) |
| B | A cheaper native export: our own SKILL that sets OCEAN up once per session, drops `ddsRefresh` (every edit happens and is saved in this session) and netlists incrementally (`?recreateAll nil`), so only the changed cell is re-netlisted | unknown until timed on Virtuoso; part of 0.25 s | optimizer code, digest step. Keeps a native export for every candidate. `validate_export` would refuse a stale incremental netlist, stopping the run rather than mis-simulating |
| C | One SKILL call for window check, read, write and readback: the write checks the cell still holds the previous values and returns the new ones | about 0.1 s | workbench SKILL plus optimizer protocol, digest step |
| D | A second Virtuoso session on a second cell copy, two preparation processes | doubles preparation throughput | a second Virtuoso licence and bridge daemon, routing, keeping copies in step. Large |

With A, the preparation (about 0.27 s) would no longer be the limit at 4 lanes. Spectre would be: about 1.2 s per job
over 4 lanes, 0.3 s per evaluation. The expected run speed is then about 0.35-0.4 s per evaluation, against 0.70 s now
and 1.99 s at one lane. Each Spectre job spends 0.4-0.9 s reading the PDK model files (`spectre.out`), which is the next
target. With 8 cores, more than the package's 4 lanes is possible but not tried.

**Suggested order:** first, time B on Virtuoso. That needs about 30 netlist exports of `tb_ota7t`: Virtuoso only, no
Spectre, no cell write. Then build A, checked on Spectre with the native export compared on EVERY candidate for 20-50
candidates on several MScR tasks, before it is allowed to skip. Both need the user's go-ahead (Virtuoso use, and a
digest step). Committed in `8043c07`.

### 7.140 The cheaper export timed on Virtuoso; netlists built from the readback, checked against every native export on Spectre; a value at its bound no longer stops a run (2026-10-02, same session as 7.115; user: "go on", then "Yes, with writes like a run", "Yes, check-only first", "Yes, 60 simulations", and after the bug "Yes, 40 more simulations")

**Option B timed on Virtuoso** (7.138): 28 exports of `tb_ota7t`. Before each export, a legal size set from earlier runs
was written into `mscr_ota7t`, as a run does. Every export was checked against the cell with `validate_export`.
Medians leave out the first export of each variant.

| export variant | per export |
|---|---|
| the bridge's own: OCEAN set up, libraries refreshed, everything re-netlisted | 0.27 s |
| without the library refresh | 0.27 s |
| OCEAN set up once, `createNetlist(?recreateAll t)` per export | 0.18 s |
| set up once, incremental `createNetlist(?recreateAll nil)` | 0.19 s |

All 28 exports matched their cell, the incremental ones too. The write took 0.06 s. So B is worth about 0.09 s per
native export, all of it from not setting OCEAN up again. The library refresh and re-netlisting the unchanged testbench
cost nothing measurable.

A slip: the first attempt stopped on a SKILL syntax error in the set-up-once variant after 14 exports, with the cell
still holding a test size set. The rerun of the two remaining variants restores the cell in a `finally`. Afterwards the
cell equalled its record in every field.

**Option A in check-only mode** (switched on by `GATOR_NETLIST_BUILD=check`; without it the preparation is as in 7.137):
- `CdfWritableProfile.render_export(template, snapshot)` (`cdf_write.py`) puts the readback's written and derived
  values into the template's value slots, in its brackets.
  - A written parameter takes the form field of its own name when the instance has one, since the netlister writes the
    total width `w`. Otherwise it takes the field it is typed in.
  - Every other value stays as the template has it, which `validate_export` holds to the record.
- The preparation service (`native_preparation.py`) uses the session's first native export as the template.
  - For every candidate, the build must have the native export's statements (`netlist_statements`, `first_difference`:
    continuation lines joined, comments left out).
  - A difference raises, which stops the run. The native export is still the one simulated.
  - The export manifest notes `built_export: matches the native export`.
- `VirtuosoBridgeBackend.written_snapshot()` gives the build the write's readback.

**A value at its bound no longer stops a run** (`graph/circuit_graph.py`). The first trial stopped `mscr_ring3` (after
15 evaluations) and `mscr_vco_lin` (after 11) on their first policy design, with "l: invalid bounds/current value":
- The length group's value was exactly its top, 180 nm.
- The netlist parser reads "180n" as 180 × 1e-9 = 1.8000000000000002e-07.
- `ParameterGroup.validate` compared that strictly with the upper bound 1.8e-07.

The 7.133 runs of these tasks were all warm-up, so they never proposed a top value, and any real run whose policy chose
one would have stopped the same way. `validate` now allows a relative 1e-12 at either bound, the span `Write.legal`
already allows. Only values that were refused before change outcome; the parser is unchanged, since its numbers feed
stored values and hashes. On `mscr_ring3`'s own netlist with every length at 180n, the exported graph is now accepted.

Follow-up, found by the "RL reward design" session: `ActionMapper.encode` (`parameters/actions.py`) still tested
the bounds exactly. A starting design one float step past a bound passed the group check but failed encoding, so
`SeededProposer` (the LLM sizing benchmark's `matched_seeded` arm) failed at start, and an LLM reply repeating the
start was refused instead of recognised. SAC was unaffected, since `legalize` clamps. `encode` now allows the same
span and maps such a value to -1 or +1. Values 1e-9 outside stay refused (`test_llm_audit_fixes.py`).

**Trial on Spectre**, 4 lanes, 20 evaluations each, check-only:

| task | digest | evaluations | built netlist = native export | cell afterwards |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `mscr_ota7t` | `046e35c6…` | 20: 4 resets, 8 warm-up, 8 policy | every candidate | equal to its record |
| `mscr_ring3` | `78e8ca37…` | 20: 4 resets, 8 warm-up, 8 policy, one design on a bound | every candidate | equal to its record |
| `mscr_vco_lin` | `78e8ca37…` | 20: 4 resets, 16 warm-up (9 designs gave no valid result, as in 7.133) | every candidate | equal to its record |

The stopped first runs also matched on every candidate until their stop. That makes 86 simulations in all: the user
approved 60, then 40 more. No read log reached the live settings folder (7.137's `state_path`).

**Tests:**
- `test_native_preparation.py`: check-only mode compares every candidate and a differing build stops the service;
  without the variable nothing is built.
- `test_wb_cdf_write.py`: the build equals the fake form's own export after a write and back; on the real ring export
  it keeps brackets, line breaks and the untouched statements.
- `tests/test_bound_slack.py`, the group check and `encode`.
- Full suite on a clean copy of HEAD `87f4dd8` plus my files: 1,573 passed, 1 skipped, 1 xfailed.

Optimizer digest `66ce02ab…` -> `046e35c6…` (check-only) -> `78e8ca37…` (bound) ->
`c3a340b9c426742923327c881dcf04d2eeab89efa7a4e1e192b348e89de4513b` (`encode`). It is one uncommitted step together with
7.137.

**Open, the user's choice:**
- Let the build replace most native exports, with a native export every Nth candidate (N = 5 suggested). The
  preparation would drop from about 0.53 s to about 0.27 s per candidate, and 4 lanes from 0.70 s towards 0.35-0.4 s per
  evaluation.
- Option B, OCEAN set up once: about 0.09 s per native export.

Documented in `docs/workbench.md` ("Netlists built from the readback, check-only") and `docs/decisions.md`. Committed as
`1c308a5` and `8043c07`.

### 7.141 A parameter bound to a Maestro variable gets its own value back, even after its cell was read again while bound (2026-10-02, session "Project optimization review"; user: "go on")

The last open item of review round 5 (PLAUSIBLE there). Binding a parameter to a design variable sets its value in
Virtuoso to the variable's name (`bridge_setup.write_bindings`). Dropping the binding put back the "original" value,
taken from the cell's newest import. But since 7.72 the copy is the cell itself, so a cell read again while a
parameter was bound has the variable's name as that value. Dropping the binding then wrote the name back. If the
variable went too, the instance named a variable that no longer exists, and nothing reported it. Reproduced with the
setup writer's stand-in: the restore sent `['tb_ring_osc_3stage', 'V0', 'vdc', 'VSUP']`. Latent: no working copy has a
binding today.

**Fix** (`working_copy.write_setup_on`, workbench only). When the workbench first binds a parameter, its value is kept
in `values_before_binding.json` in the copy's state folder, for as long as the parameter stays bound, and that value
goes back. A binding written before this fix, whose cell now reads the variable's own name, is refused in words
before anything is sent: "type its value in Virtuoso and read the cell again".

**Proof.** 2 new tests in `test_wb_virtuoso_setup.py`; on the old code one gets the name back and the other is not
refused. Full suite on a copy of the working tree: 1,567 passed before, 1,569 after, no other outcome changed.
`docs/workbench.md` says it under "What may be a Maestro variable". `working_copy.py` also holds 7.137's uncommitted
`state_path` change, untouched; its session was told first. No package change.

**Commit.** `cd8f68a` (10-02 19:08, pushed): this entry's hunks only (5 files, +76/-4), on 4597fc6 from a private
index, after "Work review and functionality references" committed 7.139. The new line of `write_setup_on` is in
HEAD's form, `Path(target['state_dir'])`: 7.137's uncommitted `state_path()` converts it in the working tree, with
its other call sites, and goes with 7.137. Left in the tree for their owners: 7.137's hunks in `working_copy.py`,
7.122/7.123 and 7.136-7.138/7.140/7.142 with their rows, the other sessions' edits of `docs/workbench.md`. The
committed tree passed the full suite in a clean copy: 1,539 passed, 21 skipped, 1 xfailed.

### 7.142 Real runs build most netlists from the readback, with Virtuoso's export every 5th candidate; the export sets OCEAN up once (2026-10-02, same session as 7.115; user: "go on", asked "tell me what is the difference? Does the simulation result differ?", then chose "On by default" and "Yes", and "Yes, 60 simulations")

**The user's question, answered before they chose:**
- The netlist Spectre receives has the same statements either way: same devices, connections and values, character for
  character. Only the line breaks and a date comment can differ, and Spectre ignores both. So the results are the same.
- The values are the cell's own, read back after the PDK callbacks ran. Nothing computes a device value.
- What changes is who writes the file: the builder for 4 of 5 candidates, Virtuoso for every 5th, compared with the
  build.
- If Virtuoso ever wrote something the builder does not reproduce, up to 4 candidates would run on the built version
  before the next check stops the run.
- Option B changes no netlist, only how often OCEAN is set up.

**Skip mode, the default** (`native_preparation.py`):
- `GATOR_NETLIST_BUILD` is `skip` unless set; `check` is 7.140's mode, `native` builds nothing, and any other value
  stops the start in words.
- In `skip` mode, the session's first candidate and every `NATIVE_EVERY` = 5th get the native export, compared with
  the build as in 7.140.
- The others get `generate_netlist(..., built=...)`: the build written as the package's netlist, held to the cell by
  `validate_export`, no export and no read after the write. Labelled `preparation: actual-candidate-cdf-built-netlist`
  and `netlist_source: built from the readback`.

**Option B** (`VirtuosoBridgeBackend._export_netlist`):
- A client that runs SKILL and downloads files sets OCEAN up once (`simulator`, `design`), then calls
  `createNetlist(?recreateAll t)` and downloads the package.
- It sets up again when the path returned does not name the testbench.
- Maestro-recipe tasks (`measures_in_virtuoso`), and clients without those calls (the tests' stand-ins), keep the
  bridge's export.
- Every MScR task uses the Maestro recipe, so B changed nothing in the trial (the stack samples show only the bridge's
  export). With native exports on 1 candidate in 5, extending it would save about 0.02 s per candidate, so it was left
  so.

**Trial on Spectre**, 4 lanes, 20 evaluations each, the new defaults, the same settings as the check-only runs of 7.140:

| task | per evaluation, check-only | default | waiting for the preparation | job |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `mscr_ota7t` | 0.70 s (7.137) | 0.54 s | 1.33 -> 0.65 s | 1.2 s |
| `mscr_ring3` | 0.71 s | 0.64 s | 0.96 -> 0.65 s | 1.7 s |
| `mscr_vco_lin` | 2.02 s | 2.03 s | 2.42 -> 1.74 s | 5.0 s |

- Every cell equalled its record afterwards, and no read log reached the live settings folder. 60 simulations.
- The 1st, 6th, 11th and 16th candidates of each run were native and matched their builds; a difference would have
  stopped the run.
- On `mscr_ota7t` (stack samples), the preparation process is busy 62% instead of 92%, about 0.29 s per candidate:
  reads 0.085 s, write 0.07 s, exports averaged over the candidates 0.055 s, journals 0.033 s, window check 0.028 s.
- The lanes spend about half their time in Spectre and a quarter to a third waiting their turn at the preparation.
- `mscr_vco_lin` is bound by its transient jobs.

**Tests:**
- `test_native_preparation.py`: by default, native exports on candidates 1 and 6 of 7, the others built with the cell's
  statements and only the reads before and after the write; `native` builds nothing; an unknown mode is refused;
  `OceanClient`: one set-up for three exports, set up again after another design took OCEAN, and a Maestro task keeps
  the bridge's export.
- `test_wb_cdf_write.py`: the default through the preparation service on the fake form, each of six candidates' netlist
  the cell's own export.
- Full suite on a clean copy of HEAD `87f4dd8` plus my files (and the other session's 7.141 test file, whose
  `working_copy.py` hunk sits beside mine): 1,579 passed, 1 skipped, 1 xfailed.

Optimizer digest `c3a340b9…` -> `e3f8c5b84c4480f5dcc1feec9539e1635b18306f7ceea86e5e5b2d229ae51315`, one uncommitted
step with 7.137 and 7.140. Documented in `docs/workbench.md` ("Netlists built from the readback") and
`docs/decisions.md`. Committed as `1c308a5` and `8043c07`.

### 7.143 Spectre's share measured, and up to 6 lanes for a real task (2026-10-02, same session as 7.115; user: "go on", then "Raise to 6" and "Yes, 40 simulations")

**Where a Spectre job's time goes** (`spectre.out` of the jobs kept by the 7.142 trial):
- `mscr_ota7t`: about 1.24 s per job as a lane sees it, 1.01 s of it Spectre itself.
  - reading the netlist and the PDK model files ("NDB parsing"): 0.43 s;
  - the analyses: 0.05 s;
  - start-up, licence and shutdown: about 0.5 s;
  - upload, download and the result parse around Spectre: about 0.23 s.
- `mscr_vco_lin`: 5-7.6 s, mostly its transient analysis (2-4 s), with 0.7-0.8 s of model reading.
- Licence time is usually 0.03 s but often waits in steps of about 0.2 s (0.22-1.23 s), even with one lane. It looks as
  if a job waits for a licence the previous one still holds. The licence server itself was not queried; its raw output
  stays out of this repository's tools.

**The cap** (user: "Raise to 6"): `MAX_SPECTRE_WORKERS` in `native_preparation.py` was 4 and is now 6. The simulator
host has 8 cores (i7-13700KF) and 12 GB. The workbench's task limit follows it as `tasks.REAL_LANES`, replacing three
literal 4s, and a test holds the two equal. The test of the rejected count now uses 7.

**Measured on Spectre**, 20 evaluations each with the 7.142 defaults, against 7.142's 4-lane runs:

| task | 4 lanes | 6 lanes | job, 4 -> 6 lanes | longest licence wait |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `mscr_ota7t` | 0.54 s per evaluation | 0.41 s (4.9× one lane) | 1.24 -> 1.46 s | 0.03 s |
| `mscr_vco_lin` | 2.03 s | 1.40 s | 5.04 -> 5.55 s | 0.84 s |

- Both runs reached 6 Spectre jobs at once with no licence error, and both cells equalled their records afterwards. No
  read log reached the live settings. 40 simulations.
- Jobs are 10-15% slower on the shared CPUs.
- The preparation process was busy 79% (`mscr_ota7t`) and 71% (`mscr_vco_lin`), and lanes waited their turn 15-38% of
  the time. So more lanes would gain little.
- What is left is the preparation itself (the cell reads cost about 0.4 s each on `mscr_vco_lin`'s 34 instances), and
  Spectre's own start-up and model reading on every job. A Spectre that stays running between jobs, changing values
  with `alter`, would remove most of that, but it would be a runner beside the bridge's. Not started.

Tests: `test_more_spectre_workers_than_the_cap_are_rejected_before_any_io`, which also checks that the task limit is
the optimizer's. Full suite on a clean copy of HEAD `4597fc6` plus my files: 1,579 passed, 1 skipped, 1 xfailed.
Optimizer digest `e3f8c5b8…` -> `efa93649d1e792602174994abf4a019e23e9dcf2ccef9ef0e1dd25a1beaab7ca`, one uncommitted
step with 7.137, 7.140 and 7.142. Documented in `docs/workbench.md` ("Several designs at once") and
`docs/decisions.md`. Committed as `1c308a5` and `8043c07`.

### 7.144 The GPDK045 profiles' copied checks merged into one shared class, every message kept; two dead cache branches and an in-loop import gone (2026-10-02, session "Project optimization review"; user: "do (a) only")

Of round 5's three optimizer leftovers, offered as (a) behaviour-identical cleanups, (b) deleting the homogeneous graph

not overlap).
