### 7.145 Cheaper cell reads measured (not worth it); the one Virtuoso session is what limits; a staying-alive Spectre is possible but would gain little now (2026-10-02, same session as 7.115; user: "go on with the plan")

An investigation, no code change. Reads and help texts only: no simulation, no write.

**Reads without instance positions** (10 reads each, MScR cells, under the session lock):

| cell | with positions | without |
|---|---|---|
| `mscr_ota7t`, 9 instances | 0.027 s | 0.025 s |
| `mscr_vco_lin`, 34 instances | 0.261 s | 0.236 s |

The parameters were identical. A read without positions also leaves out each instance's `view` (with `xy`, `orient`,
`bBox`, `numInst`), which `validate_readback` compares, so it is not a drop-in replacement. The cost of a read is
fetching every form value of every instance (about 1,100 on `mscr_vco_lin`), and the checks use them all. Not changed.

**The same reads during a run** cost more: about 0.047 s (`mscr_ota7t`, stack samples) and 0.3-0.4 s (`mscr_vco_lin`,
export manifests).

Every MScR task uses the Maestro recipe, so OCEAN measures each candidate's outputs inside the Virtuoso session. In
7.143's 6-lane runs, each lane spent about 0.1-0.15 s per candidate in that measurement, through the same SKILL
interpreter that serves the preparation's reads, writes and exports. So the limit at 6 lanes is the one Virtuoso
session doing both. Its preparation thread, busy 71-79%, partly waits for the lanes' measurements.

**A Spectre that stays running:** Spectre 23.1.0.242 offers `+interactive`, `+interactive=skill` and
`+interactive=mpsc` (`spectre -h` on the simulator host, with the bridge's Cadence set-up). One Spectre per lane could
keep its models loaded and take each candidate's values by `alter`. Each job now also pays the bridge's
`csh -fc 'source <cadence cshrc>; exec spectre ...'` before Spectre's own clock starts. But the Virtuoso session limits
`mscr_ota7t`, and the transient analysis itself limits `mscr_vco_lin`, so this would gain little on today's tasks. Not
started.

**What could still help, each the user's choice:**
- Leave out the read before each write. The read after the write still compares the whole cell with the last readback,
  so a foreign edit between candidates is still caught, but only after the write. A foreign edit of a written value
  would be overwritten unseen. It saves one of three reads: about 0.03 s on `mscr_ota7t` and 0.3-0.4 s on
  `mscr_vco_lin`.
- A second Virtuoso session for the OCEAN measurements, so they stop queueing with the preparation. That needs another
  Virtuoso licence and bridge daemon.
- Measuring Maestro outputs without Virtuoso would mean re-implementing Cadence's calculator; not proposed.

Committed in `8043c07`.


### 7.146 Project names and task selection: automatic rows, atomic switching and optional reload (2026-10-02, Codex; user: "I want you to modify it")

The Project column previously selected the new task immediately but retained edited rows from the old one,
then asked for Load task. Its two name fields followed generated defaults as a pair, so editing either stopped both.

**Changed.** Project name remains stable. The duplicate editable Task name field is removed; the run-folder/export
label follows the selected task's saved label, independently of the project name. A first Virtuoso task save also
sets that label. Choosing a task reads its saved rows before changing the selected task, circuit labels, run label,

retains the current task and rows. This includes edits made while the request is pending. Identical destination

Run, output and research settings are preserved. The former Load task icon is now **Reload saved values** for
resetting the current task, with the same edit protection. Backend guidance and agent readiness wording follow it.

### 7.148 An OCEAN of each lane's own on the simulator host: built, identical values, no faster on Spectre, removed; the read before each write stays (2026-10-02, same session as 7.115; user: "Keep the check", "how does ocean off virtuoso help?", "Yes, build it offline", "Yes, a few OCEAN starts", "Yes, 2 tasks, 120 sims", "Remove it")

**Decision recorded.** The user kept the read before each write, the first of 7.145's options ("Keep the check"),
after asking whether dropping it would matter for a run paused and edited. Nothing changed.

**Why a lane OCEAN.** At 6 lanes the one Virtuoso session takes every candidate's preparation and, for a Maestro task
(every MScR task), every candidate's OCEAN measurement, one at a time (7.145). Plan: `docs/plan-measurement-off-virtuoso.md`,
stages G0-G3; G0 (one start) was done before this entry.

**Protocol, measured on the host** (five OCEAN starts in all, no simulation, no cell write, the Virtuoso session not
used):
- `ocean -nograph` over a pipe answers on its standard output, after a `> ` prompt, and echoes no input.
- It keeps running at the end of its input. A TERM starts Cadence's crash reporter (`cdsPstack`, gdb on the process).
- Its first measurement takes 1.3-3.6 s (it loads its results reader), each later one 0.03-0.05 s. Each holds about
  660 MB.
- One probe closed its input without `exit()` and left an OCEAN tree running on the host. I removed exactly that tree:
  its own process session, its display watchdog and its message server, nothing of the user's session. The host script
  below now prevents it.

**Built** (workbench code only; `GATOR_OCEAN_MEASURE` chooses, `session` by default, so runs are unchanged):
- `ocean.host_script`: the one-line bash program on the host. OCEAN runs in a fresh folder and a process session of
  its own, fed through a named pipe. When the lane's input ends, after 15 minutes without input, or when the
  connection drops, it sends `exit()`, kills the session's leftovers after up to 10 s, and removes the folder.
- `ocean.worker_argv`: ssh with the bridge's options for its Spectre host (key, config, jump host), a connection of its
  own per lane, under the Cadence set-up the bridge sources for Spectre.
- `ocean.OceanWorker`: one request at a time, each answer between two tagged markers, 120 s per answer.
- `ocean.LaneMeasurer` (`worker`, `check`):
  - the lane's first candidate is measured in Virtuoso while its OCEAN starts in the background; that OCEAN's first
    answer is the same candidate again, compared;
  - then every 5th candidate (every one in `check`) is measured both ways;
  - a difference, or a folder only Virtuoso can read, stops the lane: each later evaluation fails as an infrastructure
    failure, so the run pauses after its limit, as after a built netlist that differs (7.142);
  - a lost OCEAN is replaced once, Virtuoso measuring meanwhile.
- `recipes.GenericMaestroProfile.analyze` calls `ocean.measure_lane` and records `measured_by` in
  `measurements.json`; in mode `session` its note is the old text, word for word.
- `_client`'s guard of the VB_ variables became `_bridge_env`, shared with `worker_argv` (same behaviour).
- Tests: `tests/workbench/test_wb_ocean_worker.py` (21) with a stand-in OCEAN (`tests/workbench/fake_ocean.py`) that,
  like the real one, keeps running at the end of its input; the host script runs under bash here. The whole suite:
  1,615 passed, 1 skipped, 1 xfailed.

**Checked on the host as a run starts it** (two lane OCEANs at once, the 12 result folders 7.143's 6-lane trials left
there; reads only):
- both started in 4.1 s;
- 12 of 12 folders measured to exactly the values the Virtuoso session recorded in those trials;
- first request 1.3 s, later ones 0.03 s;
- one closed normally (2.1 s); the other's local ssh was killed, and within 2 s the host had no OCEAN, script, message
  server or folder left.

Seen on the host, not touched: eight idle Maestro job processes of the user's Virtuoso session (`virtuoso -beanhost`,
started 29-30 Sep), about 350 MB each. Six lane OCEANs would take about 4 GB of the host's 8.2 GB available.

Docs at that point: a paragraph in `docs/workbench.md`, one row in `docs/functionality-reference.md` (both taken out
again, see **Removed**), the plan's G1 result.

**Spectre trial (G2, the user's "Yes, 2 tasks, 120 sims"): correct, but no faster.** 6 lanes, scratch settings, the
seed of 7.143's session-only 6-lane runs; a scratch-only logger recorded who measured each candidate:
- `mscr_ota7t`: `check` 20 evaluations 0.90 s/eval, `worker` 40 evaluations 0.64 s/eval, 0.39 s/eval once every
  lane's OCEAN was ready (from 18.6 s); session only (7.143) 0.41 s/eval.
- `mscr_vco_lin`: `check` 20 evaluations 1.86 s/eval, `worker` 38 evaluations 1.57 s/eval, 1.10 s/eval once ready
  (from 34.3 s); session only 1.40 s/eval, 1.11 from 8 s. The `worker` run stopped at 38 on the run's own rule (5
  failed evaluations in a row): designs without an `f5_hz`, as in the session-only runs (9-10 of 20 invalid).
- Every comparison identical (25 candidates in the `check` runs, 6 every-5th checks and 12 first answers in the
  `worker` runs); the same design gave the same values whichever OCEAN measured it. Every cell equalled its record;
  the host had nothing left after each run.
- Why no gain: Spectre jobs took as long as before (median 1.37 vs 1.50 s, 4.41 vs 4.86 s) and the wait for the
  preparation did not shrink (0.72 vs 0.60 s, 1.78 vs 1.58 s). The session's measurements were not what held the
  lanes back, so 7.145's reading was wrong: the preparation itself (about 0.3 s per candidate, one at a time) is the
  limit. Starting six OCEANs beside six Spectre jobs made those jobs 3-4 times slower for 10-20 s, which made the
  short runs slower.

**Removed** (user: "Remove it"): `ocean.py` and `recipes.py` are back to `bb1aadb` exactly, and
`test_wb_ocean_worker.py` and `fake_ocean.py` are gone. `docs/workbench.md` keeps a short "tried and removed" note;
the plan (`docs/plan-measurement-off-virtuoso.md`) and this entry keep the measured facts. Runs behave as before
this entry. Scratch tools stay in the session scratchpad (probes, `oceanlog/sitecustomize.py`, `hostcheck.sh`).

Not committed.

**Commit.** `d8b8c4f` (10-02 21:46, pushed), with every other finished change in the tree, on the user's
"commit everything and push" in the session of record 7.139.

### 7.149 Where a candidate's time goes now; runs from the page: concurrency 6 and job folders on the WSL disk; one Virtuoso call less per built candidate; a plan for a Spectre that stays running (2026-10-02, same session as 7.115; user: "go on", then chose "Concurrency 6", "Job folders on WSL disk", "One call less per candidate" and "Spectre that stays running")

**Where a candidate's time goes** (7.143's 6-lane `mscr_ota7t` run, from its tables, Spectre's own log and the stack
samples; nothing re-run). A lane's cycle is about 2.2 s:
- 0.62 s before Spectre starts: the preparation, about 0.3 s, and its queue;
- 1.47 s of Spectre job:
  - Spectre itself about 1.0 s (its log: NDB parsing 0.43 s, the analyses 0.02 s, licensing 0.03 s);
  - the upload and downloads about 0.25 s: the results folder and three log files, each its own transfer, two of the
    files usually missing;
  - the OCEAN measurement about 0.1 s;
- under 0.1 s after the job, the next candidate included: lanes wait 0.03-0.07 s between evaluations, so the engine is
  not a bottleneck.

Over the whole run, start and end included, the preparation thread waited for work half the time. Spectre's start and
model reading, about 0.9 s per job, would go only with a Spectre that stays running; the optimizer's adapter rules
that out ("No ... Spectre runner ... is reimplemented here") until the user lifts it, which the user did (below).

**Runs from the page** were not getting any of this:
- The saved concurrency was 1. Raised to 6 with the workbench's own `runs.save_settings`, at the user's choice. The page
  keeps its own copy of the run settings in the browser and uses the saved one only in a fresh browser, so the user
  sets "Concurrent Spectre jobs" to 6 there once.
- The output root is `/mnt/d/gator/outputs`, a 9p mount. Replayed offline (`fs_replay.py` in the session scratchpad,
  the real files of a kept `mscr_ota7t` job), the medians of 20:

| | WSL disk | `/mnt/d` |
|---|---|---|
| a lane's job folder (19 files, 332 kB, extracted from a tar as the bridge does) | 1.7 ms | 153.5 ms |
| the preparation's files for one candidate (journal twice, export folder) | 1.5 ms | 28.7 ms |
| deleting both afterwards | 0.2 ms | 22 ms |

**Job folders on the WSL disk** (workbench code):
- `layout.local_scratch`: a run whose folder is on a slow mount (`layout.filesystem_type`, read from /proc/mounts: 9p,
  drvfs, cifs, smb3, nfs, nfs4, fuse.sshfs) keeps its simulator scratch in `/tmp/gator-work/<experiment>/<run>`. That
  is on the WSL disk, and WSL empties `/tmp` at every boot. `GATOR_LOCAL_SCRATCH=off` turns it off.
- `lanes.ScratchLinked` wraps the lane factory, last, so the preparation service's factory stays as it is. Each new
  environment's `exports` and `simulations` folders become links into the scratch. The environment's own folder stays
  in the run folder, with its launch journals, candidate records and restoration record. So does the preparation's
  journal in `artifacts/local/bridge-state`.
- Retention (`RunEngine._inside_work`) accepts the scratch as registered: a kept job folder moves to the run folder
  when its result is durable, as before, and the rest are deleted there.
- `RunEngine._clear_scratch`, after the lanes and the preparation service close, removes the links, then the run's
  scratch. Events: `simulator_scratch`.
- Tests: `tests/workbench/test_wb_local_scratch.py` (5): the mount check, the links, a whole run with thread lanes whose
  job folders were in the scratch until retention moved each one, and the same with process lanes.

### 7.150 Run configuration: Mode first, Search method second, RL networks in a separate window (2026-10-02, Codex; user requested the window and ordering)

**Changed.** Mode is the first setting. Immediately below it, **RL networks...** opens a named browser window;
Search method is the second setting. Encoder/sizes, operating-point inputs, Components, publication/baseline presets,
saved-model selection and facts, compatibility details, structure/graph viewers and Advanced RL controls live there.
Budgets, concurrency, seed, reward, training conditions and both failure limits stay in Run configuration. The two
consecutive-failure limits now have their own **Failure limits** fold-out, available to LLM only too.

The existing popup helper copies styles, focuses the same window on another click, explains popup blocking and closes
the window when the workbench leaves. The accessible close icon and browser close keep configuration values. Preset
writes, architecture synchronization and compatibility checks remain mounted in RunPanel. Mode changes update the
window. LLM only disables the launcher and replaces an already open window's controls with an explanation; returning
to SAC restores them. Network settings needing attention leave a notice in the main module and its folded mark.

**Input handling.** The shared popup helper registers each window as a source of native input events for the config
store. Typing in a popup is one Undo step, Redo restores it, and leaving the field ends the step. This retains the
existing main-page behavior while moving the RL inputs. No backend, optimizer or model contract changed.

**Checked.** TypeScript and production build passed. Eleven browser scripts passed 390 assertions across isolated
backends: network_panel 31, model_chooser 65, model_structure 34, graph_view 54, pdk 28, reliability 20, module_fold 29,
mode_suggestion 14, typing 39, connection_panel 48 and working_copy 28. Covered blocked/repeated open, icon/browser
close, reopening, pending preference writes after close, cross-window Undo/Redo and blur, Mode and LLM switching,
model selection and architecture, graph/structure dialogs, component lists and existing windows. The optional
real-model structure check now compares displayed types with the backend's verified/unverified result instead of
assuming the first saved model can always be reconstructed under current libraries. Legacy reward checks follow the
new window selectors and passed syntax checks; fixture-dependent reward/LLM suites were not run. No simulation,
bridge mutation, agent call or user settings write was made by the checks.

Evidence: `artifacts/local/rl-network-window-checks/`, `rl-network-window-popup-checks/`,
`rl-network-window-final-checks/`, `rl-network-shared-window-checks/` (the first two include initial check-harness
failures; the latter runs plus the passing unaffected scripts supply the counts above). Screenshots inspected.
Built after confirming no active runs; port 8765 serves the matching `index-DUGO0LD0.js` and `index-C_IqOQiv.css` assets.
The browser needs a refresh; no server restart is required. `git diff --check` passed. Not committed.

### 7.152 A Spectre that stays running in each lane: built, tried on Spectre, not usable (l and m), removed (2026-10-02, same session as 7.115; user, after record 7.149's G0 and a plain explanation of why a rerun differs: "I'd rather have the ~25% speed-up with those tiny order-dependent differences, build it")

**Decision.** The user accepted that a rerun in a running Spectre starts from the previous candidate's solution, so its
values can differ from a fresh Spectre's in about the sixth digit and depend on the candidates before it. 7.149's G0
measured about 1e-6 relative on the stb loop gain and 5e-11 on DC values; no Spectre setting tried restored exact
agreement. The adapter's rule against a Spectre runner of our own is lifted for it (`docs/decisions.md`). Asked along the
way, in plain words: why a rerun differs (Spectre stops iterating once within its tolerance, and a different starting
point stops elsewhere), and what happens over many runs and changing netlists. Errors do not pile up, but values become
order-dependent. Oscillators can settle in another operating point, which the checks below would catch. Only values may
change between decks; anything else starts a new session.

**Built** (off unless `GATOR_SPECTRE_SESSION` is `on` or `check`; Maestro-measured tasks only):
- the optimizer's hook: `VirtuosoBridgeBackend._new_simulator` takes a circuit profile's own simulator for the lane
  (`lane_simulator`), the bridge's fresh Spectre otherwise. The module's docstring names the exception.
- `host_process.py`, generalised from the probes of 7.148 and 7.149:
  - the one-line host script runs the process in a folder and process session of its own, with its quit command
    (`sclQuit()`) at the end of the lane's input or after 15 minutes idle, then kills the leftovers;
  - ssh with the bridge's options on a connection of its own;
  - requests answered between tagged markers, keeping what each request printed.
- `spectre_session.py`:
  - `deck_changes` compares two decks statement by statement and finds every placement of a changed subcircuit device;
    anything beyond values is refused in words;
  - `spectre_literal` writes '2.105u' as '2.105e-6', so that it reads as the same double;
  - `run_body` sets each value, reads it back, then runs every analysis into the job's own folder;
  - `LaneSpectre` holds the rules: a fresh check every 5th run of a session, the fresh values kept for that candidate; a
    difference above 1e-4 relative (`SESSION_RTOL`) or two failed sessions send the lane back to fresh runs, recorded; a
    deck that changes in more than values starts a new session;
  - `validate_run` is a session run's completion evidence: its answer, no error line in its own output, and every
    analysis reported done there or present as a results file;
  - `check_fresh`.
- `recipes.GenericMaestroProfile.lane_simulator`; `analyze` validates session runs, measures the fresh check and records
  `simulated_by`, `fresh_check` and `session_note`.
- Tests: `tests/workbench/test_wb_spectre_session.py` (17), with a stand-in interactive Spectre
  (`tests/workbench/fake_spectre.py`) that behaves as the real one did in G0. Whole suite: 1,617 passed, 1 skipped, 1 xfailed.

Optimizer digest `f83342bc…` -> `1f71381d9f3925137fb8c35d369572339544680a60da10e512578fabe2afbfe2` (`docs/decisions.md`).
Docs: `docs/workbench.md` ("A Spectre that stays running in each lane"), `docs/functionality-reference.md` (one row),
`docs/plan-persistent-spectre.md` (G1 result).

**On the real Spectre** (the user's "Smoke test + trials"):
- Smoke test: one session, three designs, two fresh checks, on a kept `mscr_ota7t` deck. The protocol works:
  - answers arrived at once over the pipe, and `sclRun` returned `t`;
  - each run's analysis output (274 lines) arrived before its answer, so both analyses were confirmed from it;
  - a session run took 0.054-0.062 s, its results were copied back, and the host was clean afterwards.
- Trial 1 (`mscr_ota7t`, 6 lanes, 20 evaluations, every design also simulated fresh): every session's first design agreed
  with its fresh run, and every session's second design differed far beyond the tolerance (gain 263 against 342 and 87
  against 50; bias current up to 98%). The checks worked: each lane retired its session at that first difference, and
  the run finished on fresh runs, keeping the fresh values. The cell equalled its record.
- The cause, isolated with probes on trial 1's decks (both sides in text format, compared numerically): the differing
  designs had changed `l`, `m` and `sca`/`scb`/`scc`.
  - Only `sca`/`scb`/`scc` changed: identical to a fresh Spectre.
  - Only `l`: DC up to 1% off. Only `m`: DC up to 98% off.
  - The same changes made through alter analyses did not help, whether one re-elaborating alter after setting the
    values or one alter per value. One sequence of 40 alters crashed Spectre (segmentation fault).
  - G0 had changed only `w`, which a running Spectre does follow.

So, whatever command is used, a running Spectre does not end up equal to a fresh Spectre given the same `l` and `m` for
this PDK's transistors. Sizing changes `l` and `m`, so the session cannot give correct values. Trials 2 and 3 were not
run; about 65 of the approved 150 Spectre runs were used.

**Follow-up** (2026-10-03, the user: "wait have you thought that the multiplier only works with width?", then "Yes, ~4
Spectre runs"). In `mscr_ota7t` the optimizer never changes `w`: its groups are `m` (M1-M7 and the capacitor CC) and `l`.
So here the multiplier is the width control (total width = w x m), and G0's working case had changed a parameter this
task never changes. Untested until then was whether a width write makes a running Spectre pick up new `m` and `l`. Two
probes on trial 1's disagreeing pair, each a fresh Spectre and a session:
- every `m`/`l`/`sc*` change, then each changed device's unchanged `w` re-written: loop gain -25.4 in the session, -61.6
  fresh, DC up to 98% off as before;
- the same, with each `w` set to 1% more and then back to its exact value: the session's results identical to the
  previous probe's to the last digit.

So forcing a width recalculation does not make a running Spectre take the new `m` or `l`; the removal stands.

**Design variables** (2026-10-03, the user: "try the method of write the sized values as design variables and see if it
improves"; nine Spectre runs on trial 1's disagreeing pair, no cell write). Each device's `l`, `m`, `sca`, `scb` and `scc`
referred to a variable of its own (`l=gl_M7 ...`), set in one added `parameters` statement.
- A fresh Spectre on the deck with variables gave exactly the plain-number deck's results (25/25 DC, 603/603 stb values).
- Set directly in a running Spectre (`sclGetParameter(sclGetCircuit("") "gl_M7")`, `sclSetAttribute`), the variables took
  their values, but the circuit did not change: the results equalled the old design's (loop gain -292.27; the target
  -86.8).
- Changed through `alter`, re-elaborating each time or, as Spectre's help recommends, only at the last: Spectre crashed both
  times (segmentation fault).
- On the way, Spectre's own list of circuits showed each transistor (`DUT.M7`, master `g45n1svt`) as a subcircuit with a
  gate-resistor subcircuit inside (`DUT.M7.xrg`, `ressnpoly_rg`), which fits the transistors being built at load time.

So design variables do not help: set, they are not re-read; altered, Spectre crashes. The removal stands.

**Removed** (the user's "Remove it"):
- the optimizer's hook and the module docstring's sentence are gone; the digest is back to 7.149's `f83342bc…`, and the
  adapter's rule against a Spectre runner of our own stands again;
- `recipes.py` is back to the committed version;
- `spectre_session.py`, `host_process.py`, `test_wb_spectre_session.py` and `fake_spectre.py` are gone.

`docs/workbench.md` keeps a "tried and removed" note; this entry, `docs/decisions.md` and `docs/plan-persistent-spectre.md`
keep the findings. Scratch tools stay in the session scratchpad (`smoke7152.py`, `g1_classes.py`, `g2_alter.py`,
`g3_reelab.py`, `g4_alters.py`, `session_summary.py`).

Not committed.

**Commit.** `a16bdd1` (10-03 18:26, pushed): its docs and `docs/plan-persistent-spectre.md`; the session code had been removed before, on the user's "commit and push 7.149, 7.152 and 7.153 too".

### 7.153 A trimmed model library: exact for mscr_ota7t; saves about 0.25 s of model reading per Spectre job, noisy (2026-10-03, same session as 7.115; user: "but for my case is there any method to accelerate? I am changing parameters on one topology", then chose "Test a trimmed model file" and "Timing runs first")

An investigation: no code change, no cell write, no Virtuoso session. Fifteen Spectre runs on the simulator host, on the
kept `mscr_ota7t` deck. No model text was shown or copied.

**Why.** Every fresh Spectre reads the deck's `include ".../gpdk045.scs" section=tt`, which loads 22 sub-sections from 9
files:
- every transistor family: standard, high- and low-Vt, native, 1.8 V and varactors, about 51,000 lines of a 4 MB file;
- diodes, bipolars, the MOS-capacitor file (1.1 MB), resistors, an inductor and the SOA checks.

The circuit uses `g45n1svt`, `g45p1svt` and `g45cmim`. Mapped by names and line numbers only:
- `gpdk045_mos.scs` section `tt` brings `stat_mis_std` and `mos` (the standard-Vt devices), and `mos` brings `res_rg`, the
  gate resistors inside each transistor;
- `gpdk045_mimcap.scs` section `tt3_mim` brings `cap3` (the MIM capacitor).

**The trimmed library:** a 7-line file on the host whose `tt` section includes only `gpdk045_mos.scs` section `tt`,
`gpdk045_mimcap.scs` section `tt3_mim` and `gpdk045_soa.scs` section `OFF`, referring to the PDK's own files.

**Exactness** (values sections compared, in text format): DC (25 values), stb (603), the stability margins, every device
instance's parameters (`element.info`) and the model parameters of the devices used (`modelParameter.info`) are all
identical. Only two inventories of defined but unused things differ, as they must: `designParamVals.info` (design
parameters) and `subckts.info` (subcircuits). A first whole-file checksum had reported every file different because of
the dates and paths in the headers.

**Speed** (one session, a warm-up run, then full and trimmed alternating, four each; the medians):

| | full library | trimmed |
|---|---|---|
| Spectre's model reading ("Time for NDB Parsing", elapsed) | 0.73 s | 0.47 s |
| Spectre's total ("Time used") | 1.21 s | 1.08 s |
| wall clock per run | 1.04 s | 0.95 s |

The host is noisy: the same run varies by about 0.5 s (model reading 0.24-0.95 s), and the first trimmed run took
2.64 s. So the saving is about 0.25 s of model reading per job, and 0.1-0.45 s of Spectre's total.

What it would take in runs: each task's deck would include its own small library file on the host. The sections would
be derived from the netlist's devices and verified identical as above, per task. Today the deck must name the
connection's model file, so this is a small code change. The gain is small while the one-at-a-time Virtuoso
preparation limits 6 lanes (about 5-10% expected), and larger once that limit goes. Nothing built. Not committed.

**Commit.** `a16bdd1` (10-03 18:26, pushed), docs only, on the user's "commit and push 7.149, 7.152 and 7.153 too".

### 7.154 A trimmed model library for any circuit: a real run's decks load only the parts of the model file its devices need (2026-10-03, same session as 7.115; user: "build the trimmed library for any circuit")

Workbench code only, so no optimizer digest step. No Spectre run and no cell write; one read-only look at the real
library's layout on the simulator host, by names only.

**What it does** (`gator_workbench/model_library.py`). At the start of a real run, where the run's environment is built
(`tasks._generated_runtime`), `attach`:
- reads the model file's layout on the simulator host with an awk script, run by bash through the bridge's own SSH runner
  (the one its SpectreSimulator uses; locally when that host is this machine). Out come names and numbers only: each
  file's sections, the includes in each, the subcircuits and models defined outside any subcircuit, the masters used
  inside subcircuits, and a count of other statements. Spectre syntax and SPICE syntax (`.lib` sections, `.subckt`,
  `.model`, `+` continuations) are read. Includes are followed in rounds: GPDK045 took 2 calls, 0.66 s, 9 files, 154
  sections;
- lists the masters of the circuit's instances (the saved structure, plus a Maestro deck's head and tail);
- keeps, for every section of the model file, the parts it includes that define one of those devices, then the parts
  that the kept parts' subcircuits use, then every part that defines no device at all (switches such as GPDK045's
  SOA checks), in the library's order. A section with statements of its own besides its includes is left whole, and so
  is one from which nothing could be left out.

The decks (the Maestro recipe's head, the ring recipe's header, and the op-amp recipe's deck through a new
`GenericMosWidthProfile.build_deck` equal to the adapter's) then carry a comment line and the kept parts' include lines,
absolute paths into the PDK's own folder, in place of the one model include of their own section. The run event
`model_library` names the parts kept, or why the whole file is included.

**When.** Only for a task with a recorded baseline. That baseline was simulated with the whole file (a Single baseline
simulation has no baseline yet, so it includes the whole file, and a reference is never replaced). Every simulated reset
of the start design is compared with it at 1e-6 by `NativeEnvironmentFactory`, which stops the run on a difference. A
part left out that a device needs makes Spectre stop with an undefined-subcircuit error. The corner check includes the
whole file: it has no baseline at the corners, and generated profiles are nominal-only anyway.
`GATOR_MODEL_LIBRARY=full` turns it off. A first-run comparison of the whole and the trimmed file (two more Spectre runs
per task) was not built: those runs would sit outside the simulations a run is authorized for.

**What the MScR tasks get** (read-only planning against the real library; every section: tt, ff, ss, fs, sf, mc):

| task | devices | parts of section tt kept |
|---|---|---|
| `mscr_ota7t`, `mscr_ring3` | `g45n1svt`, `g45p1svt`, `g45cmim` | 3 of 23: `gpdk045_mos.scs` `tt`, `gpdk045_mimcap.scs` `tt3_mim`, `gpdk045_soa.scs` `OFF` (7.153's file) |
| `mscr_vco_lin` | `g45n1svt`, `g45p1svt` | 2 of 23: `gpdk045_mos.scs` `tt`, `gpdk045_soa.scs` `OFF` |
| `mscr_ota5t`, `mscr_ldo_basic` | `g45n2svt`, `g45p2svt` | 2 of 23: `gpdk045_mos.scs` `tt_18`, `gpdk045_soa.scs` `OFF` |
| `mscr_ldo1_rgcn`, `mscr_ldo2_rgcn` | `g45n2svt`, `g45p2svt`, `g45cmim` | 3 of 23: `gpdk045_mos.scs` `tt_18`, `gpdk045_mimcap.scs` `tt3_mim`, `gpdk045_soa.scs` `OFF` |

**Checked.** `tests/workbench/test_wb_model_library.py` (12): a made-up library in GPDK045's shape read with the system's
gawk and with mawk (identical), the parts kept per section and per corner, a part a kept subcircuit uses, sections left
whole, the deck rewrite (exactly one include of that file and section, else the deck is left alone), devices of a deck,
when a run gets the library, paths with shell characters refused, the three recipes' decks, and the run event. Full suite:
1,612 passed, 1 skipped, 1 xfailed.

**On Spectre** (user: "Check + timing run"):
- 14 standalone runs, no Virtuoso: each MScR task's kept deck with the whole file and with its trimmed library, through
  the bridge's SpectreSimulator, in text format. Every result file compared from its first TYPE/VALUE section on is
  identical (7, 6, 6, 9, 11, 10 and 10 files); only the two inventories of defined things differ, as they must
  (`designParamVals.info`, `subckts.info`). Single runs on a noisy host: model reading 0.13-0.41 s shorter per job (median
  0.25 s; the 1.8 V tasks gain most, about 0.4 s), Spectre's total 0.19-0.80 s shorter.
- One run, `mscr_ota7t`, 6 lanes, 40 evaluations, all on the WSL disk. None of the tasks has a recorded baseline (below),
  so for this run the scratch settings copy got one first: `profiles.record_reference` from 7.143's 6-lane run of the
  same design, the whole file, no new simulation. The run's event said trimmed, 3 of 23 parts in every corner section (4
  of 24 in `mc`); its decks carry the three include lines. All 6 simulated resets matched the baseline at 1e-6; the cell
  equals its record afterwards. Inside the run Spectre took 0.72-0.79 s per job (4 kept jobs) against 0.99-1.10 s in
  7.143's run, with model reading 0.26-0.47 s against 0.43-0.45 s.
- But no gain in throughput: 0.43 s per evaluation against 0.41 s (7.143, 20 evaluations). A job's span in the tables,
  which includes the OCEAN measurement, went from 1.46 to 1.40 s, and the wait for the preparation from 0.73 to 0.83 s.
  The one Virtuoso session prepares every candidate and measures every Maestro result (7.145), so the lanes that finish
  Spectre sooner wait there instead. The saving shows where Spectre is the limit: long transient jobs (`mscr_vco_lin`),
  fewer lanes, or once that session is no longer the limit.

**Not on yet in the user's runs:** no MScR task in the live settings has a recorded baseline
(`reference_measurements.json`), so their runs include the whole file - and run without the baseline comparison at all,
as before. A "Single baseline simulation" of each task (one Spectre run each, from the page header) records it; after

### 7.155 Agent tab model lists: Codex's from the Codex app's own file, Claude Code's table brought up to date (2026-10-03, session "Frontend agent tab list update"; user: "frontend agent tab agent list is not updated. Is there a method to update that?", then "build option 1 and update the Claude list too")

Workbench code only (`src/gator_workbench/`), no optimizer digest step. No model was called, no Spectre run.

**Why.** Both lists were a table kept by hand (`agents/catalog.py`, last checked 2026-09-30), and nothing refreshed
them: by 2026-10-03 Codex offered `gpt-6.1-sol` as its first choice (`gpt-6-sol` now "previous generation") plus three
older `gpt-5.6-*` models the table lacked. Neither CLI can list its models, and the vendors' model endpoints need an
API key the workbench never holds. But the Codex desktop app keeps the models the signed-in account may use in
`models_cache.json` in its home folder, refreshes it by itself (fetched 15:50 and again 16:04 UTC that day), and the
file carries each model's effort levels, default effort and retirement date.

**What changed.**
- `catalog.codex_entry()`: the Codex list is that file whenever it can be read - `GATOR_CODEX_MODELS_CACHE` (a file, or
  `off`), else `$CODEX_HOME`, `~/.codex` and `/mnt/c/Users/*/.codex` (the desktop app seen from WSL); the most recently
  written wins. Only `visibility: "list"` models, in the app's order; a name must look like a model name (it is passed
  as `--model`); each model keeps its own levels; a retiring one is labelled `(retires YYYY-MM-DD)` and its note carries
  the app's migration line. Nothing else of the file is taken (its `identity` part is never served). Without a usable
  file the hand table is served with `cache_note` saying why. The routes' settings read searches again each time (~8 ms);
  validation (`efforts_for`, `model_entry`, `supports_effort`) reuses the place found and re-reads only a changed file
  (~0.4 ms). An effort level only the app knows becomes valid for Codex.
- Claude Code table re-checked against code.claude.com/docs/en/model-config and the API price list: added the pinned
  `claude-opus-4-8`, `-4-7`, `-4-6`, `claude-sonnet-4-6` (4.6: no xhigh) and `claude-sonnet-4-5` (no effort), `best`
  now says "Fable where available, otherwise Opus", newest-first order. Left out on purpose: `default` (= "CLI
  default"), `opusplan` (Opus only in plan mode, which `claude -p` never enters), the `[1m]` spellings (the current
  models already run with 1M context on the Anthropic API). The Codex hand table (the fallback) got `gpt-6.1-sol`.
- Agent tab (`AgentTab.tsx`): the hint says where the list comes from - "List from the Codex app (your account's models,
  fetched <time>)" or "List checked by hand on <date>[; <reason>]" - and, for Codex only, a text action "Re-read list"
  (the same `GET /api/agent/settings`, nothing else sent).

**Checks.** `tests/workbench/test_wb_agent_catalog.py` +4 tests (the app file: order, hidden/unsafe/repeated names,
levels, retirement, no account data, a changed file re-read; fallback reasons; where it is looked for and newest wins;
the route); `tests/conftest.py` sets `GATOR_CODEX_MODELS_CACHE=off` so no test reads the user's file. New hermetic
browser check `frontend/scripts/agent_model_list_check.mjs` (14 checks, fixtures only) passed on a scratch build;
`agent_tab_check.mjs` (97 checks) passed on a scratch build in front of a temporary backend that read the real Codex
file (8 models, `gpt-6.1-sol` first, `gpt-5.5 (retires 2026-10-14)`). Live `frontend/dist` not rebuilt, 8765 not
restarted: the new lists appear after the user restarts the backend and the page is rebuilt.

app ships a Linux build (`codex-cli 0.160.0`) under `C:\Users\<name>\.codex\bin\wsl\<hash>\codex`, but the hash folder
changes with every app update. Nothing was linked.

### 7.157 Maestro's six unread info outputs left out of run decks: about 0.36 s less per Spectre job, the same results (2026-10-03, same session as 7.115; user: "check not the whole run but during one spectre evaluation", then "yes, run the info-output test", "yes, run the OCEAN check", "run it", "yes, build it")

An investigation first (one evaluation step by step, then the test), then built. Workbench code only.

**One evaluation, step by step** (medians of the four jobs 7.154's 6-lane `mscr_ota7t` run kept; its tables, the job
manifests, the lanes' launch journals, the bridge's own step times and Spectre's log):

| step | where | seconds |
|---|---|---|
| wait for the preparation | queue | 0.24 (none at one lane) |
| preparation: write, read back, build the netlist | Virtuoso | 0.30 (about 1.0 with a native export, every 5th) |
| hand-over to the lane, checks, circuit graph | WSL | about 0.13 |
| upload of the deck | SSH | 0.12 |
| login shell and the Cadence setup file on the host | host | about 0.12 (`sh -l` 0.06, `csh` + setup file 0.06) |
| Spectre: netlist and models read | host | 0.26-0.47 |
| Spectre: elaboration, stb and dc | host | about 0.07 |
| Spectre: the six info outputs and exit | host | about 0.2-0.34 |
| download of the results | SSH | 0.09 |
| OCEAN measurement and result files | Virtuoso | 0.16 (up to 0.7 while Virtuoso is busy) |
| recorded | WSL | 0.06 |
| dispatch to recorded | | 1.9 (1.76-2.68) |

Spectre's own end-of-run clock ("Time used", "wall clock") is no wall time here: in two jobs it is longer than the
bridge's whole remote command. Its accumulated time where the last analysis ends is used instead.

**The info outputs.** A Maestro deck's tail asks for `modelParameter`, `element`, `outputParameter`, `designParamVals`,
`primitives` and `subckts` info, written to the results folder: 232 of the 268 kB a job downloads. Nothing in the
workbench or the measurements reads them (searched); `dcOpInfo`, the operating points of the other recipes, is not among
them.

**The test** (`$SP/ml_info_test.py`): the current run deck of `mscr_ota7t` (trimmed library) with and without the six
statements, alternating, three runs each, in the format real runs use for a Maestro task:

| | with the info outputs | without |
|---|---|---|
| wall per job | 1.11-1.14 s | 0.73-0.81 s |
| bridge's remote command | 1.03-1.05 s | 0.66-0.73 s |
| Spectre's clock where the analyses end | 0.67-0.80 s | 0.41-0.47 s |
| after the analyses | 0.24-0.36 s | 0.23-0.28 s |
| results folder | 239 kB, 11 files | 17 kB, 5 files |

Median 0.36 s less per job (about a third). Most of it comes before the analyses end, not only from writing the files.
`dc.dc`, `stb.stb` and `stb.margin.stb` are identical in every pair after their headers (the first 584 bytes: date and
job folder); only `logStatus` differs, as the list of what ran.

**What building it would take:** leave those six statements out of a run's decks in the Maestro recipe, except one
whose result an output expression names, with a comment line in the deck saying so. Kept results would no longer carry
the files ADE's results browser shows. At one lane an evaluation gets about 0.35 s shorter; at six lanes the Virtuoso
session stays the limit (7.154), so it mainly frees host CPU there.

**Is anything else affected?** (user: "you sure dropping the six info outputs will not break any other type of
evaluations?") Checked:
- nothing in the optimizer, the workbench or the frontend reads those six files;
- the decks GATOR builds itself (ring oscillator, op-amp, LDO, VCO) have no info output but `dcOpInfo`, the operating
  points; the six come only with a Maestro test's tail;
- `validate_completion` reads Spectre's completion summary and error markers only;
- of the calculator functions a task's outputs may use (`virtuoso_setup.FUNCTIONS`), only `VAR` (a design variable) and
  `getData(... ?result "<one of the six>")` could read them (`pv` is not allowed); none of the 55 outputs of the seven
  MScR tasks does. A task whose outputs did would keep all six. Five tasks also have `finalTimeOP` (the transient's
  final operating point, read only by `OPT`, used by none); it would stay.

**The OCEAN check** (user: "yes, run the OCEAN check"; `$SP/ml_ocean_check.py`): the `mscr_ota7t` and `mscr_vco_lin`
run decks with and without the six, four standalone Spectre runs with the results kept on the host, each folder then
measured with GATOR's own `ocean.measure` in the Virtuoso session (read-only, no cell write); the four host folders
deleted afterwards. All 10 outputs identical: gain 0.8101 and bias current 1.1261 mA of the OTA, three frequencies and
the power of the VCO. Four outputs were empty in both variants because of the designs themselves: the OTA design (a kept
measurement failure) has a loop gain below 1, so no unity-gain frequency or phase margin, and two VCO frequencies.

Repeated on designs whose every output was valid (user: "run it"; the newest such kept job of each task, the OTA's from
7.154's trimmed-library run): all 10 outputs have values and are identical with and without the six - gain 292.267,
unity-gain frequency 24.832 MHz, phase margin 74.720 degrees, bias current 0.831 mA; the VCO's five frequencies (1.535 to
0.922 GHz) and power 0.388 mW - and each also equals, to the last digit, what the original run recorded for that design.
Four Spectre runs, read-only OCEAN, the host folders deleted.

**Built** (user: "yes, build it"): `recipes.without_unread_info(tail, outputs)` leaves the six statements out of a
Maestro deck's tail, with continuation lines, and puts one comment line where they were;
`GenericMaestroProfile.build_deck` applies it. It keeps the tail as it is when an output expression uses `VAR` or names
one of the six, when the tail has none of them, or with `GATOR_INFO_OUTPUTS=keep`; another value is refused. The
operating points (`dcOpInfo`, `finalTimeOP`) stay.
- The code's decks for the two decks of the OCEAN check have exactly the statements of the decks simulated and measured
  there without the six.
- All seven MScR tasks' saved decks lose exactly the six; `finalTimeOP` stays in the five with a transient, and every
  deck still ends with `saveOptions`.
- `tests/workbench/test_wb_info_outputs.py` (7). Full suite: 1,623 passed, 1 skipped, 1 xfailed.

**A short real run** (user: "yes, do the short run"): `mscr_ota7t`, one lane, 20 evaluations, scratch settings with
the baseline recorded in 7.154 (whole library, info outputs present). All 20 evaluations valid; every deck carries the
comment line, none of the six and the trimmed library; each results folder holds 16.7 kB. The one simulated reset of the
start design matched that baseline at 1e-6, so the trimmed library and the leaner deck together reproduce it. The cell
equals its record afterwards. One evaluation, medians:

| step | seconds |
|---|---|
| preparation (no queue at one lane) | 0.22 |
| upload | 0.02 |
| remote: shell and Spectre (models read 0.27, analyses done at 0.33) | 0.58 |
| download | 0.05 |
| OCEAN measurement and result files | 0.10 |
| dispatch to recorded | 1.28 (0.83-1.92) |

1.30 s per evaluation in the run's loop, against 1.99 s at one lane in 7.136, before the built netlists (7.142), the
window check before exports only (7.149), the trimmed library (7.154) and this.

### 7.158 The page's two looks and layout moves: Whiteprint and Silicon Night by one switch, every colour by name, a title-block header, a splitter above the bottom panel, one-line ids in Data tables, each editor's colour, the circuit picture (2026-10-03, session "Frontend workbench layout design"; user: "For the frontend workbench layout, do you have any other more artistic ideas, give me plan and picture", then, on the pictured page, "1 A+B, 2 yes, 3 yes, 4 yes, 5 yes, 6 Windows")

Frontend only: no route, schema or optimizer change. No simulation.

**The ideas.** A private page, "GATOR Workbench Looks" (claude.ai artifact `SQ8cnK3csPpNkhQQTbD9PQ`), drew three looks in
full with the rows of `real:mscr_ldo2_rgcn`, five layout moves, a plan and six numbered decisions. The looks: A Whiteprint
(a drawing sheet), B Silicon Night (Virtuoso's black canvas), C Bench Instrument (a scope's front panel). A and B were
also shown on the live page: their stylesheets were added in a throwaway headless Edge, every write was refused and
nothing in the repository changed. The user chose A + B as a day / night pair, the header switch, the title-block
header, the splitter, the circuit picture and Windows' own fonts. The "REAL evaluation backend" notice had been removed
at 17:05 by record 7.156, and the plan left it out.

**Colours by name first, with no visible change.** `styles.css` wrote 86 colour values into 69 rules. Each became a
named colour carrying exactly its value: 50 names. The fills that carry white text got names of their own
(`--accent-fill`, `--bad-fill`, `--warn-fill`, `--on-warn`), so a dark look can darken a fill without changing a text
colour of the same hue. Proof: seven views of the page on the live backend, from a build of the tree before and one
after, taken in a write-refusing headless Edge. The views covered the LDO task and its baseline run: Parameters, Specs
and Probes with Run status, Data tables, Logs, Agent, Output files, Corner check, and the Runs window. Six were
byte-identical. The Runs window differs only in a 23 × 9 px box, its "read at" time, where two shots of the unchanged
build differ as well.

**The looks** (`theme.ts` and `themes.css` new, `index.html`, `main.tsx`, `styles.css`, `popupWindow.ts`, `Icons.tsx`,
`CircuitGraphView.tsx`, `App.tsx`):
- `styles.css` `:root` holds Whiteprint's values. `themes.css` holds Silicon Night's, plus what each look adds:
  - Whiteprint: the paper grid, the sheet frame with zone letters A-H / 1-4, the double-ruled header, sheet tabs, ruled
    table heads, numbered editor tabs.
  - Silicon Night: the dot grid, alignment marks, the seal-ring header, a hatched layer swatch per editor, a glowing
    Start.
- A moon / sun icon at the left of the header switches the look (`data-theme` on `<html>`). The choice is kept in this
  browser (`gator-workbench-look-v1`) and set by `index.html` before the page draws.
- Every pop-up window takes the look when it opens and follows the switch (`followLook`).
- The graph view reads its canvas, line and label colours from the look, and restyles on a switch.
- The fonts are Windows' own: Bahnschrift for headings, tabs and table heads, Segoe UI for text, Consolas for numbers.
- Selectors that would outrank a rule of meaning (an editor's colour, an error row) are wrapped in `:where()`.

**Layout moves:**
- **Title block** (`App.tsx` header, `Left.tsx` `TitleFacts` through a portal): Task, Rev, Class · PDK, and since 7.159
  the RL model label. The version and the environment are in the tooltip of the name.
- **Splitter** (`App.tsx`):
  - drag, double-click to fold to the tab line, or keys (arrows 24 px, Enter);
  - a press on a bottom tab, the header's run line or an agent request opens it, Start does not;
  - kept in this browser (`gator-workbench-bottom-v1`); the editors keep the window less 280 px;
  - none below 1100 px.
- **Data tables** (`RunTabs.tsx`): ids in one line ending in "...", the whole value on hover and copied by a click;
  times kept whole; no word cut; JSON in a bounded column.
- **Each editor's colour** (`Editors.tsx` `data-editor`): on the tab and the table head; the Specs line's Probe ids
  are chips with the same text.
- **Circuit picture** (`CircuitPicture.tsx` new, in the Project column under the task):
  - the summary's DUT devices (else every non-source device), placed by `hintedLayout` from `circuitLayout.ts`, the
    graph view's own;
  - a supply the circuit does not mark is found as the net a voltage source holds against ground that devices conduct
    from (VDD of the MScR LDO); names are drawn without a shared hierarchy;
  - pointing at a Parameters row lights its devices and the rows tied to it (`pointAt`): `w_pair` lights M1 and M2;
  - folded at first like every block of that column, the user's rule that `project_help_check` pins; the fold is kept
    (`gator-workbench-circuit-picture-v1`).

**Checks:**

- Five existing checks pinned what this changed:

### 7.159 The Project name box removed; the RL model gets a label of its own (2026-10-03, same session as 7.158; user: "remove the project name box in the Project module. Give the RL model its own label, independent of the project name.")

**What the project name did.** One thing: `prepare_run` remembered each project's newest run (`settings.json`
`last_run[project.name]`). A Mode that loads a model (resume, fine_tune, frozen) then started from that run's `latest`
save by itself (`GET /api/runs/default-model?project=`).

**Changed:**
- **Project column**: no Project name box, and no "Project" heading over nothing; the column starts with Registered
  task. A configuration keeps its `project.name`, unshown. The import confirmation calls the section "project labels".
- **RL model label** (Run configuration, below Search method; `run.model_label`, at most 80 characters, disabled for
  LLM only). Empty, it is the task's run label, shown as the placeholder. `runs.model_label(config)` gives the label in
  use.
- **Remembered under the label**, and only by runs that save an RL model (`runs.saves_rl_model`: save points on, not
  LLM only). A Single baseline simulation no longer pushes a model out, as it did under a project name.
- **The default model**: `GET /api/runs/default-model?label=&project=` looks for the label, then for the project name
  runs were remembered under before. A configuration from before still finds its model.
- **Seen**: the run manifest carries `model_label`; `saved_models` passes it on. The model's card is named by it (else
  the task name, as before), and the filter finds it. The title block shows it in place of the project name. A folded
  Run configuration shows a typed one.
- **Hashes**: `_hashed` treats an empty label as absent, so configurations and runs from before keep their
  `config_sha256`. A label changes no task hash.

**Tests:**
- `tests/workbench/test_wb_model_label.py`, 6 tests: the label in use, hash stability, which runs save a model, the
  manifest and settings, a baseline leaving the label alone, the default model by label and by the old name, and the
  card's label.
- Browser checks that typed into the Project name box use the label box: `blocking_issues`, `model_chooser`, `typing`,
  `task_rows` (its typed name became a typed label; the old name is checked in the saved configuration).
  `virtuoso_source` expects the one heading, Registered task; `looks_check` the title block's new cell.
- Full browser run after both records (37 scripts): 1,365 checks passed, with two failures:
  - `network_panel`: the label box first stood between Mode and Search method, the order 7.150 pins. It was moved below
    Search method and passed.
  - `spec_equations`: a storage-and-reload race in a flow neither record touches. It passed in three reruns.
- After the move, the seven affected scripts passed (310 checks).
- Python suite (`-n 12 --dist loadgroup`): 1,629 passed, 1 skipped, 1 xfailed. `tsc --noEmit` clean.

### 7.161 Preparation in one Virtuoso call (option C of 7.138) measured: about 0.01 s per candidate, not worth it; every Virtuoso call waited about 44 ms in the bridge's SSH tunnel, fixed by starting the tunnel with a terminal (2026-10-03, same session as 7.115; user: "Preparation in one Virtuoso call (option C from 7.138) what does it do in plain words?", then "yes, run the Virtuoso timing test")

An investigation: nothing built. On `mscr_ota7t`'s working copy through the real run adapter (`CdfBridgeBackend`: session
lock, window check, the cell equal to its record at connect); the adapter wrote the recorded sizes back when it closed.
No Spectre.

**The test** (`$SP/optc_timing.py`): 12 designs alternating today's preparation - `set_parameters`: read the cell, write
through the PDK form, read again, three calls, then the checks and the crash journal - with one call carrying the same
three SKILL programs (the bridge's own read program twice around `cdf_write.write_skill`), both readings parsed by the
bridge's own parser, then the same checks and journal.

| | median | range |
|---|---|---|
| today, three calls | 0.139 s | 0.127-0.269 s |
| one call | 0.130 s | 0.094-0.224 s |
| of which the Virtuoso call itself | 0.105 s | 0.078-0.202 s |
| one whole-cell read alone | 0.025 s | 0.024-0.110 s |
| an empty call (`1+1`) | 0.045 s | 0.045-0.047 s |

The one call saves about 0.01 s per candidate: the work inside Virtuoso (the form write with the PDK's callbacks, two
reads) is what costs, not the trips. Not worth a change of the optimizer package. In runs the preparation took about
0.22 s per candidate at one lane (7.157); the 0.08 s beyond this test's 0.14 s is GATOR's own work in the preparation
process (the netlist built from the readback, its files and hashes, the hand-over to the lane), not Virtuoso.

**Small replies wait.** Read-only calls by the size of their reply (`$SP/reply_size_probe.py`, ten each): `t` and `1+1`
44 ms every time, a 1 kB string 48 ms, a 10 kB string 22-101 ms (median 50 ms), while a whole-cell read (a larger reply)
took 25 ms. A floor of about 40 ms for a short answer is the pattern of small network packets waiting for an
acknowledgement, somewhere between the bridge's client, the SSH tunnel and the daemon on the host - not Virtuoso's work.
Each evaluation has at least two calls with a short answer (the write, the OCEAN measurement).

**Where the 44 ms sits** (user: "locate the 44 ms delay first"; read-only, `1+1` ten times per path,
`$SP/delay_probe.py`, `delay_probe_host.py`, `delay_probe_port.py`):

| path of one call | median |
|---|---|
| through the bridge's SSH tunnel, as runs go | 47.8 ms (44.1 in a second series) |
| the same, with TCP_NODELAY on the WSL client's socket | 52.0 ms |
| from WSL straight to the bridge's service on the host (it listens on 0.0.0.0:65221), no SSH | 3.1 ms |
| on the host itself, straight to the service (Python 2.7, signed with the host's token) | 1.8 ms |
| through a temporary second SSH tunnel started with a terminal (`ssh -tt -L 65300:...`, closed after) | 2.3 ms |

Virtuoso, the bridge's service and Cadence's relay (`cdsServIpc`) take about 2 ms. The bridge's tunnel is `ssh -N -L
65221:127.0.0.1:65221` (OpenSSH 8.9): without a terminal, OpenSSH leaves Nagle's algorithm on, on both ends, so every
short request or answer waits for a delayed acknowledgement, about 40 ms. A tunnel with a terminal has it off. Each call
opens a fresh connection, so every call pays it once at least.

**Fixed** (user: "apply fix 1 and restart the tunnel now"): the bridge checkout `~/virtuoso-bridge-lite` (a third-party
package, outside this repository) now starts its tunnel in `transport/ssh.py` `SSHRunner.start_port_forward` as
`ssh ... -tt -L 65221:127.0.0.1:65221 <host> sleep 2147483647` instead of `-N`: a session with a terminal that only
sleeps, so OpenSSH turns Nagle's algorithm off on both ends; killing the ssh hangs up the terminal and ends the sleep.
The tunnel was restarted with the bridge's own CLI (`virtuoso-bridge stop`, then `start`, `--env
~/.virtuoso-bridge/.env`); its port check saw the running service as the user's own and kept 65221, and the service in
Virtuoso kept running. No run was active; the busy sessions were told. After it:

| call | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| `1+1` / `t` | 44.0 / 44.4 ms | 2.7 / 2.5 ms |
| window check (the run adapter's) | about 30 ms | 4.0 ms |
| whole-cell read (a large reply, Virtuoso's work) | 25 ms | 30 ms |

The patch is uncommitted in the bridge checkout: a bridge update must keep it (worth offering upstream). Every later
tunnel start through the bridge uses it.

**Two short runs after the fix** (user: "yes, run both short runs"; `mscr_ota7t`, scratch settings, every job folder kept,
`$SP/anatomy.py`), against 7.157's one-lane run and 7.154's six-lane run (which still had the info outputs):

| step (median) | 1 lane before | 1 lane after | 6 lanes before | 6 lanes after |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Virtuoso preparation | 0.219 s | 0.168 s | 0.301 s | 0.201 s |
| OCEAN measurement and result files | 0.095 s | 0.083 s | 0.157 s | 0.085 s |
| remote shell and Spectre | 0.578 s | 0.762 s | 0.788 s | 0.671 s |
| seconds per evaluation (the loop) | 1.300 | 2.016 | 0.427 | 0.389 |

Both runs completed, every cell equal to its record afterwards. The steps that call Virtuoso got faster as expected, but
the whole runs are not comparable: WSL's 15-minute load average was 7.03 (1-minute 0.67 just after), another session's
work using about seven cores while they ran, and the steps on the WSL side grew (Spectre's step as the bridge's client
times it, 0.5 s gaps between one-lane evaluations). The simulator host was quiet (load 1.3).

**Repeated on a quiet PC** (user: "yes, repeat both runs now"; WSL's 1-minute load sampled every 2 s: one lane at most
0.70, six lanes median 1.41, at most 2.20 - the run's own work):

| | 1 lane before | 1 lane after | 6 lanes before (7.154) | 6 lanes after |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Virtuoso preparation, median | 0.219 s | 0.145 s | 0.301 s | 0.227 s |
| OCEAN measurement and result files, median | 0.095 s | 0.056 s | 0.157 s | 0.093 s |
| per evaluation, mean: the remote step (shell and Spectre) | 0.678 s | 0.742 s | 0.805 s (4 jobs kept) | 0.916 s |
| per evaluation, mean: everything else | 0.602 s | 0.572 s | 1.261 s | 1.567 s |
| seconds per evaluation (the loop) | 1.300 | 1.334 | 0.427 | 0.456 |

The calls through the tunnel got shorter in nearly every evaluation (a built netlist's preparation typically 0.20 ->
0.13 s, a native export's 0.52-0.71 -> 0.43-0.55 s, OCEAN about 0.10 -> 0.05 s): about 0.09 s less Virtuoso time per
evaluation at one lane, as means. The totals did not move measurably: Spectre's model reading on the host varied from
0.22 to 0.78 s per job in both runs and averaged 0.06 s longer in the second, and the transfers varied too; in runs of
20 and 40 evaluations that noise is larger than the saving. At six lanes everything besides Spectre took 1.57 s per
evaluation against 0.57 s at one lane - waiting for the preparation (0.38 s median) and work in the lanes - which is
where the next limit sits.

**The Spectre runner's SSH sessions do not wait** (user: "yes, check the Spectre runner's SSH sessions"; read-only). A
Spectre job opens six sessions over the bridge's shared SSH master (`ControlPath=/tmp/vb_ssh_...`, opened without a
terminal): the upload (`tar | ssh`), a login shell (`sh -l -s`) the command is written into, the results download
(`tar` over ssh) and three `scp` of log files. Ten of each through the bridge's master and through a temporary master
opened with a terminal (closed after): `true` 10.0 against 9.1 ms, `echo` of 20 bytes 8.4 against 8.6 ms, `scp` of a
1 kB file 16.5 against 17.2 ms. So the 40 ms hit only the tunnel's port forward (its extra connection to the service),
not command sessions. What SSH costs a job: about 10 ms per session, the login shell about 0.06 s and the Cadence setup
file about 0.06 s - together about 0.2 s. Merging the three log copies into the results download (about 0.03-0.05 s)
or keeping one shell per lane instead of a login shell per job (about 0.07 s) would be changes in the bridge's runner or
in the optimizer's adapter; not done.

### 7.165 Where a six-lane run's time goes besides Spectre (2026-10-03, same session as 7.115; user: "yes, break down the 6-lane overhead")

An investigation from runs already kept (7.161's quiet one-lane and six-lane `mscr_ota7t` runs, every job folder kept;
`$SP/overhead.py`); no simulation, nothing changed.

Per evaluation, means, from the design's proposal to its recorded result (tables, job manifests, the lanes' launch
journals, the bridge's step times):

| step | 1 lane | 6 lanes |
|---|---|---|
| proposal -> dispatch, hand-over, the lane's work before the job | 0.014 s | 0.018 s |
| waiting for the preparation (its queue) | 0.000 s | 0.443 s |
| the preparation | 0.233 s | 0.315 s |
| the bridge's Spectre job (upload, remote shell and Spectre, download) | 0.858 s | 1.320 s |
| OCEAN measurement and result files | 0.069 s | 0.171 s |
| job end -> completed -> recorded | 0.145 s | 0.220 s |
| proposal -> recorded | 1.318 s | 2.489 s |
| a lane idle between its evaluations | 0.026 s | 0.147 s |

A lane's cycle at six lanes is about 2.64 s, so 0.44 s per evaluation (the loop: 0.456 s). What grows:
- **The queue for the preparation** (+0.44 s). The one preparation process is about 70% busy at six lanes (2.27
  evaluations a second times 0.315 s). The native export of every 5th candidate takes 0.51 s (one lane) to 0.67 s (six
  lanes) against 0.13-0.20 s for a netlist built from the readback, and makes 44% of the preparation's work.
- **The Spectre job** (+0.46 s). Spectre's own part hardly grows (model reading median 0.36 -> 0.41 s, analyses done at
  0.42 -> 0.50 s); the time around it does: the remote shell and Spectre's start and exit (remote step minus analyses
  0.21 -> 0.37 s), and the transfers. Eight virtual cores serve six Spectre jobs, Virtuoso and the shells.
- **OCEAN, the hand-back and idle time** (+0.10, +0.08, +0.12 s): six lanes' OCEAN calls and the preparation share the
  bridge's service, which takes one call at a time; the optimizer's main loop did 23 learner updates (2.41 s of an 18 s
  loop) between hand-backs and dispatches.

Levers, by size, none built: a native export less often (every 10th instead of every 5th: about 16% less preparation
work, the queue perhaps 0.44 -> 0.2 s; a drift would then show after up to 9 candidates instead of 4; the constant
`NATIVE_EVERY` is in the optimizer package); trimming GATOR's own work in the preparation (about half of a built
netlist's 0.2 s; offline profile); fewer lanes or more cores on the simulator VM against the contention around Spectre
(needs runs); learner updates off the main loop's critical path (an engine change).

**The preparation's own work, profiled offline** (user: "yes, profile the preparation work offline";
`$SP/prep_profile.py`): every Python step of a built-netlist candidate's preparation (`_serve`, `set_parameters`,
`generate_netlist(built=...)`, and the lane's hash and graph), replayed without Virtuoso on 19 consecutive recorded
readbacks of `mscr_ota7t` (7.152's kept exports) with the session's recorded native export as template:

| step | median |
|---|---|
| the crash journal, written twice, where runs write it (`artifacts/local/bridge-state` on D:, 9p) | 16.2 ms (on the WSL disk 1.3 ms) |
| `validate_export` (the built netlist parsed and held to the cell) | 5.3 ms |
| `validate_readback` x3, `compare_written`, `verify_transition` | about 9 ms |
| everything else (render, files, hashes, deck, manifest, the hand-over) | under 3 ms |
| GATOR's own work in all | about 32 ms |

The recorded split of a built candidate's 0.134 s at one lane (write step 0.078 s, netlist step 0.006 s, the rest 0.050
s) reads with it: the write step's timer starts after the read before the write and the first journal entry and stops
before the second, so "the rest" holds that read (a Virtuoso call, about 0.03 s), both journal writes and the checks.
So of 0.134 s, about 0.09 s is the three Virtuoso calls (read, write, read; the read before the write stays, 7.148),
about 0.016 s the journal on D:, about 0.02 s checks and files. The one lever in GATOR's own work is the journal on D:
(about 0.015 s per candidate at one lane, 11%; through the preparation's queue perhaps 0.1 s per evaluation at six
lanes). Moving it means a session folder off `artifacts/local/bridge-state`, which also holds the session lock every
GATOR process takes; not done.

"commit everything and push" in the session of record 7.139.

### 7.167 The run adapter's session lock and crash journals moved off D: to the WSL disk (2026-10-03, same session as 7.115; user: "move the crash journal off D:")

After 7.165's offline profile: of GATOR's own work in a built-netlist candidate's preparation (about 32 ms), the crash
journal - written before and after each write to the cell - took 16.2 ms on D: (`artifacts/local/bridge-state`, a 9p
mount) against 1.3 ms on the WSL disk.

**What changed** (workbench code only; the optimizer package takes the folder from `BridgeConfig.state_root`):
- `paths.bridge_state_dir()`: `~/.local/state/gator/bridge-state` on the WSL disk, kept across restarts (`/tmp` is not);
  `GATOR_BRIDGE_STATE_DIR` moves it. It is the adapter's state root: the session lock `session.lock`, which every
  process that works on the Virtuoso cell takes, and a folder per session with its journals and restoration record.
  It does not follow the settings folder: runs from scratch settings and from the page share one Virtuoso session and
  must take the same lock.
- Its users: `tasks.generated_bridge_config` and `tasks._real_runtime` (the run's `BridgeConfig`), the corner check
  (`bridge_busy`, its `BridgeConfig`; the constant `BRIDGE_STATE` is gone), `bridge_setup` (the lock Save task's
  Maestro writes take; the constant `LOCK` is gone), and `profile_preflight`'s docstring.
- `tests/workbench/conftest.py`: an autouse fixture points `GATOR_BRIDGE_STATE_DIR` at each test's folder, so no test
  takes the real lock (before, a test that loaded a real task used the repository's folder unless it patched
  `repo_root`); `test_wb_profiles` checks that the check's folder lies there.

Sessions before this record stay in `artifacts/local/bridge-state` as evidence. A scratch copy of the repository that
shared the live lock through a link to `artifacts/local/bridge-state` must now set `GATOR_BRIDGE_STATE_DIR` to the new
folder. The switch was made with no process holding the old lock and no run active; the busy sessions were told.

**Checked:** a read-only connect through the real adapter (`$SP/readonly_after_fix.py`) took `session.lock` in the new
folder and opened its session folder there (connect 0.30 s, nothing written); a journal written twice there took
0.95 ms (16.2 ms on D:). Full suite: 1,632 passed, 1 skipped, 1 xfailed. Expected effect: about 0.015 s less per
candidate's preparation at one lane, more at six lanes through the preparation's queue (not measured on Spectre).

### 7.168 Folder settings in the connection window: five folders outside the repository, each defaulting to the value used before (2026-10-03, same session as 7.115; user: "I realized the project might be used by others. Add directory settings for necessary files in the connection module with current setting as default")

**In plain words.** The connection window has a new button, **Folders...**. It lists the folders GATOR keeps files in
outside the repository - three on this machine, two on the simulator host - each filled in with the value the code has
always used. Nothing changes until a folder is changed and **Save folders** is pressed.

**What was fixed in the code before** (survey of the workbench for machine-specific folders):
- `paths.bridge_state_dir()`: `~/.local/state/gator/bridge-state` (record 7.167);
- `layout.LOCAL_SCRATCH`: `/tmp/gator-work` (record 7.149);
- `bridge_connect.detect_setup` and `agents/diagnostics.skill_visibility`: the bridge checkout `~/virtuoso-bridge-lite`;
- `bridge_setup.IC_DIR`: `.gator/ic` under the simulator user's home;
- `bridge_setup.MAESTRO_NETLIST_ROOT`: `/tmp/gator_maestro_netlist` on the simulator host. On a host shared by several
  users, the first user's folder there blocks the others, and where Virtuoso's /tmp is not the one SSH sees, the
  download fails.

Left out on purpose:
- Spectre's job folders on the simulator host: the bridge's own `VB_REMOTE_SCRATCH_ROOT` in the bridge env file, `/tmp`
  when unset (the window says so);
- the agents' scratch: the system's temporary folder, or `/tmp/gator-llm-<run>` for the LLM sizing agent;
- the settings folder, which holds the connection file: `GATOR_WORKBENCH_SETTINGS_DIR`;
- the output folder: already chosen in the output panel;
- the bridge env file: per connection, in the connection file.

**What changed:**
- `directories.py` (new) defines the five folders: side, label, default, what each holds and when a change takes
  effect. It reads the connection file's `"directories"` block, checks values, and answers `describe()`.
  Precedence: the field's environment variable (only Adapter state has one, `GATOR_BRIDGE_STATE_DIR`), then the file,
  then the default. A value of the file that fails its check is not used; the window shows why.
- `bridge_connect.save_directories` sits next to `create_profile` and shares its lock. Writing rules:
  - every changed value is checked first, and nothing is written unless all pass;
  - the file gets only the values that differ from the defaults;
  - the connections and every other key are written back as they were, and names this backend does not know are kept;
  - the previous file is kept as `bridge_connection.json.before-<UTC>-folders`;
  - an unreadable file is never replaced, and a missing one is made with no connection in it.

  Refused with 409 `folder_busy`:
  - Adapter state while its session lock is held, in the old or the new folder;
  - a folder that an environment variable decides.

  Checks (422 `invalid_folder`, naming the folder):
  - a full path (`/` or `~/`), one line, no `..`, not `/` or the home folder itself;
  - on the simulator host, only letters, digits and `_ . + -`, because the value goes into SKILL and shell commands;
  - Adapter state and Simulator scratch are made when missing and written once;
  - the scratch must not be on a slow mount;
  - the bridge checkout must exist.
- Routes `GET` and `PUT /api/connection/directories` (`DirectoriesBody`: `{"values": {folder: path or null}}`, unknown
  fields refused).
- Where each folder is used:
  - `paths.bridge_state_dir()` delegates to the setting;
  - `layout.local_scratch()` takes its root from it (`LOCAL_SCRATCH` is gone);
  - `detect_setup` and the agent diagnostics take the bridge checkout from it;
  - in `bridge_setup`, `_remote_path` makes a folder absolute on the host: a `~/` folder through one
    `strcat(getShellEnvVar("HOME") ...)` call, as `_ic_path` did before. `_ic_write` makes every level of the folder.
    The ic filter `_is_ic_file` replaces the `/.gator/ic/` substring: a file directly in the configured folder or in
    the default one, so tests saved before a change are still recognized, and the next Save task with conditions
    moves them. `maestro_netlist` writes under the configured folder.
- Window: `ConnectionFolders.tsx` (new), opened by **Folders...** at the foot of `Connection.tsx`. The folders are
  grouped by machine and filled in with the values in use; a folder an environment variable decides is greyed out
  with the variable's name. Buttons: **Save folders** sends the changed folders only, **Undo changes**, and **Use
  defaults** (fills the defaults in, sends nothing). A refused folder is named and marked. A server started before this
  record answers 404, and the window then asks for a restart in words. The shared style sheet is untouched (the box
  reuses the "Add a connection" style). `api.ts`: `connectionDirectories`, `saveConnectionDirectories`.

**Checked:**
- `tests/workbench/test_wb_directories.py`, 21 tests, with a fake SKILL client for the two simulator-side folders:
  - the defaults are the values used before;
  - saved folders are used at all five places;
  - the connections and other keys stay as they were;
  - the 12 refused spellings write nothing;
  - the local folders' checks;
  - a missing file, an unreadable one, a bad hand edit;
  - Adapter state under a held lock and under the environment variable;
  - an old ic file in the default folder replaced by one in the new folder;
  - Maestro's netlist folder under the host's home.
- `test_wb_local_scratch` now compares with the default folder.
- Full suite: 1,653 passed, 1 skipped, 1 xfailed.
- tsc clean.
- `connection_panel_check.mjs`: 62 of 62 against the rebuilt page. 13 checks are new for Folders: one read when
  opened, the values in use, the environment's folder locked, one request with the changed folder alone, the refused
  folder marked, Undo and Use defaults sending nothing. The ngspice step's button list now ignores "Folders...".

Not done:
- no real Save task with a changed folder: the simulator-side paths were checked against a fake client only;
- the backend serving the page (port 8765, started 19:07) predates the code of 7.167 and of this record, and needs a
  restart for the Folders window to work.

### 7.171 The three 0-to-1 reward boxes without the browser's up/down arrows (2026-10-03, same session as 7.158; user, with a screenshot of Worst hard-spec share showing them: "remove the toggle for these 0 to 1 value boxes. What is the objective for?")

Worst hard-spec share, Objective bonus weight and Tightest-margin bonus weight (Run configuration, Reward and conditions)
are `Num bounded`: `type="number"` with min 0, max 1 and step "any", so Edge drew its spin arrows while the pointer was on
the box or it had focus. They are number boxes still - `reward_v2_frontend_check` asserts the declared 0..1 range, and the
backend checks the bounds - only without the arrows: Num gives a bounded box the class `bounded`, and `styles.css` draws
`input.bounded` as a plain text field (`appearance: textfield`, the WebKit spin buttons hidden). Nothing else changed:
no setting, default, schema or hash.

The question, answered in the conversation: the objective is the one Specs row whose limit reads "..., then lower is
better" (or higher). Among the designs that pass every hard spec, the run's best design is the one lowest (highest)
there; and only when the Objective bonus weight is above 0, the reward of a design that passes every hard spec also
grows the further that spec goes past its limit, measured in the row's Scale (Objective shape: clipped at one scale,
or compressed). "Objective: none" means no row has it, so the bonus weight does nothing.

**Checks.** `looks_check.mjs` (61): the three boxes keep type number and min 0 / max 1 and are drawn as text fields, and a
picture of the first one, focused with the pointer resting on it, shows no arrows. The arrows are a pseudo-element
`getComputedStyle` cannot read, so the picture is the proof: without the new rule the same picture shows them and the
check fails (control run). `module_fold` (29), `typing` (39) and `network_panel` (42) passed; `tsc --noEmit` clean.
