### 7.172 The editor columns that repeated another: two retired, four behind More columns, two hidden, three filled for you; six faults fixed on the way (2026-10-03, same session as 7.113; user: "check the current tabs are there any columns redundant?", then chose "Fix the 6 bugs", "Tidy without data change" and "Delete redundant columns")

**The review.** Read-only, from the data of the seven saved MScR tasks (164 Parameters rows, 55 probes, 51 specs) and a
trace of every column through the code, the key claims re-read in the code. Report with file:line evidence:
`artifacts/local/reviews/editor-columns-review-2026-10-03.md`. What the data showed:
- The Probe ID is "m_" + the Maestro output name in 55 of 55 probes. `result_source` is "ade_output" and `expression` empty
  in all 55.
- 45 of 51 specs are one probe, with its unit and its name minus "m_".
- `target`/`tolerance` are never used. `initial_value` is blank in every saved row (Save task empties it).
- The 101 follower rows have no bounds, and notes that only repeat their group.

**Retired (deleted, with a migration, identities kept).** Only these two: no saved task, run, test fixture or the parked
AnalogGym import used them.
- Specs `target` and `tolerance`, with the "target ± tolerance" choice. That choice compiled to exactly the interval
  [target - tolerance, target + tolerance], and a tolerance on any other spec only loosened its limit (it was not written
  to Maestro, so the copy and the optimizer disagreed). `PerformanceTarget._retired_fields` rewrites an older row to those
  limits, and an empty scale gets the size it had. A target without a positive tolerance never ran and is refused in plain
  words.
- Probes `expression` with `result_source` "expression" (a probe computed from other probes, the spec equation's job since
  7.52). `WorkbenchConfig._expression_probes` writes its arithmetic, in parentheses, into the equations of the specs that
  use it and drops the probe; a cycle is refused. `SpecPlan` keeps (ID, source name) pairs.
- Both hashes see the three at their defaults (`RETIRED_TARGET_FIELDS`, `RETIRED_MEASUREMENT_FIELDS`). Checked: the task
  and configuration hashes of the 6 runs the live backend lists (each equal to the hash its manifest recorded) and of the
  7 tasks are the same under HEAD's code and the new code.
- `store.ts` migrates the browser's copy the same way.

**Hidden or behind "More columns", the rows keep them.**
- `initial_value`, `ratio` and `active` stay in the data because the parked AnalogGym import writes non-default values.
- `result_source` and `value_type` stay because the user's rows hold non-default values (deleting them would have moved the
  7 tasks' identities).
- `schema.HIDDEN_COLUMNS`: `initial_value` and `result_source` are not shown.
- `ADVANCED_COLUMNS` gains the Parameters' `value_type`, `step`, `scale` and `ratio`.

**Filled for you (Editors.tsx).**
- A Maestro output's Probe ID is shown read-only and follows its output name; the spec equations that use it follow in the
  same Undo step.
- A spec whose equation becomes one probe takes that probe's unit while it has no limits.
- `signal` is labelled **Expression** (its schema text now says what it holds).
- A follower row's `optimized`, `value_type`, bounds, `step` and `scale` are greyed, with why.

**Six faults found by the review, fixed.**
1. "Read setup" set analysis "other" on 20 probes in 5 of the 7 tasks (getData `?result "stb"`/noise outputs, VS/IS
   DC sweeps), and the next Save task was refused. `virtuoso_setup.analysis_of` now reads `?result` names and VS/IS
   (matches 55 of 55 stored probes, was 35); WorkingCopy keeps a probe's analysis while its expression is the cell's.
2. "Read setup" replaced each spec's citation with "Maestro spec on ..." and reset Missing value = failed, Margin sides and
   Judged at, while saying the settings stayed. They are kept now.
3. "Add" on Probes made a "backend_profile" row in a Maestro task, which Save task refused. A new probe now takes the
   task's way of measuring.
4. The "Review run results" digest compared µm bounds with executed values in metres: every µm variable looked pinned at
   its lower bound. It now reads every value in its row's unit (`run_digest._unit_size`).
5. With any fixed row (optimized = false) every candidate was "incomplete": never valid, never feasible, no best design.
   Fixed groups are never sent to the simulator, but the engine expected a value for each member. `engine._candidate`
   now takes their circuit value from the compiled graph. The new test fails on the old code (checked in a scratch copy).
6. A notes-only edit dropped the recorded baseline at Save task. `task_files.what_changed` now counts a row whose notes
   alone changed as "notes". The task identity still includes notes, so a "Mode = resume" after such an edit is still
   refused (changing that would re-key every task and run). The header's Resume and Run status's Continue use the run's
   own configuration and are not affected.

### 7.174 Five frontend/backend audit findings fixed (2026-10-05; user: "fix them")

The audit reproduced five faults even though the existing suite passed. These changes preserve append-only storage,
asynchronous editing and the shared proposal/approval contract; no optimizer source or simulator behavior changes.

1. **Crash recovery.** `storage.atomic.JsonlAppender` trims only an incomplete final line before reopening for append,
   including a partial UTF-8 character or a tail longer than a read block. Healthy opens check one final byte. A complete
   malformed record raises `PersistenceError` instead of silently hiding later acknowledged records. WAL and flush-log
   regression cases cover crash, acknowledgement, another crash, flush/compaction and restart, with no lost or duplicate
   evaluations. Corrupt complete records are never automatically deleted.
2. **Late Save task replies.** `TaskSave` binds success/error handling to the selected task/cell/connection and its mounted
   component. `Left.taskSaved` receives the configuration sent by the press and accepts normalized parameters only while
   those rows are unchanged. Newer edits remain, with a message that they are not included in that save. A switched task
   keeps its own rows and circuit, even after switching away and back. Saving stays asynchronous.
3. **Old computed probes.** The backend converts legacy spec `measurement_id` references to equations before inlining
   and removing computed probes. An explicit equation still wins. The migration test covers both reference formats.
4. **Codex proposals.** `agents/codex_schema.py` derives a closed, fully required schema, with nullable optional fields.
   Flexible row values and Maestro dictionaries travel as JSON text, then decode to the existing shared proposal shape
   before row/scope/approval checks. Other provider and manual-import formats stay as before. Invalid encoded objects
   raise `schema_failure`. The previous expected-failure marker is removed; tests inspect the actual outgoing schema,
   exercise the provider output-file boundary, and reject malformed rows or attempts to widen scope. Contract reference:
   [OpenAI Structured Outputs](https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/structured-outputs).
5. **Undo/Redo.** `store.useConfig` uses one pure reducer for the current configuration and history. React StrictMode can
   replay it without consuming or duplicating history. Typing coalescing, blur boundaries, unrecorded updates and browser
   persistence stay in place; the history cap is 100 steps.

New `frontend/scripts/edit_state_check.mjs` and its in-memory fixture exercise the real components and store under
StrictMode. The scratch runner puts it on the Vite source preview. Existing typing, task save, task selection and request
lifecycle checks also pass. The fixture refuses every backend request; it cannot write a task or call a simulator.

**Validation.**
- Full offline suite: `OMP_NUM_THREADS=1 ~/analog-optimizer-venv/bin/python -m pytest -q -n 12 --dist loadgroup -ra`:
  **1,674 passed, 5 skipped, zero xfails** (143.56 s). Four skips need ngspice, which is absent in this environment;
  one parameterized schema-error case has an empty task set. The 24 warnings are the existing Torch and Starlette
  deprecations. The Codex strict-schema check now passes as an ordinary regression test.
- TypeScript `tsc --noEmit`: passed, including the new browser fixture. Vite production builds were written only to
  scratch check output folders.
- **19 distinct browser scripts, 666 assertions passed**, taking the final result for each script. Coverage includes
  the new 24-assertion StrictMode/save-race check, typing, task save/selection, request lifecycle, run control, export,
  editor columns, connections, ngspice window, network panel, equations, working copy, output/files, circuit source,
  themes, Save now and matched devices. Logs: `artifacts/local/fix-audit-20261005-{focused,browser,final}/`.
  A fresh-build rerun passed the theme assertions added concurrently by record 7.173; the earlier preview predated
  that stylesheet change. The new Codex round-trip fixture's missing working-copy field was corrected and its final
  isolated and full-suite runs both pass.
- `git diff --check`: clean. No optimizer source changes, real simulator jobs or paid model requests.

Docs updated in place: `workbench.md`, `functionality-reference.md` and the index. At the end of the first fix pass,
live services and the production `frontend/dist` had not been restarted or replaced, and nothing was committed or
pushed. No real Virtuoso/Spectre run or paid agent call.

**Deployment follow-up (2026-10-05; user: "go on", after the rebuild/restart/smoke-test/separate-commit plan).**

### 7.175 The editors' formula bar: a cell's whole value, typed in a box as wide as the table (2026-10-05, same session as 7.158; user: "add excel like single cell whole row text edit somewhere for long text editing which cant show everything in the cell. Also, is there any content that has to be in certain format e.g. unit?")

**Changed** (`Editors.tsx`, frontend only). A formula bar above each table: a click on a cell (or Tab into it) marks the
cell (`td.active-cell`) and shows its column and row ID (`notes · input_pair_width`) and its whole value in a box as wide
as the table, which grows to about five lines for a long text (`field-sizing: content`). The name's tooltip is the column's
format: type, choices, required, and its guidance.
- Typing in the bar changes nothing until Enter, Tab or leaving the box writes the text into the cell; Enter and Esc go
  back to the cell; Esc keeps the cell as it was. The text goes through the cell's own `update`, so what follows a cell
  follows the bar too (a renamed Maestro output's Probe ID and the equations that use it, a spec's unit from its one Probe).
- One Undo step per written text: the store merges only changes made by input events in one field (`typingField`), and the
  bar writes on a key press or a blur.
- A number is read as its number cell reads it (`Number`, empty = none where allowed); "abc" is refused in words under
  the bar and the cell keeps its value. A JSON list is parsed as JSON.
- Shown, not typed: a list (its choices in the tooltip), a tick box, the Specs limit list, Judged at, a Maestro output's
  Probe ID (follows its output name) and a follower's bounds (its leader decides them): read-only, the reason in the tooltip.
- The chosen cell is kept by row position checked against the row's ID: a Delete or Undo that moves rows finds it by ID;
  while the ID itself is typed, the position holds. Another tab starts with no cell chosen.
- A fault found by the new check and fixed before the end: Esc went back to the cell, the box's blur came first and wrote
  the text Esc had just dropped (the blur read the text from the render). The text is now also kept in a ref.

**The question about formats**, answered in the conversation from `schema.py`, `units.py`, `validation.py` and
`spec_equations.py`: IDs (`variable_id`, `measurement_id`, `metric_id`) start with a letter or `_` and hold letters,
digits, `_ . -`; a Probe ID inside a formula must be a plain identifier. Units come from a fixed list, spelled exactly
(`um`, `pF`, `kohm`, `mV`, `uA`, `MHz`, `deg`, `dB`, `count`, ...); a Parameters unit must match what the PDK declares,
a follower's its leader's, and a spec of one Probe a unit of the same kind as the Probe's. Number cells take plain numbers
or exponents in the row's unit, no SI letters; a spec equation takes Probe IDs, numbers (SI letters allowed there, 2u),
`+ - * / **` and brackets.

**Checks.** `formula_bar_check.mjs` (new, 19): nothing chosen at first; a clicked cell is marked and named with its whole
value; the bar is more than three times the cell's width; the column's format in the name's tooltip; typing waits for
Enter, which writes the whole text, goes back to the cell and is kept; one Undo and one Redo; Esc keeps the cell; typing in
the cell shows in the bar; another tab chooses nothing; a number cell refuses "abc" and takes 2.5e-3 as 0.0025; a list is
read-only with its choices. The page writes nothing of its own. Full browser suite: 40 scripts, 1,455 checks passed;
`pdk_check`'s width check failed once under load and passed 2 of 2 alone. `tsc --noEmit` clean.

### 7.178 pdk_check's two timing failures: a pop-up measured before its styles were in, a list read before it arrived (2026-10-05, same session as 7.158; user: "fix the pdk_check timing flake")

**The failures.** In full browser runs (and now and then alone) `pdk_check` failed one of two ways:
- "the list spans the narrow column" with `{"noSideScroll":true,"inside":true,"wide":false}`: the Components list in the
  RL networks window was narrower than 80 % of its panel;
- "it lists the loaded PDKs" with `[]`, then `TypeError: Cannot read properties of null (reading 'dispatchEvent')`: the
  PDK window's list was read empty, and the double click on its first row found none.

**Causes.** A pop-up of the page (`popupWindow.ts`) is an `about:blank` window into which the page's stylesheet links are
copied; they load after the window opens. `openNetworks` waited only for the panel to exist, and the width was measured
before the stylesheet applied (unstyled, the list keeps its default width). Measured with a log in the harness for one
run of five scripts: 3 of about 15 pop-ups (all RL networks windows) had 0 of 1 stylesheets loaded when found, all in
about 100 ms later. The PDK window starts a read of the PDK list when it opens, and the check read the rows at once.

**Changed** (checks only, nothing of the page):
- `lib/harness.mjs` `browser.attach`: a pop-up is handed over once every `link[rel="stylesheet"]` has its sheet (5 s at
  most; a window without such links goes on at once). Every check that opens one of the page's pop-up windows (20
  scripts) gets it, not only `pdk_check`.
- `pdk_check.mjs`: the PDK window's rows are read once the list has arrived (60 looks at most; still empty, the check
  fails as before).

**Checks.** `pdk_check` alone 8 of 8 (before: 2 of 3, then 2 of 2 and 3 of 3 on other attempts); the full browser suite
twice, 40 scripts, 1,456 checks passed, none flaky; the five pop-up scripts with the timing log: 253 checks passed.

### 7.180 The editor tables as a spreadsheet: tied rows folded under their leader, Up / Down / Enter between rows, a block pasted from Excel, columns sized by what they hold (2026-10-05, same session as 7.158; user: "go on", after 7.179 and the list of open advice)

**Changed** (`Editors.tsx`, `styles.css`; frontend only):
- Tied rows (advice 4): a Parameters row whose `binding_group` names a row of the table is folded under that leader; the
  rows keep their order, so a position in the table still names the same row. The leader's first cell, beside its tick
  box (no new column: checks find cells by position), counts its tied rows (`▸2`) and opens and folds them (per leader,
  for the page; another tab folds them again). An open tied row is marked `↳` with its ID indented. Never folded away: a
  tied row with an error or blocker report, or the one holding the formula bar's cell.
- Keys (advice 6): in a cell's box, Up and Down go to the same column of the row above or below, passing folded rows; Enter
  goes down and Shift+Enter up; the box's text is selected, as in a spreadsheet. A list keeps its own keys; the tick box
  column is left be.
- A pasted block (advice 6): tab-separated lines pasted into a cell fill the rows in view from it down and the columns in
  view from it right, as ONE Undo step (one configuration change). Each text is read as its column takes it - a number as
  the number cell reads it, a list's choice (any case), true/false/yes/no/1/0/x for a tick box, `>=` `<=` `interval` (and
  ≥ ≤) for a spec's limit, "Nominal only" / "All planned conditions" for Judged at, JSON for a list column - and a spec's
  suggested scale follows its new limits as when typed. What cannot be read is named and its cell left as it was; lines
  below the last row are named, not added; cells that follow another field or that a leader decides are kept. The line
  under the formula bar says it: "Pasted 6 cells into 3 rows (one Undo step).", or "Nothing pasted." with the reasons.
- Columns (advice: content-sized columns): ID columns 140 px, `binding_group` 120 px, `unit` 52 px; a tick box drops the
  84 px minimum of a text box; the Issues column is narrow while no row has a report (it kept 170 px always).

### 7.181 Readable small and large numbers in the editor tables; the rest of the advice weighed (2026-10-05, same session as 7.158; user: "go on", after 7.180 and the four items still open)

**Changed** (`Editors.tsx`, frontend only). A bound, limit or scale - Parameters `lower`, `upper`, `step`, `initial_value`,
Specs `lower`, `upper`, `normalization_scale` - of a row whose unit is an unprefixed base unit (m, F, ohm, H, V, A, W, J,
Hz, s) reads in engineering notation below 0.01 and from 10,000 up: 0.0004 A as `400µ`, 4.5e-7 m as `450n`, 1.5e6 as `1.5M`.
Between 0.01 and 10,000 (0.22 V, 53.3 dB), in a prefixed unit (um, pF, mV) and in every other column numbers read as
stored. Number cells, the formula bar and a pasted block read a number plain, with an exponent or with an SI letter
(f p n u µ m k M G T: 2.5u, 400µ, 10k); the formula bar shows the stored number.

Exact both ways (`engineering`, `readNumber`): the display moves the decimal point in the digits of `toExponential()` (the
shortest digits that read back as the same number) and never multiplies; a typed "450n" is read as `Number("450e-9")`, so a
cell that is only looked at keeps its stored value, and a typed one is stored as typed. Tried on 21 numbers and 10 texts,
each display reading back to the very number. On the live page the LDO's `iq_a <= 0.0004 A` reads `400µ`; its Parameters
(um) are unchanged.

**The rest of the advice, weighed** (nothing built): the red Abort is the user's own request ("make the abort button red",
record 7.33, faded while nothing runs); the pop-up windows each came from a user request (Connection 7.32, Runs 7.38, the
PDK window in the Virtuoso window 7.100, RL networks 7.150), so merging them waits for the user. The header status strip
waits for the user's answer to whether the Connection button they placed in the left column moves into the header; a
read-only copy of its state in the header would add a line, not take one away.

**Checks.** `sheet_check.mjs` (25; 5 new): a bound in metres reads in engineering notation; the formula bar shows the
stored number; "450n" typed is 4.5e-7 and reads `450n` once the box is left; the same bound in um reads as stored. The
editor checks (9 scripts) passed; full browser suite: 42 scripts, 1,492 checks passed, and `virtuoso_source_check` (the

### 7.183 The tested defaults: three soft actor-critic values in the GATOR baseline, the reward as one sentence, the Specs scale as the size of the limit (2026-10-05, session "RL reward design"; user: "check and test other default setting values, and give best default value", "can you test with spectre simulations not ngspice?", "record the reasons somewhere", "update the reward plan according to finding and build it")

**The measurement** (no code involved; `artifacts/local/reviews/defaults-study-2026-10-05/README.md` has every number,
the scripts and the run data; the reasons per value are in `docs/decisions.md`, "The tested defaults").
- 175 soft actor-critic runs through the workbench's own run path on the local ngspice (195,961 simulations, two
  circuits written for it, limits set so that about 1 % of random designs pass), and five Spectre runs on
  `real:mscr_ota7t` from scratch settings (user's approval: about an hour, at most 8,000 launches; 1,925 used, 69
  minutes; the cell equal to its record after every run, the live settings untouched).
- **With the schema's values - starting temperature 0.2, one update per evaluation - the search is uniform and no
  reward setting changes it.** Spectre, same seed, "capped, share 0" against "compressed, share 0.5": the same design in
  147 of 150 positions. 922 Spectre designs, none passing; ngspice: 1 % passing after 1,500 evaluations, the random level.
- Starting temperature 0.01 with 8 updates per evaluation learns: ngspice 97 % passing in the last third of 1,500
  evaluations (learned at about 470), Spectre 36 of 760 designs passing (the first at design 591). Discount 0 as well:
  learned at about 270 (ngspice only). Neither value alone, a larger batch, a higher learning rate or a longer warm-up
  does it; a faster temperature learning rate made two runs collapse.
- Reward settings, compared where it learns (one ngspice circuit, 4 to 6 seeds, two SAC settings): share 0.5 ends
  with the most passing designs; capped and compressed do not differ; the objective bonus lowers the power of passing
  designs by 27 to 30 %; the tightest-margin bonus lifts the final pass rate from 88-94 % to 99 %; "Missing value =
  failed" learns more slowly. The Specs scale was settled before on the 227 recorded designs (the size of the limit).

**Changed** (workbench, frontend, agent texts, docs; no file under `src/analog_optimizer/`, digest `f83342bc`).
- **`network_presets.py`.** `TESTED` = `initial_alpha` 0.01, `updates_per_transition` 8, `gamma` 0, with what was
  measured as each field's note. `gator_baseline()` is the schema's sizes with these three; `legacy_baseline()` is the
  schema defaults alone. A preset field no publication reports takes the tested value (new basis `gator_tested`); a
  published value stays. An encoder whose saved values equal the legacy baseline and were saved before `TESTED_SINCE`
  is read as "on the baseline" and follows it (`followed_baseline`); the file is not rewritten, and the same numbers
  saved later are the operator's own. `describe()` adds `tested` and `legacy_baseline`.
- **`validation.py`.** Two warnings and one blocker: `sac_values_random_search` (a run that trains - scratch or
  fine_tune, any method but LLM only - with fewer than 4 updates per evaluation or a starting temperature above 0.05),
  `budget_below_sac_learning` (a from-scratch SAC run below `SAC_LEARNS_AFTER` = 300 evaluations), and
  `scale_needed_for_zero_limit` (an active Specs row whose limits are all 0 with an empty scale: it was measured
  against 1 of its unit).
- **`schema.py`.** `ADVANCED_COLUMNS['targets']` gains `hard` and `normalization_scale`. No default changed: the
  schema's `AdvancedRL` and `RewardConfig` defaults stay what a file without those fields meant.
- **`store.ts`.** `SIMPLE_REWARD` - compressed, share 0.5, objective weight 1, objective shape compressed, margin
  weight 1 - is the reward of a new configuration.
- **`Right.tsx`.** Under Reward and conditions (on top of record 7.182's corner block): the sentence while the
  configuration holds `SIMPLE_REWARD`, else the values that differ and **Use the tested reward**; the seven controls in
  a closed **Advanced** fold; the "No active objective" warning gone. In RL networks: a warning naming the update count
  and the temperature with which the search stays random, also counted by Run configuration's "Review the settings in
  RL networks.", and the GATOR baseline button's tooltip.
- **`Editors.tsx`.** The scale suggestion is the size of the limit, 6 dB for a dB row; a row still holding the former
  suggestion (3 dB, 10 degrees, half an interval) counts as untouched and follows its next edit; the column is headed
  **Scale**.
- **Agent.** The readiness brief's "Reviewing settings" states the measured values and the budget a from-scratch SAC
  run needs; the presets data sent with a settings review carries `tested`.

**Not changed, and checked.** The seven saved tasks' task and configuration hashes are identical under the committed

applies them; Run configuration names the reward values that differ, and **Use the tested reward** sets them. A new

**Found on the way.**
- A six-lane Spectre run failed after 225 designs with "AuthError: nonce cache at capacity", and the restore failed
  the same way (the cell was intact: the failed call was the read before a write). The bridge daemon in Virtuoso
  remembers every request's one-time number for at least 15 minutes and holds 4,096 of them; a design costs about five
  requests, so about 820 designs per 15 minutes - under one per second - is the limit for the whole session. Request
  timeouts do not matter (the 15 minutes are a floor). The daemon running here knows `RB_NONCE_CACHE_MAX` and has it
  unset; setting it (32768) in Virtuoso's environment and restarting the daemon lifts the limit. Not applied with
  the build; applied afterwards on the user's word ("The bridge setting" below).
- One ngspice run of 175 ended with `TypeError: _deepcopy_atomic() missing 1 required positional argument: 'memo'`
  (thread lanes). Not looked into.
- `reward_v21_check.mjs` still typed the retired "target" constraint and tolerance column (record 7.172); those two
  steps were removed with this record's edits.

**The bridge setting** (user, 06:16: "Apply the bridge cache setting"). Applied at 06:25 without restarting Virtuoso.
The SSH tunnel was down and was started first (`virtuoso-bridge start`). Then `setShellEnvVar("RB_NONCE_CACHE_MAX"
"32768")` in the running Virtuoso, read back, and only the daemon restarted: `RBStop()` and `RBStart()` on Virtuoso
timers one and three seconds later, so the request that asked for them was answered first (daemon pid 4988, running
since 26 September, became 78399). The bridge files on the VM had the checksums of the local bridge checkout, and the
old daemon differed from them only in reading this variable. Proof by doing: 4,500 requests in 19 s, all answered - the
old daemon refused the 4,097th inside 15 minutes. No cell was read or written. **Not permanent:** a restarted Virtuoso
starts the daemon with 4,096 again unless the variable is set before the bridge is loaded. `/home/minlei/.cdsinit` on
the VM has no bridge line (the setup is loaded by hand in the CIW) and belongs to root, so it was left alone; until the
variable is in Virtuoso's start-up, type `setShellEnvVar("RB_NONCE_CACHE_MAX" "32768")` in the CIW before the
`load(".../virtuoso_setup.il")` line.

Docs: `docs/decisions.md` ("The tested defaults", the reasons), `docs/network-presets.md` ("The three tested values"),
`docs/workbench.md` ("The reward as one sentence, and the tested defaults"), `docs/functionality-reference.md`,
`docs/plan-reward-settings-simplification.md` (revision 5: built), the index row. Not committed.

### 7.184 The editor tables' last spreadsheet pieces: a find box, Ctrl+D to fill down, Ctrl+C and a Copy button to take a block out for Excel (2026-10-05, same session as 7.158; user: "go on", after 7.182 and its offer of advice item 4: "a find box to filter rows by ID or instance path, Ctrl+D to fill down, and Ctrl+C to copy a block out to Excel")

**Changed** (frontend only: `Editors.tsx`, `styles.css`).
- **Find rows.** A box in the editors' toolbar keeps in view only the rows whose text cells in view hold every word typed,
  not case-sensitive and folded or not (`matchesFind`, `inView`). Beside it: "1 of 5". Enter goes to the first row in view,
  Esc clears the box, and each table keeps its own text. Up / Down / Enter, a pasted block, Ctrl+D, Ctrl+C, Copy and Delete
  selected work on the rows in view: a ticked row out of view stays ticked and is not deleted, and a chosen cell whose row
  went out of view is let go (the formula bar says "No cell chosen").
- **Ctrl+D fills down** (`fillDown`). In a ticked row, with more rows ticked, the column takes the topmost ticked row's value
  in every other ticked row in view. In any other row the cell takes the value of the row above in view. One Undo step. A
  cell that follows another field or that its leader decides is kept and counted; an ID is not filled. The line under the
  formula bar names what was filled ("Filled 2 cells with notes = ... of input_pair_width (one Undo step).").
- **Ctrl+C copies out** (`copyOut`). In a ticked row it copies the ticked rows in view - every column in view, a header line
  first - as tab-separated lines; in any other row, the cell alone. With text chosen inside the cell the page leaves the copy
  to the browser. The **Copy for Excel** icon takes the ticked rows in view, or every row in view. Numbers go as stored
  (4.5e-7, which Excel reads; the engineering form 450n is only for reading), a limit as its sign. The copy goes through
  the browser's copy command with the text set in its copy event, so no clipboard permission is asked. A pasted block whose
  first line names the columns it lands on skips that line ("The header line was skipped."), so a block can go to Excel and
  come back.
- **One write path** (`withFollowers`). A typed cell, a pasted block and a fill now share the rules that were the typed
  cell's alone: a Maestro output's Probe ID follows its output name and the spec equations follow the ID; a spec whose
  equation becomes one Probe takes its unit while it has no limits; an untouched scale follows its limits. A pasted output
  name used to leave its Probe ID behind. New: a limit written without its objective (a pasted or filled `constraint`) keeps
  the objective only where the two still pair - as the limit list offers them - and otherwise has none, so a fill never makes
  several objectives or a "≤ upper, then higher is better".

**Checks.** New `find_fill_copy_check.mjs` (28): the find box (a folded tied row found by its instance path, "1 of 5", every
word in any case, Enter to the first row, Delete selected only in view and back with Undo, Esc and the folding back, the
chosen cell let go); Ctrl+D from the row above, from the topmost ticked row (folded rows left out), one Undo step, a cell its
leader decides kept, an ID refused; Ctrl+C of a cell and of ticked rows with the header, chosen text left to the browser,
the Copy button on the rows a find leaves, a copied block pasted back with its header skipped; a pasted output name
renaming its Probe ID and the equation using it; a pasted limit dropping an objective that does not pair and keeping one
that does. The page's copy command is answered inside the check, so nothing reaches the system clipboard. Control runs
with the old paste: without the header skip, "pasted back" fails; without the one write path, the Probe ID and objective
checks fail, and nothing else does. The editor checks (sheet, formula bar, spec equations, typing, task rows, testbench
probes, matched devices, looks, room: 9 scripts, 316 checks) passed with the new write path. `tsc --noEmit` clean. The
whole browser suite: on the working tree 44 scripts, 1,558 checks passed; on a clean copy of HEAD `4a106f5` (record 7.183)
plus this entry's files 44 scripts, 1,554 checks passed, none flaky.

### 7.187 Save task refused every cell with an ideal current source or resistor since the example circuits were deleted; the Scale column said in fewer words (2026-10-05, session "RL reward design"; user: "fill them with 6 and explain in shorter and clearer way for the scale column", then, asked whether to restart for the fix: "tell me why it doesnt recognizes them after deleting example circuiis? It should be recognizing all the loaded components")

**Found.** "Fill them with 6" is decision 5 of record 7.183's plan: 6 in the Scale of the 13 dB rows of `mscr_ota5t`,
`mscr_ldo1_rgcn`, `mscr_ldo2_rgcn` and `mscr_ldo_basic`. It went through the live backend's own Save task
(`POST /api/virtuoso/task/save` with the rows of `/api/tasks/starter`, the scale set; the four task folders copied
first, the connection tested). The first save, `mscr_ota5t`, was refused after 47 s: "Device type(s) isource are neither
verified in this project nor described by a loaded PDK". The task was untouched (its folder equal to the copy); the
working copy's Maestro test had been written again with the same content and the cell read again.

**Cause.** A cell's device types are known from two places: the technology library's own description, read by Load PDK
(GPDK045: 54 models), and, for everything outside the PDK, the "verified" list that `native_cells.verified_models()`
collects from `configs/gpdk045_*.json`. analogLib's ideal current source and resistor were named by one file only, the
LDO example's mapping `configs/gpdk045_ldo.json`. Record 7.128 (commit `e2584fa`, 2026-10-01) deleted the example
circuits and that file with them. From then on Save task - and a first save of any new cell - refused every cell holding
an `isource` or a `resistor`: six of the seven MScR tasks, all saved the day before. Runs were not affected, because a
saved task carries its own mapping. No test saw it: no fixture holds either part.

**Changed.**
- **`native_cells.py`.** `ANALOGLIB_VERIFIED` holds the two definitions word for word as the deleted file and every
  saved task have them; `verified_models()` starts from it. A mapping file that defines one of them differently still
  drops it, as between two files.
- **`Editors.tsx`.** The Scale column's explanation, 74 words -> 54: "How far outside its limit a value counts as a miss

### 7.188 A crash panel instead of a blank page; Ctrl+Z / Ctrl+Y in the editor tables; the "no real baseline yet" note in one line; the editor tables' headers in plain words (2026-10-05, same session as 7.158; user: "do all 1-4", after the answer to "any other optimization possible?" listed them)

**Changed** (frontend only: `App.tsx`, `main.tsx`, new `panels/CrashPanel.tsx`, `Editors.tsx`, `Left.tsx`, `store.ts`, `styles.css`).
- **Crash panel** (item 1). A failure while drawing the page left it blank, with nothing to press. It was seen while
  measuring, with a configuration missing its `run` section. Now `App` keeps only the configuration and its Undo history
  (`useConfig`) and draws the page as `Page` under a `CrashPanel`, a React error boundary. A failure anywhere below it, the
  page's own former body included, shows the error, **Undo the last change**, **Export JSON** and **Reload**. Undo draws
  the page again from the configuration before the change. Without an Undo - the saved configuration already fails when
  the page opens - **Start from an empty configuration...** downloads the saved one first, removes it from this browser
  and opens the page empty. A second panel in `main.tsx`, around everything, has no Undo to offer.
- **Ctrl+Z / Ctrl+Y** (item 2). In a cell they are the toolbar's Undo and Redo (Ctrl+Shift+Z too), as in a spreadsheet. A
  cell's typing is one Undo step, so the whole entry goes back at once. Before, Ctrl+Z only undid the typing inside that
  one box, and only the buttons reached the page's history.
- **The baseline note** (item 3). "This task has no real baseline yet: begin with Single baseline simulation (one real
  Spectre run, asks for your budget) before an optimization run. When it finishes, its result becomes the task's
  reference by itself." took five lines of the Project column. It now reads "No real baseline yet: run Single baseline
  simulation first.", the rest in its tooltip.
- **Headers in plain words** (item 4, the user's call). The tables' headers read ID, Instance, Parameter, Unit,
  Optimized, Lower, Upper, Tied to, Notes; Probe ID, Analysis, Output name, Expression; Spec ID, Equation, Limit, Hard,
  Soft weight, Active, Basis (`LABELS`). The columns that had a word of their own keep it (Expression, Judged at, Missing
  value = failed, Margin sides, Scale). Each field name stays in its header's tooltip ("binding_group: ..."), in the
  formula bar's format note and on the help line a header press opens ("Tied to (binding_group, string) - ..."). The More
  columns tooltip, the fill and copy notes and the paste note name the columns as the headers do. A copied block's header
  line reads in the new words, and the paste-back skips a header line in either words or field names.

configuration the way React's developer tools do: a stand-in for their hook, in place before the page loads, is handed
the page's root. Through it the check gives the configuration a `null` row, which the Parameters table cannot draw. The

### 7.189 A lighter page opening: the output folder no longer walked for its size at every page open; the page's weight measured (2026-10-05, same session as 7.158; user: "any other optimization possible? make it lighter overall")

**Measured first** (the live page, a real task's rows `real:mscr_ota7t`, a fresh headless browser):
- **Opening the page:** 128 ms of main-thread work (script 49 ms, style 15 ms, layout 31 ms), a 6 MB heap and 1,125
  elements, 627 of them inside closed folds. 14 requests.
- **The script:** 504 KB, React's 133 KB plus the workbench's own code, every panel and window in it. A correction to
  7.185's note: the circuit-graph library (cytoscape, 444 KB) was already a separate file, fetched only when a graph is
  drawn, not part of the 504 KB. Moving the rarely opened windows out (Connection, PDK, ngspice, Runs, Corner check, the
  graph view) would take about a quarter of the script and some 5 ms off the opening, at the cost of a pause the first
  time each opens and a module split per window. Not done.
- **The styles:** 48 KB. All but 18 of their 285 class names occur in the code, and those 18 are built at run time
  (`check-${state}`, `level-${level}`), so nothing to remove.
- **No compression:** the files and the schema go out uncompressed. On this machine compressing them would cost the
  backend more than the transfer saves, and could hold back the Agent tab's streamed answers. Not done.
- **The requests:** warm, the circuit load and a validation take 50 ms each and the run list 0.1 s; they do not slow each
  other. The 0.7 s the browser showed for them was the opening's burst of requests. One request is slow on its own:
  `/api/output/check` took 0.7 s warm and 2 s when the page opens. It walked the whole output folder on D: (9p, about 100
  times slower per file than the WSL disk) to add up its size, at every page open and every change of the root. The answer
  is one number, "used here", in a table folded away since record 7.179. The walk grows with every run kept.

**Changed.** The output check walks the folder only when asked (`PathBody.usage`, default off; `bytes_used` is null
otherwise). The table's "used here" is a **Measure** button that asks for it, for the root it was pressed for. The page
sends `usage` only from that button, so its normal look also works against a backend that predates this.
`frontend/src/api.ts`, `frontend/src/panels/Right.tsx`, `src/gator_workbench/api/models.py`,
`src/gator_workbench/api/settings_routes.py`.

A backend from before this change refuses the `usage` field (its request bodies forbid unknown fields). Measure then takes
the size from that backend's plain look, so the page works whichever backend is running.

**Checks.** `output_panel_check.mjs` (65; 4 new): opening the page does not ask for the size; the table offers Measure; a
press asks once, with `usage: true`, and shows "used here 3.00 GiB"; against an older backend (a 422 for `usage`) it shows
the plain look's size. `room_check.mjs` now runs its output-facts check (15), which it used to skip because the walked
answer came after the check had looked. `test_wb_api.py`: a look's `bytes_used` is null, and with `usage` it is the bytes of
the folder's files. Python suite 1,718 passed, 1 skipped. On a clean copy of HEAD `358cc85` plus this entry's files: `tsc
--noEmit` clean, the browser suite 45 scripts, 1,575 checks passed, none flaky.

### 7.190 Load PDK reads the ideal parts too: analogLib's own description, read with every PDK (2026-10-05, session "RL reward design"; user, told why Save task had stopped recognizing two parts in record 7.187: "It should be recognizing all the loaded components", then "build it, read analogLib with the PDK")

**In plain words.** A circuit's sources, ideal resistors, capacitors and inductors, probes and controlled sources belong
to no PDK: they come from Cadence's generic library analogLib. The workbench knew five of them from definitions written
by hand into example files. Now every read of a PDK also asks Virtuoso what analogLib says about its cells - which
Spectre component each netlists as, with which pins - and a cell's ideal parts are recognized from that.

**Read from the real library first** (one read-only query, 1.4 s): 180 cells, 142 with a Spectre entry, 50 components.
Two things in it shaped the design. One component has several pin orders: `vdc`, `vpulse`, `vsin` netlist a `vsource`
with two nodes, `vprbs` and the cell called `vsource` itself one with six. And the library names a current source's pins
`PLUS` and `MINUS`, while every saved task has them as `p` and `n`.

**Changed** (workbench, frontend, docs; no file under `src/analog_optimizer/`).
- **`pdk.py`.** `IDEAL_SKILL` is the PDK query's one line per cell and nothing else (no parameter, no form, no
  schematic opened); `read_ideal_parts` runs it in the reader process right after the PDK's own query and returns the
  text or the reason - it cannot fail the PDK. `build_ideal_parts` groups the cells by Spectre component and keeps every
  stated pin order with its cells (most cells first; a pin called `t` is not SKILL's truth value; more than 64 pins are
  counted, not kept). `IDEAL_KINDS` is the table of Spectre primitives the standard list has a kind for: `vsource`,
  `isource`, `resistor`, `capacitor`, `inductor` (its `l` is the inductance column `ind`) and `iprobe` (a 0 V source).
  `finish_load` stores the result as the record's `ideal_parts` and keeps an earlier read's when this one brought
  none; the record's version is still the network vocabulary's, which the ideal parts do not touch. `ideal_models`
  gives the parser's mappings, choosing per component the order that has as many pins as the netlist's lines have
  nodes. `describe` carries a summary, and the full list for one PDK.
- **`native_cells.py`.** `_mapping` (behind `build_mapping`): verified definitions first, then the PDK's, then the
  ideal parts'; each is used only when its pin count fits the netlist's lines, so lines of two lengths under one
  component leave it unrecognized instead of stopping the parser. A mapping that uses an ideal part says so in its
  version (`...+ideal-analogLib-<hash>`). The import's record lists `ideal_described_models`.
- **`bridge_read.py`.** A cell's PDK is read before its device mapping is built (it was read after), and the step's
  answer carries the ideal parts' summary.
- **`profiles.py`, `tasks.py`.** The refusal and the draft's note name both descriptions and what to do.
- **Page.** `Pdk.tsx`: the window says that each read brings the ideal parts; the list of loaded PDKs has an "Ideal
  parts" column; a shown PDK says how many were read, which have a kind of their own and which reach the network as
  "unknown", with a folded list (component, kind, every pin order, cells); a PDK without them says that they were not
  read. `Left.tsx`: the line after a cell is read adds "and 45 ideal parts of analogLib".

**Not changed, and checked on the real system** (scratch settings, read-only on Virtuoso).
- The reader as the backend starts it (op `pdk`): GPDK045 with 54 components and the same version as before
  (`fa5c3946`), analogLib with 50 components, 45 recognized, 6 with a kind of their own.
- All seven saved tasks: the mapping built from their own netlist with the new record equals the saved one, models and
  version. No saved task's description changes.
- A netlist with an ideal inductor, a `vcvs` and a `cccs` added: all three recognized (inductor with `l` as `ind`, the
  two controlled sources as "unknown" with 4 and 2 pins); without the record they are refused by name. A six-node
  `isource` alone takes the six-pin order; two-node and six-node lines together are left unrecognized.
- One cell read through the whole path into empty settings (`MScR/mscr_ldo1_rgcn` with its testbench, 5.4 s): the PDK
  and analogLib read in the same press, the PDK's transistors recognized at this first read, the mapping equal to the
  saved task's.

**Tests.** `test_wb_pdk.py` (19, four new: the record of an invented generic library, the choice by node count, a PDK
stored before, a cell with ideal parts parsed end to end; the reader's fake answers both queries, and a failed ideal
query leaves the PDK read with the earlier parts kept); `test_wb_native_cells.py` (one new: a part this very read
describes is recognized at once). New fixture `tests/workbench/fixtures/pdk_demo/ideal.txt`, invented like the demo PDK
- no vendor file is copied. `pdk_check.mjs` 35 checks (7 new); `virtuoso_source_check.mjs` pins the new line.

**For the user.** Live after a restart of 8765 and a page build. The stored description appears with the next read of
a PDK - any cell read, any Save task, or Load in the PDK window. Nothing changes for the seven saved tasks; a cell with
an ideal inductor or a controlled source can now become a task.

**Limits, said where they are met.** Only analogLib is read. Five of its components state no pin order (a mutual
inductor has no nodes of its own). The optimizer's standard list gives a kind to six; the rest are "unknown" to the
network until the package learns them.

Docs: `docs/workbench.md` ("The ideal parts, read with the PDK"), `docs/decisions.md`, the functionality reference's
three rows, the index row. Not committed.

**Commit.** `661aca8` (10-05 15:35), only this record's files and hunks, on the user's "Commit and push". That tree in a
clean copy: 1,723 passed, 1 skipped; tsc clean; the browser suite 45 scripts, 44 passed in the full run (1,578 checks)
and `agent_tab_check`, which this record does not touch, failed one timing check there and passed alone (97). Record
7.191 (another session, uncommitted) stays in the tree. Not live yet: the user's word is "at a safe moment", which is

**Live.** At 16:10, on the user's "Yes, at a safe moment". After the session of records 7.191 and 7.192 reported its

### 7.191 The GATOR baseline deleted from RL networks; the publication preset kept; both preset buttons gone (2026-10-05, session "Gator baseline deletion in RL networks"; user: "Delete the gator baseline in RL networks and keep the publication preset, delete two buttons")

**In plain words.** The RL networks window offered two ready-made settings per encoder, each on a button: the numbers
from publications, and the GATOR baseline (32 / 4 / 2 for every encoder with the three values measured in 7.183). Now
there is one: the publication preset, or your edits of it. Both buttons are gone; "In use: publication preset" still
says when the preset is what runs. To return to a published number after an edit, type it again - when all of an
encoder's numbers equal its preset, the backend forgets its saved copy.

**Changed** (workbench, frontend, docs; no file under `src/analog_optimizer/`).
- **`network_presets.py`.** `gator_baseline()` and `reset()` are gone, and so are the `gator_baseline` origin and the
  `followed_baseline` field of an entry. `_was_baseline` reads an encoder that was put on the baseline as its
  publication preset: the schema defaults saved before `TESTED_SINCE` (the user's own Graph Transformer entry of
  2026-10-03 is one) or the schema's sizes with `TESTED` saved before `BASELINE_DELETED` (end of 2026-10-05, since a
  backend not yet restarted could still store it). The file is not rewritten; an edit of such an encoder starts from
  the preset. `TESTED`, `legacy_baseline()` and the presets are unchanged; `TESTED_NOTE` and `SCALE_NOTE` no longer
  point at the baseline, and `describe()` no longer carries it.
- **API.** `POST /api/network-settings/{encoder}/reset` and its body `NetworkResetBody` are removed: the two buttons
  were its only callers. `PUT` and `GET` are unchanged.
- **`validation.py`.** `sac_values_random_search` ends "..., under Advanced in RL networks." instead of naming the
  baseline. `agents/settings_review.py` no longer hands the agent the baseline; `agents/run_readiness.md` no longer
  calls the tested values by its name.
- **Page.** `Right.tsx`: the button row and `reset()` are gone; the warnings about a starved budget and about values
  with which the search stays random say "under Advanced" for every Mode. `api.ts`: `resetNetworkSettings` is gone.

**Checked.** The user's real settings file read by the new code, read-only (its checksum unchanged): Graph Transformer

32, warm-up 8, replay 50, episode 30 - so the slow-SAC warning stays quiet; rgcn and gatv2 were already on theirs.

### 7.192 The starting preset: each encoder's published network size with every training number of the runs that learned (2026-10-05, session "Gator baseline deletion in RL networks"; user: "can you transfer all the learned settings to the the current preset", then "What I meant is merge the publication preset and overwrite those settings with the previous learned settings, keep this as the starting preset with no multiple choices between gator baseline and publication preset", then, asked which numbers, "All training numbers")

**In plain words.** After 7.191 each network type had one preset, with the papers' training numbers. Now each starts
from one **starting preset**: the network size from the papers (Graph Transformer 48 / 4 / 4) and every training number
from your 2026-10-05 runs that learned - starting temperature 0.01, 8 updates per evaluation, discount 0, batch 8,
learning rate 3e-4, warm-up 8, replay 10,000, episode length 4. There is still no choice to make and no button. The
window says "In use: starting preset".

**Asked first, because "learned" had two readings.** The study (7.183) compared and chose only temperature, updates
and discount; batch, learning rate, warm-up, replay and episode length were simply what every run was made with (the
"RL reward design" session confirmed from `tables/sac_table_ota5.txt` and `scripts/run_arm.py`: learning rate 1e-3 was
better in both runs that tried it, never with 8 updates; episode length was never varied). Keeping the papers' replay
50 and episode 30 next to 8 updates would have been a mix that never ran. The user chose all training numbers (in the
other session, about replay and episode: "take 10000 and 4 as well"); the network sizes stay published.

**Changed** (workbench, frontend, docs; no file under `src/analog_optimizer/`).
- **`network_presets.py`.** `TESTED` is again the three compared values; `LEARNED` adds the five the measurements were
  made with. `_preset` replaces every published training number by `LEARNED`'s: the field gets basis `gator_tested`
  (the three) or the new `gator_measured_with` (the five, each note beginning "The value the measurements of 2026-10-05

### 7.193 The Training tab: live graphs of the recorded training numbers (2026-10-05, session "Training tab with live graphs"; user: "Add a Training tab beside Output in the workbench's existing bottom panel, using uPlot for live training graphs. Keep Output available for logs and errors.")

**Asked.** A Training tab in the bottom panel with live uPlot charts, Output kept for logs and errors; built on the
metrics the runs already record (structured, not console text), with run identity, order and the right x axis; the
initial charts episode return / reward, evaluation success rate, actor and critic losses, entropy and temperature,
circuit specs against their targets, best feasible objective - each only where existing records support it, the rest
reported as unavailable; one or two charts, hover values, legend toggles, zoom, reset, optional smoothing over the raw
samples, training apart from evaluation; incremental updates through the existing connection, redraws at most once a
second, nothing while hidden, bounded memory with extrema-preserving downsampling, uPlot bundled locally; history then
live, no duplicates, runs apart, resumes and counter resets explicit, gaps not zeros, zoom kept with a Follow latest
control, everything disposed; validated on recorded logs or fixtures without expensive training.

**What the runs record (inspection).** The page talks to the backend over JSON polled by HTTP (status every 1.5 s while a
run is live, Logs every 2 s); runs have no WebSocket or event stream. Every training number is already structured, in
the run's Parquet tables (storage/tables.py), published every `flush_every_evaluations` (16) bundles and journaled
before that in `data/_journal/wal.jsonl`: `evaluations` (reward per evaluation, phase, validity, hard-spec verdict,
normalized violation, clearance margin, best-so-far design, timing, commit order), `learner_updates` (actor, critic and
temperature losses, alpha, mean log-probability, TD target, gradient norms, cumulative update count; written unless
"save learner updates" is off, never for frozen or AI-only runs) and `metrics` (each spec's value per evaluation with its
limits). Not recorded anywhere: an episode return; an evaluation of the policy during training (only a frozen run
evaluates a policy, phase `frozen_eval`; phase `validation` exists in the enum and is never written); an objective,
unless a Specs row says "then lower/higher is better". Nothing was added to the training loop.

**Built.**
- Backend `training_series.py` + `GET /api/runs/training?run_dir=&flushes=` (run_routes.py): the rows of flush records
  from the page's cursor on (pages of 30,000 rows), as columns under their recorded names, timestamps as epoch seconds,
  words coded once per answer; then, once the page holds every flush, the journal's bundles after the newest flush (the
  tail: read backwards from the end, torn last line skipped; a journal rewritten between the two reads gives an empty
  tail that once, never a hole). Parts read eight at a time with one open each: an 800-evaluation run's 192 parts on the
  9p drive 5.9 s one by one, 0.24 s eight at a time (measured). The objective from the run's own effective_config
  (compile.build_specs, the tracker's rule); why learner updates are missing, in words. Read-only, nothing simulated.
- Frontend: `TrainingTab.tsx` (the tab, its reading and drawing schedule), `TrainingChart.tsx` (one uPlot chart),
  `trainingData.ts` (no React, no imports: the store, chart series, smoothing, downsampling, thinning, counter resets);
  `App.tsx` (the tab after Output files, mounted like the Agent tab, hidden = another tab or the panel folded), `api.ts`
  (one call), `Icons.tsx` (follow, zoom-reset), `styles.css` (the tab's rules at the end). `uplot` 1.6.32 (MIT) pinned
  exactly in package.json, loaded only when a chart is first drawn (a 52 kB chunk of its own; its 1.6 kB stylesheet in
  the page's).
- Charts and their sources: workbench.md "The Training tab". The evaluation axis is the evaluation's number in recorded
  order (= completion_index except where a crash-resume repeated one); the learner axis is cumulative_updates. Episode
  return is listed as not recorded and the step reward drawn as what it is; "Evaluation success rate" exists for frozen
  runs only; the best feasible objective only with an objective row.
- `corner_check_check.mjs` pins the new tab order (Output files, Training, Corner check), reported by the 7.198 session.
- A baseline run that calibrates (record 7.197, in progress in another session) records its random designs as phase
  `warmup`; keyed on the status's `calibration` (that session's advice), they read "Calibration (random designs)" and the
  learner charts say nothing learns. Without that field (older backends, every other run) nothing changes.
- CLAUDE.md's data-only rule names the second exception (this tab), package.json's description too.

**Verified.**
- `tests/workbench/test_wb_training_series.py` (8): every delivered value equals the run's tables (a MOCK run with a NaN
  measurement and a timeout: gaps with their reasons); pages add up to one answer; the journal tail (torn line, lines
  longer than a read block, a rewritten journal); a worker killed with SIGKILL and resumed - the tail before, the parts
  after, every evaluation once, segments start + resume, learner rows as recorded; a frozen run (evaluation phase, no
  learner, the reason); the best feasible objective equals the tracker's value; the route's 404/403/422.
- Full regression: on HEAD c7b00c5 + only these changes in a clean copy, 1,712 passed, 21 skipped (evidence files not in
  git), 0 failed. In the shared working tree (with other sessions' unfinished work) 1,744 passed, 2 failed - a pause
  timing test and the 13-run MOCK workflow test - both passed when run again alone.
- `training_data_check.mjs` (50, node, no browser): values = recorded rows, gaps, categories, tail replaced and never
  counted twice, late answers ignored, smoothing, downsampling keeps spikes, gaps, edges and each pixel column's extremes
  (100,000 points to 400 columns), counter resets, the memory bound (spikes and gaps kept, counts recorded).
- `training_tab_check.mjs` (48, hermetic): the tab order; no read or redraw while hidden or folded; history in pages,
  then every 2 s while live; at most one redraw per chart per second and none without news; the head (run, state, counts,
  last metric); hover = recorded value; legend toggle; drag-zoom holds while results arrive, Follow latest, Reset zoom;
  Logs and Output files still work; resizing; completion (one last read, then none); a failed, an empty and a frozen
  run; a backend without the route ("restart it"); a calibrating baseline run (7.197); choices kept after a reload; window listeners 6 -> 6 after six chart changes; 20,000 evaluations + 127,952 updates
  in 6 pages in under a second, hover in 2 ms; caps lowered for the memory bound; both looks; no script error.
- The whole browser suite on the working tree, three times: 47 scripts / 1,702 checks all passed; then 48 scripts (a
  peer's new one) with one failure each time in a check of the Agent tab's flows (agent_tab_check once, llm_sizing_check
  once - each passed in the other runs and alone again); the Training checks again after the last touches: 98 passed.
- Recorded Spectre runs (defaults study of 2026-10-05, `artifacts/local/reviews/defaults-study-2026-10-05/spectre/
  runs-data.tar.gz`, extracted to a scratch folder, read by a scratch backend): the learning run's 800 evaluations and
  5,904 updates in 4 pages, every chart value equal to its Parquet value (reward, violation, best design, pass flags,
  4 specs x 800, actor/critic/alpha/entropy x 5,904); 36 passing designs, as the study counted; hovered values equal; the
  failed run shows failed, its error and its 226 evaluations.
- A MOCK run started on that scratch backend (240 evaluations, 2 lanes, free) and watched: the count grew 0 -> 240 in 22
  steps, between parts too (the journal tail), never back; its learner-update count equalled the run's (185).

**Limits.** The charts are on screen only, with no image export (the numbers are in Data tables and Output files). A
spec of a run that trains at corners is its worst value over them, as recorded. The journal's tail can be missing for
one read during a save point (said in a line). The memory bound thins the oldest half past 50,000 evaluations or 200,000
updates: no mean over the thinned stretch. Whether the killed test run repeats update counts depends on when the kill
lands (the test accepts both); the drawing of a learner restart is checked on fixtures (training_data_check).

### 7.196 Load PDK sees through a diode wrapped with its series resistance (2026-10-05, session "Analoglib PDK compatibility"; user, after a second foundry PDK on the VM was loaded to check that analogLib's ideal parts come with any PDK: "teach the reader to see through the diode wrapper", then "delete the [...] pdk read so it wont be push to the github")

**Found.** The second PDK places each of its nine diodes inside an inline subcircuit with the diode's series
resistance: `r1 (1 3) resistor` and `diode (3 2) <diode model>`. The inner element is not named after the subcircuit, so
the inline rule does not apply, and `classify_model` saw "diode, resistor between its first two pins - not classified":
family `other`, both pins `unknown`. The network got them as components of their own but not as diodes.

**Changed** (`pdk.classify_model`, nothing else): after the MOS / bipolar core search, a subcircuit is a diode when every
path between its first two pins crosses one diode and nothing but resistors besides it, and exactly one diode element
can lie on such a path (a diode to a further pin, the body, never does). That element becomes the core, so the existing
pin rule gives the roles: a pin on the diode is its terminal, a pin one series element away reaches it through that
element. A resistor with a diode in parallel, and two diodes in series, stay `other`. The basis reads "element d uses
model ... = diode (...), with only resistance in series between its first two pins".

**Checked.**
- The second PDK's read (CDF text + model-file scans + analogLib), built once with the old rule and once with the new:
  the nine diodes become `diode` with PLUS = anode, MINUS = cathode; nothing else changes.
- GPDK045's read through the same two builds gives sha `fa5c3946...` both times, so the stored record and every run
  that names `pdk:gpdk045` are as they were.
- New test `test_a_diode_wrapped_with_its_series_resistance_is_a_diode` (invented definitions: the wrapper, the
  cathode-first order, a bare diode, a body diode, the parallel and two-diode cases). It fails on HEAD's `pdk.py`
  (`'other' != 'diode'`) and passes now. `test_wb_pdk.py` + `test_wb_round5_perf.py` 33 passed.
- Whole suite on a clean copy of the committed tree: 1,703 passed, 21 skipped, 1 failed. The failure,
  `test_a_candidate_a_pause_stops_before_its_launch_is_cancelled_not_a_failure_of_the_link` (round5_followup), is a
  timing test that never touches the PDK code; run alone on the same copy it passed 3 times out of 3.
- docs/workbench.md "How each fact is decided" names the rule.

**Removed, on the user's request.** The second PDK's record (`configs/local/workbench/pdks/<id>/`, git-ignored, so it
was never at risk of a push) and every scratch copy of its model file, CDF read and scans are deleted. Nothing tracked
names it. GPDK045 stays the only loaded PDK.

The backend runs the working tree's code once 8765 is restarted; until then a Load PDK uses the old rule.

Committed and pushed as `7f52cfe` (5 files, only this record's hunks, built in a private index; the 7.193-7.195 and
7.197 work of other sessions stayed in the working tree).

### 7.197 Calibration with the baseline run: a Scale per Specs row and the Initial alpha, proposed from 64 random designs (2026-10-05, session "RL reward design"; user: "shouldnt each task run the baseline run? can you add the calibration functionality to that")

**In plain words.** Beside **Single baseline simulation** there is now a tick, **+ calibration**, on by default. With it
the run goes on after the baseline with 64 random designs and proposes two settings from what they measure: the Scale of
each Specs row (the typical miss of the designs that miss it) and the Initial alpha (the starting temperature). The run
changes nothing; Run status shows the proposals, and **Apply calibration** writes them into the page as one Undo step.

**How it came about.** The user asked whether settings could be tuned in a first phase of random designs, for "all the
settings in RL network and reward settings", and then placed it: every task runs the baseline run anyway. A free study
(66 ngspice runs, below) said which settings such a phase can set: the two that depend on the task. The others either
made no difference (network size, encoder) or are better as one tested value for every task (learning rate).

**Changed** (workbench, frontend, docs; no file under `src/analog_optimizer/`, digest `f83342bc` as before).
- **`calibration.py`** (new). `prepare` turns the baseline run's configuration into "the starting design on every lane,
  then N random designs": budget lanes + N, the warm-up covers every step, one episode, no learner update due, a
  passing design does not stop the run, and the limit of consecutive failures is raised to the budget. `calibrate` is
  the arithmetic over the run's own rows: per active Specs row the designs that measured it, those that miss it and
  their median miss in the row's unit; a Scale is proposed when at least 8 designs (and one in eight) miss the row, the
  row does not carry the objective and the reward's shape is compressed, kept within 0.01 to 10 times the size of the
  limit and rounded to two digits. The Initial alpha is 0.1 x the standard deviation of the rewards worked out again
  with the proposed scales by the run's own reward code (`SpecificationEvaluator`), within 0.0001 to 0.05 - and only
  if that code first gives every recorded reward again with the scales the run had (`reward_check`). `from_run` reads
  a run folder; the answer is `calibration.json` there.
- **`engine.py`.** A run whose manifest holds `calibration` dispatches nothing but the baseline until that evaluation
  is committed (`_baseline_checked`); an invalid baseline ends the run there (`baseline_invalid`, the run still
  "completed", so the task's column says why it has no baseline, as for a run of one evaluation). `_calibrate`, after
  the baseline's adoption, writes the file and one line into the manifest's notes; a failure in it is logged and never
  fails the run. Every other run takes none of these branches.
- **`api/run_routes.py`, `api/models.py`.** `POST /api/runs/start` takes `calibration_designs` (0 to 500) with
  `baseline_only` (400 with an optimization run); the manifest records the request. `GET /api/runs/status` carries
  `calibration: {request, result}` for such a run, null for any other.
- **`profiles.py`.** A calibration run stopped by the user among the random designs can still give the task its
  reference (state `stopped` is accepted for it; `record_reference` checks the baseline evaluation as before).
- **Page.** `App.tsx`: the tick (kept in the browser, `gator-workbench-baseline-calibration-v1`), the budget in the
  question before a real run ("the starting design once on each of 6 lanes, then 64 random designs for the
  calibration"), the button's tooltip. `RunTabs.tsx`: the Calibration block above the run's facts - the table, the
  Initial alpha, Apply. Apply writes a Scale only into a row whose limits and unit are those of the run, says "not
  applied" for a row that was edited since, offers nothing for another task's run, and names Save task for a saved task.
- **Agent brief** (`run_readiness.md`, `readiness.py`): the baseline step mentions the tick.

**Measured** (free ngspice circuit `ota5`, 1,000 evaluations per run, 3 seeds per setting; no training run on Spectre).
- **The scales the workbench proposes** (gain 10 dB, bandwidth 70 MHz, output voltage 0.04 V; phase margin and the
  objective row as they are), with the page's tested reward: 99.1 % of the designs pass in the last third of the run
  against 97.0 % with the scales of an empty box; learned at design 214 / 240 / 301 against 215 / 262 / 263.
- **The price:** the passing designs' median power is 80 uW against 75 uW - about 7 % more. A miss costs more with the
  smaller scales, so the search keeps more distance from the limits. Without bonuses the same scales gave 98 % against
  93 % (two rounds of three seeds).
- **The objective row:** calibrated too, 99.3 % and 81 uW - no measurable difference. It is left alone as a judgment
  (its Scale also defines the objective bonus), not because of a measured harm.
- **The Initial alpha:** its proposal with those scales, 0.014, against 0.01: 98.6 % against 99.1 %, no difference.
  Where the reward is flat it decides: scales ten times too large, 64 % with 0.01 and 91 % with the calibrated 0.0025;
  a hundred times, 1 % and 20 %.
- **Settings that need no calibration:** network 16 / 2 / 1, 32 / 4 / 2 and 48 / 4 / 4 and the three encoders end at 93
  to 95 %; episode length 1 at 89 %, 8 at 93 %; replay 50 with episode 30 at 88 %. Learning rate 1e-3 ends at 98 % and
  learns about 100 designs earlier - one value for every task, see "Passed on" below.

**Checked on a real simulator** (ngspice, the working tree's code): a baseline run with 64 random designs on 4 lanes
took 4 to 14 s: 1 baseline, 3 lane resets, 64 random designs, no learner update, every recorded reward given again by
the reward code (64 of 64). Its proposals over six seeds: gain 7.7 to 11 dB, bandwidth 64 to 73 MHz, output voltage
0.026 to 0.043 V - the 4,153-design values are 10 dB, 70 MHz, 0.04 V - phase margin none (no design misses it), power
none (the objective row), Initial alpha 0.011 to 0.014. With 32 designs the output-voltage row had too few misses in 3
of 5 seeds, with 16 in all 5: hence 64.

**Checked on Spectre, once** (18:03, on the user's "Yes, one run on mscr_ota7t"; through the live backend as the page
asks for it, one lane, the user's output root). The first start was refused before anything was created: the gate's
full check has every layer ok and the Spectre licence layer unknown (`license_not_checkable`), the question the page
puts before every real run here; the second start acknowledged that one layer. The run: 65 Spectre simulations in 114 s
(65 confirmed launches, none uncertain) - the baseline, then 64 random designs, of which 63 gave a reward and none

|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `gain_vv` | >= 400 V/V | 55 of 63 | 260 V/V | 400 | 260 |
| `ugbw_hz` | >= 25 MHz | 47 of 63 | 11.4 MHz | 2.5e7 | 1.1e7 |
| `pm_deg` | >= 60 deg | 23 of 63 | 9.6 deg | 60 | 9.6 |
| `ibias_a` | <= 1 mA | 22 of 63 | 0.28 mA | 1e-3 | 2.8e-4 |

### 7.198 Save task shows its steps while it works and allows one save at a time, also after the page is loaded again (2026-10-05, same session as 7.113; user: "show saving (is it possible to show progress?) and if the backend is saving dont allow another save task press even after refreshing page")

**Why.** Record 7.194: a save of a Maestro task takes about half a minute, almost all of it in Virtuoso, and the page
showed only a greyed icon (its "Saving..." is screen-reader text since record 7.82). The user loaded the page again
during two saves; the backend finished both, and nothing kept a second press from going out.

**Backend.**
- `task_saving.py` (new) keeps, in memory, the save running in this process and the last one that ended.
  `running(task_key)` holds the one place for a save (`Busy` when it is taken) and records how the save ended: its
  answer, or the problem that stopped it. `status()` gives the running save - its task, its steps with their titles,
  the step it is in and its number, the seconds so far, the seconds the last successful save of that task took - and
  the last one.
- `profiles.save_task(..., progress=)` names each step as it starts, together with every step it takes: `setup` and
  `deck` (Maestro tasks), `read`, `files`; `copy` first for the API's Save as.
- `POST /api/virtuoso/task/save` takes the place for its save. Another save meanwhile is refused (409 `save_running`,
  the running save in the answer). `GET /api/tasks/saving` (new) answers from memory, nothing is read or contacted.
  Delete task is refused for the task being saved.

**Page (`TaskSave.tsx`).**
- It asks `/api/tasks/saving` when Save task appears (also after the page is loaded again), then every second while a
  save runs, and not otherwise.
- While any save runs: a short bar and one line under the toolbar ("Saving real:<name> - step 2 of 4: Virtuoso
  netlists the Maestro test (12 s; its last save took 34 s)"), Save task closed, its tooltip naming the save it waits
  for.
- A save the page did not send (from before the reload, or from another tab) says how it ended - the revision and the
  baseline, or why it failed - and the task list and the open task are read again. The rows stay as they are: the
  check says if they differ. A read that fails does not end the following; when the backend was started again during
  the save, the page says the save ended without an answer.
- A press refused because another save runs shows the refusal and follows that save.

The user's rule that nothing happens on its own: the page asks once when Save task appears and asks again only while a
save runs - both what this request is about.

**Checked.**
- `test_wb_task_saving.py` (new, 4 tests): the steps of a ring-oscillator save, the API's Save as and a Maestro save;
  through the routes with a held stand-in save: the status while it runs, a second save refused with its code and the
  running save, Delete task refused, the answer and `last` afterwards, the usual seconds on the next save; a failed
  save records its problem and frees the place. With the save-related test files: 96 passed. tsc clean.
- `task_save_check.mjs`: 28 -> 46 checks (fixtures for the save state): the line at once and the counted step, Save
  task closed, asked again while the save runs and no more after; a page loaded during a save shows it, keeps Save task
  closed (a press sends nothing), follows the steps, says how it ended, reads the task list again (the new revision
  shows); a save refused because another runs, followed to a failed end; failing reads keep the following, a backend
  started again ends it with a warning. Three rounds with `edit_state_check`, `typing_check` and `working_copy_check`
  (140 checks each round), all passed. The whole browser suite on the working tree: 1,655 checks passed;
  `corner_check_check` failed one check because of the Training tab of record 7.193 (in progress; its session has
  fixed the check since). The whole Python suite on the working tree: 1,736 passed, 1 skipped.

### 7.201 Training tab audit fixes: feasible objectives and bounded live data (2026-10-05; user: "fix them")

**Problem.** The best-design tracker can retain an infeasible design, but the Training endpoint extracted its objective
as though it were feasible. Also, the endpoint paged published flushes but resent the entire unflushed journal tail;
the browser's row caps applied only to published rows. An unflushed tail of 200,001 learner updates exceeded the cap.

**Changed.** Parquet and journal conversion emit a best feasible objective only when `best_so_far_feasible` is true.
The browser also masks false/unknown feasibility for older backends, in both single-run and comparison charts, and
does not offer that chart before a feasible value exists. Live journal rows now use the page's remaining 30,000-row
budget and an opaque byte/row cursor (`tail_cursor`, answer `tail_page`), including splits inside a bundle. Subsequent
polls seek directly to new data; after a new flush, a backwards boundary search skips already published history.
Flushes and atomic journal replacements reset the provisional rows. Published flushes still travel whole.

Both browser polling loops fetch remaining tail pages immediately. The store rejects repeated/stale pages, joins
spec-only pages to earlier evaluation rows, and thins the combined published and provisional tables to the existing
50,000-evaluation / 200,000-update caps. Full source counts preserve numbering and totals through thinning and flush
replacement. Oversized typed-array buffers and unused word entries are compacted. Existing run-comparison edits
(7.200) are preserved; no optimizer or training-loop change.

**Verification.** Backend: 12 tests passed, covering false/unknown/true feasibility, bounded pages within a bundle, exact row
reconstruction, quiet polls, append, torn lines, compaction, stale cursors and seeking past published history.
Frontend arithmetic: 74 checks, including the actual 200,001-update unflushed reproduction and flush replacement after
thinning. TypeScript clean. Browser: 62 checks passed using scratch backends and an isolated build; both polling loops
carry the journal cursor, quiet polls do not redraw, and the 20,000-evaluation / 127,952-update fixture loaded in 0.9 s.
Browser evidence: `artifacts/local/training-tab-fixes-20261005/`. Live frontend build and backend were not replaced.
