### 7.203 Save task asks before rows older than the saved task replace it; the check points to Reload saved values (2026-10-05, same session as 7.113; user, told what this would do: "yes build it")

**Why.** Record 7.194: a page that held the rows of revision 2 of `mscr_ldo2_rgcn` showed "Specs differ from the saved
task ... Press Save task to save the rows as they are now, or Reload saved values ...", and Save task stored those rows
over revision 3, taking out the Scale 6 that revision had added. Nothing told rows older than the saved task from rows
the user had edited.

**What the page notes.** Which saved revision its rows came from (`store.ts`: `rowsFrom` / `noteRowsFrom`, for the tab
and in the browser's storage, key `gator-workbench-rows-from-v1`). It is noted when a task's rows are loaded (choosing
the task, Reload saved values) and after each Save task (the revision it made). `GET /api/tasks/starter` now answers
a saved task with its `revision` beside the configuration's parts; `tasks.starter_config` itself stays a configuration,
as tests and the check harness use it as one. Save task sends the note as `base_revision`, the check while editing as
`rows_from`.

**Backend.**
- `consistency.undone_by_saving(config, name, saved, base)` says what saving the rows here would undo, per row ID: a field
  the saved task changed since the rows' revision that the rows here still have as before; a row added or changed since
  that they lack; a row removed since that they still have. The rows' revision is the newest earlier one whose rows are
  exactly the rows here (a page from before this change, or rows brought back by Undo), else the page's note. Without
  either nothing can be told, and nothing is refused. Earlier revisions are read from `history/r<N>/task.json`, kept
  parsed by file stamp.
- `profiles.save_task(..., base_revision, replace_newer)`: on an update such rows are refused before any step - nothing
  read or written in Virtuoso, no file - with `NewerSaved`, which carries what they would undo. The route answers 409
  `saved_task_newer` with it (`_bridge_call` now passes an exception's `detail_extra` on). "Save anyway" sends
  `replace_newer`.
- The check while editing (`_check_saved_task`; `rows_from` comes through `validate_loaded` and the new `ValidateBody`):
  for such rows the advice reads "These rows are older than the saved task: it was saved again (revision C) after they
  were loaded (revision B). Press Reload saved values to take the saved rows; Save task would undo that change, and asks
  first." instead of `FIX_ROWS`.

**Page (`TaskSave.tsx`).** A refusal shows the backend's sentence under the toolbar with **Reload saved values** (the
task block's own reload, with its question about discarding edits) and **Save anyway**.

**On the real task.** The rows of revision 5 of `mscr_ldo2_rgcn` against revision 6: revision 5 found, three items -
`normalization_scale` 6 saved, none here, on the three PSRR rows. The rows of revision 6: nothing. Revision 5's rows with
an edit of one's own: nothing without the page's note, the same three items with it.

**Checked.**
- `test_wb_older_rows.py` (new, 4 tests): what would be undone, told from the rows or from the note, and never the user's
  own edit; a row added since and a row taken out since; Save task refused before any step with the files unchanged,
  rows that hold the newer change saved as usual, Save anyway saved; through the routes the 409 with what would be
  undone, `last` in `/api/tasks/saving`, the check's advice with and without `rows_from`, the starter's revision.
- `task_save_check.mjs` 46 -> 58 checks: no revision before the page knows one, the revision of the last save, the
  reloaded page's check gets it; a refusal asks under the toolbar with both buttons and saves nothing; Save anyway sends
  `replace_newer`; Reload saved values reads the saved rows after its own question, and the next save starts from their
  revision.
- The whole Python suite on the working tree: 1,754 passed, 1 skipped. tsc clean. The whole browser suite on the working
  tree: 48 scripts, 1,780 checks passed; `typing_check` failed two checks that look for the header button titled "ONE
  costed ...", which record 7.202 replaced with one "Baseline simulation" button (its session told).

ready* with the proposed values and two buttons: **Apply calibration** and **Not now**. Until now the proposals sat only
in Run status, where they could be missed. It is still the user's press: nothing is applied by itself.

**Asked first.** The user asked whether applying was prompted or automatic: neither was. Of the two ways offered they
took the prompt. Applying automatically was advised against: the page would then hold Specs the saved task does not and
block every start until Save task, without anyone having asked for the change.

**Changed** (page, two wordings in the backend, docs; no file under `src/analog_optimizer/`).
- **`frontend/src/panels/calibrationPlan.ts`** (new): what Apply does to the configuration the page holds, for one
  calibration result - which rows still are the rows the run simulated, how many values would change, the words, and

  do the same; the block's own copy of that logic is gone.

### 7.207 Check now says when another real run holds Virtuoso, instead of blaming the working copy (2026-10-05, session "Run adapter working copy sync issue"; user, after Check now on real:mscr_ldo2_rgcn failed with "BlockingIOError: [Errno 11] Resource temporarily unavailable": "why the eroor", then "yes fix the message")

**In plain words.** Check now on `real:mscr_ldo2_rgcn` failed with "The run adapter could not use the working copy" and
the advice "The working copy was changed in Virtuoso after the task was saved: press Save task". Nothing had changed:
another Claude session ("Reward module scoring design") had started a real 800-evaluation rgcn run on
`real:mscr_ota7t` from a command line at 18:49, and that run held the run adapter's session lock (`session.lock`, one
process on Virtuoso at a time). The adapter's non-blocking `flock` said `BlockingIOError`, and `_connect_fix` gave every
connect failure it did not recognise the advice for a changed copy. A run started from a command line is not shown on
the page, so nothing on the page explained it. Now the check reads nothing from Virtuoso while the lock is held, and
says in one step "Another real run is using Virtuoso, so the working copy could not be checked", who holds it, and that
nothing in the task needs changing.

**Changed** (backend only).
- `consistency.py`: `lock_holder(path)` names the holder of a flock from `/proc/locks` WITHOUT taking the lock (even
  a shared probe, `RunLock.held`, would make a real run that connects at that moment fail): its kind by command line
  (`_HOLDERS`: a run started from a workbench page, from a command line, a Corner check, another check), process number,
  start time and command line; a multiprocessing lane is named by the process that started it. `copy_steps` looks
  first and returns the one step `busy` (`FAILED['busy']`, `FIX_BUSY`) without starting the preflight; a lock taken
  between that look and the adapter's connect (the connect step's detail starts `BlockingIOError`) gives the same step.
  Start, Baseline simulation and Resume are refused with the same words (`summary`: "Not started: another real run is
  using Virtuoso, ...").
- The page needs no change: it shows each step's title, fix and reason as the backend sends them. Its headline still
  says "one step does not agree, so a run would not start".
- Docs: workbench.md ("When another real run holds Virtuoso"), the functionality-reference row of Check now.

**Verified.** Against the live holder (read-only, no lock taken): "held by a run started from a command line (process
3034035, started 18:49: python -m gator_workbench.cli run .../rgcn-spectre/config.json ...)". New test
`test_a_busy_virtuoso_is_named_and_the_task_is_not_blamed` (a real flock held by a child process; the preflight must not
be called; a refused start; the connect-time `BlockingIOError`): test_wb_consistency 8 passed; tests/workbench 1,148 passed, 1 skipped (working tree with other sessions' uncommitted edits). Not live until the backend on 8765 is restarted; not committed.

### 7.208 One way of writing a quantity in Parameters, Probes and Specs: number, SI prefix and unit in every cell; any form typed (2026-10-05, same session as 7.113; user: "can you find a unified way of unit representation in the parameters probes and specs windows considering the convenience usage of users", then chose "Prefix per value")

**Before: four ways.**
- Parameters: plain numbers in the row's own unit (a width in um: 1 ... 20; a resistor in ohm: 9535).
- Specs: an SI letter only below 0.01 and from 10,000 up (record 7.181), so 0.0004 A read `400µ` beside "A" in the Unit
  column, but 0.22 V read `0.22`.
- Run status, Corner check, the graph view and the Training tab: `200 µA` (api.ts `si`, 4 digits). But ohm read "kohm", deg
  read "deg", and a row stored in um was not converted.
- The check's messages: `2e+08`.

Typing took a plain number, an exponent or one SI letter; "200 µA" or "0.2mA" were refused.

**Asked first.** Three looks with previews: Prefix per value (recommended), One prefix per row, Keep today's look. The user
chose Prefix per value.

**The rule** (`frontend/src/units.ts`, `units.py`):
- **Written** as Virtuoso's forms write it: the number, an SI prefix and the unit - `1.205 um`, `45 nm`, `9.535 kohm`,
  `400 uA`, `100 MHz`, `-30 dB`, `60 deg` (English letters since the follow-up below).
  - The digits are exact: the stored number's shortest decimal digits, moved by the prefix and by the unit's power of ten.
  - Zero is written in the row's own prefix (`0 um`); beyond the prefixes a to T, the exponent form.
  - A unit that takes no prefix (dB, deg, %, V/V, a count, a plain number) is written as stored.
- **Read**:
  - A plain number is in the row's unit, the one the Unit column shows. This is Virtuoso's rule, and that of the example
    "4e-4" the user was shown.
  - A prefix letter, or a unit of the same quantity, says its own (`450n`, `0.45 um`, `0.4mA`, `2.2 kohm`, `60 deg`). The
    Greek spellings are read too (µm, kΩ, °).
  - Another quantity ("200 mV" in a current row) or an unknown unit is left unread; a line under the formula bar says why.
  - First built otherwise: a plain number kept the prefix the box showed. A check that typed 0.000002 (meaning 2 µm) into a
    box showing 1 µm stored 2 pm, so that rule was dropped before anything was handed over.
- **The Unit column** writes the row's unit in English letters (`um`, `kohm`, `deg`; a row stored as µm reads `um`) and
  offers each quantity in a list.
  - Another unit of the same quantity (`um` -> `nm`) moves the row's numbers with it, exactly, so every value stays the same.
  - The same unit spelled otherwise changes nothing; an unknown unit is refused in words.
  - Its tooltip says all this; the Probes' Unit column says that a Spec on the probe takes its unit.
- **One table** for the page and the backend: `units.written_forms()` (each spelling with its canonical unit, factor and
  offset; the units that take a prefix; symbols; the quantities' names), sent with `/api/schema` as `unit_forms`.
  - `units.text` writes the backend's messages: the check's row differences ("lower 1.3 GHz here, 1.1 GHz saved"), record
    7.203's sentences, and validation's four messages about canonical numbers (leader range, initial value, circuit
    value), which used `:g`.
  - api.ts `si` is `quantity(value, unit, 4)`.
- **Nothing stored changes by being shown**: tasks, baselines and hashes are untouched. Copy and Ctrl+D fill still carry
  the stored numbers, which Excel reads.

**Checked.**
- `test_wb_unit_text.py` (new, 36 tests: 34 written cases, a non-number and an unknown unit, the schema's table).
- The tests pinning the old wording updated (`test_wb_consistency`, `test_wb_agent_readiness`, `test_wb_older_rows`: 1.3 GHz,
  280 µA, 9.6°, 11 MHz, 200 MHz).
- The whole Python suite on the working tree: 1,792 passed, 1 skipped. tsc clean.
- `unit_text_check.mjs` (new, 11 checks; units.ts imported as it is, with the scratch backend's table): the page writes the
  34 cases exactly as the backend does, everything written reads back exactly, 29 typed forms, the refusals, the
  conversions, the Unit column's labels and list.
- `sheet_check` (record 7.181's section rewritten for this: typing 450n, 0.6 µm, a plain number, a voltage, unit changes to
  nm and um, an unknown unit; 44 checks).
- `typing_check`, `task_rows_check` and `find_fill_copy_check` updated to the written form.
- The whole browser suite: 49 scripts, 1,825 checks, none failed or flaky.

**English letters** (user, after seeing the first version: "dont use greek characters for unit, use english letters
instead"). Micro is written `u`, ohm `ohm`, degrees `deg` and `degC`: `units.py` SYMBOLS and the prefix table, units.ts
`english()` for a unit stored with a Greek letter. The degree sign is not Greek, but it is not a letter either, so it went
too. Typing still reads µ, Ω and °. The tests and checks were changed to the English forms; `sheet_check` now also checks
that no cell of the table writes a Greek letter, and `unit_text_check` that the Unit column's list has none. Then: the unit
and message tests 65 passed; `unit_text_check` 11, `sheet_check` 45, `typing_check` 42, `task_rows_check` 46 and
`find_fill_copy_check` 30, all passed.

Two more checks pinned the old `µ` of api.ts `si()` (`corner_check_check`: "4.2 um", "2.6 uW", "1.65 uW";
`graph_view_check`: "w 2 um -> 5.2 um"), changed to the English forms. The whole browser suite then: 49 scripts, 1,820
checks passed, and those two scripts 91 of 91.

**Live** since 20:57 without a restart of this session's: the session of record 7.202 rebuilt the page (bundle
`index-DcI8KTPF.js`) and restarted 8765 at 20:58:00 (PID 3172631) on the user's approval, from the working tree, which held
this record finished, English letters included (it asked first; answered "safe"). Checked afterwards, read-only:
`/api/schema` carries `unit_forms` with Ω written ohm and ° written deg, and the live bundle writes micro as u.

**No Unit column in Parameters and Specs** (user, looking at the live page: "Is the unit column just for reference now? I
saw values with full unit now. By the way the drag down menu of the unit is in a different style"; told what the column
still did, then: "can you delete the unit column but keep the rules for value. e.g. if a length related value use illegal
units other than ()m then prompt error. Keep the unit rule for probe tab which doesnt need a value input (is this
true?)" - yes: Probes have no values).
- `schema.HIDDEN_COLUMNS` holds `unit` for Parameters and Specs; the rows keep their unit (task files, hashes, the
  baseline untouched). `units.written_forms` also sends the unit of each parameter kind (w, l: m; m, nf: count; r: ohm;
  c: F; ind: H).
- A row's values are read in its own unit unless what the row is says another quantity: a parameter of a known kind, a
  Spec on one Probe (its Probe's unit). Then the row takes that unit with the value; a row without one takes the unit its
  first typed value says (a Spec over several Probes). A value of another quantity is refused in words ("is a length;
  this row holds a count").
- Followers: a Parameters row whose parameter changes takes the new kind's unit; a Spec whose equation becomes one Probe of
  another quantity takes the Probe's unit; a Probe changed to another quantity moves the Specs on it alone, their Scale
  following as a unit typed in the Spec made it.
- Ctrl+C copies a value as the cell writes it (`2 um`), since no column says the unit any more; a paste reads it back.
  Ctrl+D converts a value into the target row's unit and keeps a row of another quantity.
- The Probes' Unit is a list (`select`) like the page's others; the browser's own suggestion list (`datalist`) went.
- Checks: `sheet_check` (no Unit column; a row whose parameter becomes m is a count and refuses a length; back to w),
  `reward_block_check` (Specs without the column; the Probes' list in English letters; a Spec follows its Probe to dB and
  deg, the Scale with it), `find_fill_copy_check` (copy writes the unit), `spec_equations_check` (no typing into a Unit

compressed + worst-spec share 0.5 with no bonuses, the starting preset's training numbers as the live backend served

the live ones at 18:22 (task files equal to that run's apart from per-run preflight and setup logs); the shared bridge
session lock; `gator_workbench.cli run --authorize-real`. The configurations differ from 7.199's only in `run.network`
(RGCN 32 / 4 / 4, GATv2 192 / 3 / 4) and in names and output folders, checked field by field. Order: RGCN 18:49-19:33,
GATv2 19:33-20:10, chained by a script that started GATv2 only after a cell check (9 instances, cell == record) and
checked again after it (cell == record). The live `settings.json` and `network_settings.json` are byte-identical
afterwards and no file in the live task folders is newer than the runs. A first RGCN start at 18:24 was refused by the
session lock, which 7.199's run held, before any launch - with the message that blamed the working copy, which record

**Result** (measures of `defaults-study-2026-10-05/scripts/spectre_analyze.py`; `spectre-rgcn/spectre_table.txt`):

| | morning run (7.183): GT 32 / 4 / 2, lr 3e-4, discount 0.99 | GT preset 48 / 4 / 4 (7.199) | RGCN preset 32 / 4 / 4 | GATv2 preset 192 / 3 / 4 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| designs passing | 36 of 760 | 469 of 760 | 444 of 761 | 419 of 761 |
| first passing design | 591 | 202 | 218 | 289 |
| passing per 100 designs (last: of about 60) | 0 0 0 0 0 2 16 18 | 0 0 27 95 94 98 97 58 | 0 0 49 80 84 92 85 54 | 0 0 5 86 85 93 95 55 |
| last third passing | 36 of 254 | 248 of 254 | 224 of 254 | 237 of 254 |
| median of passing designs: gain, UGBW, PM, bias current | 469, 30.7 MHz, 63.2 deg, 684 uA | 456, 33.1 MHz, 65.8 deg, 691 uA | 445, 28.4 MHz, 65.4 deg, 572 uA | 463, 31.7 MHz, 66.5 deg, 527 uA |

- RGCN passed the most designs in the third hundred (49) but stayed the least steady after it (80-92 per 100).

  Specs row is an objective here, so nothing asked for low current: it is a side effect, not an optimization.

  Transformer preset took 2.0 s, RGCN 2.1 s (hundreds 4-8) and GATv2 2.6-2.9 s: its 192-wide network's learning steps.
  RGCN's designs 100 to 300 took 3.9-4.6 s while another session's 12-worker test suite loaded the machine (load 91 on
  24 cores; the simulator VM shares the CPU) - only the time changed: with one lane and one seed the designs come in
  the same order whatever the speed.

### 7.212 Choosing a registered task shows its newest run in the bottom tabs, or none (2026-10-05, session "Task-run linking in frontend"; user: "Can you tie run with registered tasks? For the frontend, after selecting specific task, it automatically load the most recent corresponding run of the registered task. If there is no recent run, then leave run selection and run status default blank unless you choose other runs.")

**In plain words.** The bottom tabs (Run status, Logs, Data tables, Output files, Training) showed whichever run had been
chosen or started last, so after choosing another task they still showed a run of the previous one. Now choosing a task
under **Task** shows that task's newest run. A Baseline simulation counts: it is one of the task's runs, although its
folder reads `<name>.baseline`. A task without a run shows none ("No run selected", Run status blank). A run chosen in
**Runs...** still counts, and the header's Start / Pause / Abort keep their own run.

**Changed.**
- `runs.py` `list_runs`: every row carries `task_key`, the registered task the run ran, read from `run_manifest.json`
  (`circuit.task_key`). Every run writes it, since the first build: all 15 runs under the live output root carry it,
  the four Baseline simulations included.
- `App.tsx`, an effect on the page's registered task (`config.circuit.task_id` while the source is a registered task).
  - When the task changes, the run list is read again and the tabs show the newest row with that `task_key` (the list
    is newest first by `created_utc`), or no run. The task changes on a choice under Task, an Undo / Redo of that choice
    and a first Save task.
  - `refreshRuns` now wraps `readRuns`, so the effect can tell a failed read from an empty list.
  - The answer is dropped when the task changed again meanwhile, or when the run shown changed meanwhile (a double click
    in the run window, a start). It is also dropped when the read failed, and when no row carries `task_key`. Such a
    server is older than the page, and without that guard every choice would show none until `serve` was restarted.
  - A Virtuoso cell (no task) leaves the run shown. Opening the page follows nothing.
- The run shown is kept in the browser (`gator-workbench-shown-run-v1`, `{run, header}`) together with the header's run of
  that moment, and kept again when the header takes or lets go of a run.
  - A reload therefore shows what was shown, a blank included. Before, a reload put the header's run back, possibly a
    run of another task, under a task that has none.
  - When the header holds another run by the next opening, the page opens on the header's run as it always did. That
    happens after a run started in another tab of the page, or when a check sets `gator-workbench-current-run-v1` and
    reloads: training_tab_check's `showRun` does that 8 times.
  - A first version took the kept run alone and broke exactly that `showRun` (the Training session reported 8 failures).
    The header comparison fixed it.
- `RunList.tsx`: Details names the **Registered task**, the filter finds a task's runs by `real:<name>`, and the window's
  hint says what choosing a task does.
- Docs: workbench.md (the run window: "The run that follows the task"; Choosing a registered task: one bullet),
  functionality-reference (the Task row, the Runs... row, a new row).

**Verified.**
- Backend: new `test_a_row_names_the_registered_task_its_run_ran` (fabricated manifests: newest first, a Baseline
  simulation of the same task, a manifest without a task gives None); `task_key` asserted on the real MOCK run and on
  the API row. test_wb_run_list 9 passed.
- New browser check `frontend/scripts/task_run_check.mjs` (hermetic, fixtures, safe on 8765), 24 checks:
  - choosing a task shows its newest run, not its older Baseline simulation;
  - a task without a run shows none, and Run status is blank;
  - Undo and Redo follow; opening the page follows nothing;
  - a reload keeps a blank, and keeps a run chosen in the window;
  - a run double-clicked while the list read is held stays when the answer comes;
  - a failed read and an older server leave the run shown;
  - Details and the filter name the registered task; the header's run is never changed; no question, no write.
- The whole browser suite (run_checks.sh, 4 lanes) on this tree with the other sessions' uncommitted work: 50 scripts,
  1,873 checks passed, one red. That red was run_control_live_check's "each step once" (the header button read
  "Pausing... > Pause > Resume": a stale status answer racing the pause under load). It passed 23/23 alone twice, and
  this record does not touch the header's phase logic.
- training_tab_check 79 passed in the suite: its `showRun` (header's run + reload) still chooses the run shown.
- pytest -n 12 on the same tree: 1,793 passed, 1 skipped. tsc clean.

**Limits.**
- "Most recent" is the newest by start, the run window's default order: a run started last week and continued today is
  not the newest.
- A task saved again under the name of a deleted task inherits that task's runs: the key is the same.

### 7.213 Learner charts by evaluation, units in the chart titles, and the day's Spectre runs opened in the Training tab (2026-10-05, session "Training tab with live graphs"; user, asked what next: "Open the real runs in the tab (Recommended), Learner charts by evaluations, Units in chart titles")

**Asked.** At "whats next" after 7.205 the user chose three of the offered steps: copy the day's recorded Spectre runs
into the page's output folder so that Runs... and Compare... list them, and say what their charts show; let the
learner's charts use evaluations as their x axis, so that runs learning 1 and 8 times per evaluation line up; write the
units in the chart titles as record 7.208 writes them. The last was offered as "pm_deg (°)", "Ω"; meanwhile the user
told the 7.208 session "dont use greek characters for unit", so the titles follow that instead (English letters).

**Built** (frontend only: `trainingData.ts`, `TrainingTab.tsx`, `TrainingChart.tsx`, the two Training checks; no backend
change).
- **Learner by: update | evaluation** among the Training controls (default update, kept in this browser with the chart
  choices). By evaluation the learner's charts (losses, temperature α, entropy estimate, TD target, gradient norms) are
  drawn along `learner_updates.evaluations_collected`, the number of evaluations collected when the update ran - the
  evaluation charts' x; the 8 updates after one evaluation share its x, in their order. The smoothing then averages the
  updates of the last N evaluations (`smoothOver`, "mean of last 50 evaluations") instead of the last N updates, so two
  runs with different update rates are smoothed over the same stretch of evaluations. After a learner restart the
  abandoned updates stay a series of their own and the restart is marked at the evaluation the learner went on from. The
  hover line names the update under the pointer. Changing the choice lets go of the learner charts' zoom (it was in the
  other unit). A run without the evaluation count would be named on the chart, not drawn at 0.
- Compared runs on one x axis: an x value now takes as many slots as the run that has it most often, so the runs share
  the slots of an evaluation and each keeps its own updates; the hover line looks along the slots of the same x, so it
  gives every run's value there (before, an evaluation had one slot: unique x values only).
- **Units in the chart titles** go through `units.ts` `unitLabel` (7.208's writer, which this record needs; committed as
  `53ae0f9`): a spec stored as µA reads "i_bias (uA)". The runs of the day store English spellings already (V/V, Hz, deg,
  A), so their titles look as before.
- **Compared runs' names.** Four runs of `mscr_ota7t`, seed 0, read alike as "mscr_ota7t · seed 0 · 5915d9" and so on,
  matching nothing the page shows elsewhere. A run is now named as the run window shows it: its experiment (the task's
  name when the experiment kept the default name "experiment"), its seed and when it was made -
  "rgcn-preset-spectre · seed 0 · 05/10, 18:49"; the start of the folder's id is added only when two compared runs would
  read the same.
- **The day's runs in the page.** Eight viewing copies in `/mnt/d/gator/outputs` (git-ignored), byte-identical to the
  archives apart from what was left out (`diff -r` against a fresh extraction: 8 of 8 identical, 2,634 files): save points
  (`checkpoints/`), simulator job folders (`work/`) and waveforms. Folders `starting-presets-7199__d06d29ee261e`,
  `rgcn-preset-spectre__838217ddef7e`, `gatv2-preset-spectre__21264190c6ab` (records 7.199, 7.209) and the five
  `defaults-study__*` (record 7.183), each with a `COPY-README.txt` naming its source; the originals in
  `artifacts/local/reviews/` are untouched. The live backend lists them at once (`GET /api/runs`: 15 runs, read only) and
  does not offer them as saved models (`GET /api/runs/models`: the 3 it offered before). Without a save point a copy
  cannot be continued: the backend's resume refuses "Nothing to resume" (`runs._prepare_resume`, read in the code, not
  tried). The run window's "Can continue" column still says yes for them: it reads the original run's `run_summary.json`.

**What the charts show** (the page's own arithmetic, `trainingData.ts`, on the scratch backend's answers for the copies;
x = the evaluation's number, episode starts included, so the first passing evaluations are a few later than 7.209's
first passing designs; one seed each):

| | GT preset (7.199) | RGCN preset (7.209) | GATv2 preset (7.209) | morning run (7.183) | old defaults: 600 / 150 / 150 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| first passing evaluation | 214 | 230 | 305 | 622 | none |
| mean of last 50 passing ≥ 0.5 from | 318 | 280 | 344 | never (0.28 at 800) | never |
| passing per 100 evaluations (of about 95 designs) | 0 0 19 84 89 92 92 93 | 0 0 38 72 83 86 81 84 | 0 0 0 73 82 85 91 88 | 0 0 0 0 0 0 11 25 | 0 throughout |
| entropy estimate (mean of last 50 evaluations) below 0 from | 272 | 217 | 290 | 555 | never (6.1 at the end) |
| temperature α at the end | 0.00098 | 0.0016 | 0.0013 | 0.0022 | 0.17-0.19 |

- By evaluation the three presets' policies settle (entropy below 0, α falling from 0.01 to about 0.002 by evaluation 300)

### 7.215 Key numbers per run in the Training tab, and "Can continue" checks that the save point is there (2026-10-05, session "Training tab with live graphs"; user, asked what next: "Key numbers in the tab (Recommended), Fix "Can continue" wording")

**Asked.** At "whats next" after 7.213 the user chose two of the offered steps: a line per run with the numbers this
session had worked out by hand for 7.213 (first passing design, passing share of the last 100 evaluations, how the
policy settled, the best design's miss); and the run window's "Can continue", which said "yes" for 7.213's viewing copies
although they hold no save point.

**Built.**
- **Key numbers** (`trainingData.ts` `keyNumbers`, `TrainingTab.tsx`, `styles.css`): a table above the charts, one row per
  run on them, in its chart colour when runs are compared: evaluations recorded; the first passing design (its
  evaluation number); the passing share of the last 100 evaluations (designs with a verdict); the best design's
  worst-spec miss at the newest evaluation; the entropy estimate and the temperature α as the mean of the learner's
  updates of the first 50 evaluations it learned at, then of the last 50. Episode starts are left out as on the charts,
  and every number is read from the recorded rows. Offered as "when the policy settled"; written as first-to-last
  values instead, because any "settled at" point would need a threshold of our own. Folds to its one line; kept as left
  in this browser (the Training settings key gains `keys`).
- **"Can continue"** (`runs.py` `save_point`, `_resumability`): the run list takes the summary's answer only while the
  save point the run's `checkpoints/latest.json` names is still in the folder; otherwise "no". `GET /api/runs/status`
  names that save point as `save_point` (null: none), so the run window's Details say "none: the run's summary names its
  save point ... but the run folder no longer holds it" (`RunList.tsx`), and Run status's **Continue** waits with that
  reason (`RunTabs.tsx`) - before, it was offered and the backend refused "Nothing to resume". A backend without the
  field (older) leaves both as they were.

### 7.216 One setting on every MScR circuit: the Graph Transformer starting preset learns on three of seven, and a new VCO task (2026-10-05/06, session "Gator baseline deletion in RL networks"; user: "Yes try a plan to persuade me that it shall work over all circuits", the runs' approvals "Two other circuits", "LDO 1 lane, OTA 4 lanes", "Yes, 4 lanes", "Yes, raise it", "+200 each", and for the VCO "considering your knowledge, set a starting point for the optimization and add more constraints to the task and save it as a new task. Dont run the original optimization, queue and run the new one.")

**In plain words.** Before any of these runs, a plan fixed the claim - one setting, the Graph Transformer starting preset
with the reward a new page starts with and every Scale box empty, learns on every circuit within 800 designs with nothing
changed per circuit - and how each circuit would be judged. On Spectre it learned on `mscr_ota5t` and `mscr_ldo_basic`
(as on `mscr_ota7t` before), partly on `mscr_ring3`, and not on `mscr_ldo1_rgcn` and `mscr_ldo2_rgcn`; the hold-out
`mscr_vco_lin` stopped after 60 designs because half of its designs do not oscillate. By the rule written down first, one
setting does not hold. Both LDOs fail on the same single row, which no working design met, random or learned, so these
runs cannot tell a learner too slow for it from a row the circuit cannot meet. For the VCO the user had a new task made
and run instead of the original (a passing starting design, resistor ranges where the ring oscillates, missing values
counted as failures). It learned: 500 of 760 designs passed, 94 % of the last third, against 3 of 64 random designs
(4.7 %) in its Baseline simulation right after.

**The test** (Spectre, 2026-10-05 23:20 to 2026-10-06 02:22). One run per circuit, seed 0, 800 launches, the
Graph Transformer starting preset as the live backend served it, the page's starting reward (`SIMPLE_REWARD`: both
bonuses 1), each task as saved with its recorded baseline (record 7.211); code a clean copy of `61f1ea4`; 4 lanes except
`mscr_ldo2_rgcn` (1); the bridge's nonce cache raised to 32,768 for the 4-lane runs. A circuit without a passing design in
800 launches got 200 more from its last save point.

| circuit | lanes | designs | passing | first passing | last third | random designs passing | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `mscr_ota7t` (record 7.199, no reward bonuses) | 1 | 760 | 469 | 202 | 97.6 % | 0 of 64 | learns |
| `mscr_ota5t` | 4 | 760 | 629 | 69 | 99.6 % | 2 of 64 | learns |
| `mscr_ldo_basic` | 4 | 757 | 556 | 28 | 97.2 % | 3 of 63 | learns |
| `mscr_ring3` | 4 | 759 | 82 | 39 | 15.8 % | 1 of 64 | partly |
| `mscr_ldo1_rgcn` (+200) | 4 | 946 | 0 | - | 0 % | 0 of 64 | does not learn |
| `mscr_ldo2_rgcn` (+200) | 1 | 951 | 0 | - | 0 % | 0 of 63 | does not learn |
| `mscr_vco_lin` (hold-out) | 4 | 52 | 0 | - | - | 1 of 58 | not judged: stopped at 60 |
| `mscr_vco_lin_c` (the new task, below) | 4 | 760 | 500 | 18 | 94.1 % | 3 of 64 | learns |

- **The LDOs.** `psrr_hf_db` <= -5 dB (the paper's "PSRR <= -5 dB above 1 MHz", ported from SKY130 at 2.0 V to GPDK045
  at 1.8 V) is met by no working design; 213 (ldo2) and 201 (ldo1) designs met every other row. The runs moved toward
  it - the median miss of the designs meeting every other row fell from 108 to 64 % of the limit (ldo2, best -2.73 dB)
  and from 101 to 49 % (ldo1, best -3.34 dB) - and the +200 found nothing.
- **The ring.** Limited by its 0.95-1.05 GHz window: 631 designs missed only the frequency, by a median that fell from
  33 to 13 % of the limit; passing rose from 4.0 to 15.8 % by thirds.
- **The hold-out.** The failure guard (5 failed designs in a row) stopped it at 60 launches; 31 of 52 designs gave no
  value. Of 110 designs (these and the 58 random), every one with R2 / (R1 + R2) at or under 0.45 oscillated (41 of 41)
  and none of the 61 that did not was under 0.49; the task allows 150-3250 Ohm for both.

**The new task `real:mscr_vco_lin_c`.** Built with the MScR library tooling (`~/mscr_circuits`; every existing cell and
task still builds byte-identically): cells `MScR/mscr_vco_lin_c` and `tb_vco_lin_c` sized as the one random design that
met every row (the starting point of every episode), R1 2000-3250 and R2 300-1500 Ohm (divider under 0.43), "Missing
value = failed" on all 8 Specs rows. Saved through the live backend (revision 1, Maestro matches); Check now passed
every step once the request left out the starter's `revision` field, which the start check refuses (the library's
`check_tasks.py` sent it; its owner session fixed it). The run went to scratch output, so the run was linked into the
user's output root while it ran (the page lists runs there) and copied in when it ended. It learned at 279; every design
gave a value; the last third's passing designs sit mid-window (median 1.349 GHz) at 0.259 mW against the start's 0.28
mW. Its Baseline simulation ran right after (the user: "Right after this run"; through the live backend as the page's
button, 4 lanes, 3 minutes): 3 of 64 random designs passed, it recorded the task's baseline (later runs get the trimmed
library) and proposed 5 Scales, not applied. It is not the hold-out: its task was changed after the hold-out's result.

**What it means.** Not "the preset fails on LDOs": two LDOs fail one row that no design met in these runs, and a third
LDO learned. For ldo2 another session's offline analysis (2026-10-06 ~00:10) found the row out of reach within the
task's ranges - its peak at the loop crossover, the best designs at the limit of every direction that helps; the user
kept the row at -5 dB. Before a rule is tried on the failed circuits (the plan's next step), whether the row can be met
on GPDK045 needs an answer - a sweep around the best design, say. One seed per circuit; a 4-lane run is not repeatable
from its seed, and the preset had been measured on one lane. The parked AnalogGym / SKY130 benchmark stays untouched;
with it, the LDO rows could be tried on the process they were written for.

**Hygiene.** Before and after every run the cell read back equal to its record. Every run used a scratch copy of the
live settings; nothing was written to the live task folders except the new task's own files (its Save task, and the
baseline its Baseline simulation recorded). The runs are copied into `/mnt/d/gator/outputs` (last save point only), so
the page lists them.

this entry and its index row. Evidence (git-ignored): `artifacts/local/reviews/starting-presets-2026-10-05/` (README.md
section "Spectre: the Graph Transformer starting preset on every MScR circuit", `spectre-circuits/`: scripts, one folder

### 7.217 Calibration: "Apply and save", Scales written as quantities, a window rule that follows what the random designs did, no Initial alpha that changes nothing (2026-10-05, session "RL reward design"; user: "check the current reward design, is there anything to improve?", "together with the calibration flow", then "what six items are you going to build" and "build the items")

**In plain words.** Six points were offered. Built as offered: **Apply and save** in one press for a task saved from a
cell; calibration values written as the page writes every quantity (20 mV, 11 MHz); an Initial alpha proposed only
when it differs from the one in use by a factor of 2 or more. Built narrower: the calibration's rule for a window (a
row with a lower and an upper limit) - half its width only when most random designs are already inside it. Not built:
the two changes of what the page types into a new Specs row (half a window; 10 degrees on a phase row). The free test
runs made while building decided the last three; they are told below, with what was got wrong on the way.

**What the tests found** (free ngspice runs, the page's starting reward, today's presets, 63 runs;
`artifacts/local/reviews/reward-review-2026-10-05/README.md`).
- **The margin bonus is paid on the row with the least room measured in its Scale** - so a Scale decides which row the
  bonus protects. With every Scale the size of its limit it sat on the gain row for 61 % of the passing designs of the
  test circuit and on the row that fails, the frequency row, for 13 %. Over nine settings the share of passing designs
  in the last third followed that second share: 94 to 96 % with it at 0 to 23 %, 98.5 to 98.8 % with it at 39 to 87 %.
- **A window with a wide Scale takes the bonus from every other row**: 82 % and 99 % of the passing designs on two
  circuits. Half the window instead: 95.7 % to 98.7 % passing (three seeds, every one above every one without) and
  91.7 % to 96.0 % (six seeds, inside the spread between them). About 8 % more power in the typical passing design,
  the best design of a run 62 against 60 uW.
- **A narrow window most random designs miss was found as fast with half its width as its Scale** (first passing
  design at 84 to 96 against 70 to 90; 62 to 69 % passing in all four settings) - at a typical miss of 6.6
  half-widths. The ring oscillator's frequency row is missed by 30 half-widths: not tested, and not risked.
- **10 degrees on a phase row** changed nothing where the phase margin is far from its limit, and beside the window
  rule nothing where it binds - it moved the failures from the frequency row to the phase row.
- **A fixed bonus for passing**: 96.3 % against 94.4 %, the same row failing. Not built; no optimizer change.
- **Said too early.** After the first fifteen runs this session told the user that the page's 6 dB on dB rows "probably
  did most of this". Run for itself it did not (95.7 % against 94.4 %): it only moved the bonus from the gain row to
  the window. The two together did it. And a first state of this record cut EVERY window to half its width; the seven
  real calibrations of the evening showed where that would have gone - 50 MHz for a row random designs miss by
  1.5 GHz - before anything was applied.
- **On Spectre** the calibrated Scales did not help `mscr_ota7t` (record 7.211, another session, the same evening).
  Nothing of this record ran on Spectre: the window rule changes what is proposed, never what is applied.

**Changed** (no file under `src/analog_optimizer/`; the page's Scale suggestion and rows read from a cell unchanged).
- **`calibration.py`**: rule 3. A window at least half of the random designs are inside gets at most half its width -
  when too few miss it to name a typical miss and its Scale is wider, or when the typical miss is wider. A window most
  designs miss keeps the typical miss, and its note says what that leaves of the margin bonus. `initial_alpha` gains
  `estimate`; `proposed` stays empty within a factor of 2 of the value the run had, with a note. Notes write numbers
  as quantities (`units.text`). `current(run_dir, manifest)`: a file written under another rule is worked out again
  from the run's tables when it is read (once per backend process; `recalculated` names both rules; nothing is written
  into a run folder). `api/run_routes.py`: the status route answers `calibration.current`.
- **`TaskSave.tsx`**: `requestTaskSave(task)` - a window event the open task's Save task answers at once (taken, or
  why not) and then saves with the rows of the next render, so a change made in the same click is in the save.
- **`calibrationPlan.ts`**: Scales as quantities; `saves` (Apply would change a Specs row of a task saved from a
  cell); the words both places say after the press.
- **`App.tsx`, `RunTabs.tsx`**: for such a task **Apply and save** and **Apply only** (elsewhere Apply calibration as
  before); the block's Scales through `si()`; a line when the result was worked out again; "proposed nothing: <why>".
- **`calibration_save_check.mjs`** (new, hermetic, 22), `calibration_check.mjs` (36 -> 38), `run_checks.mjs` (its
  expected time), `test_wb_calibration.py` (10 -> 13).

**Seen in the seven real calibrations** (read again under rule 3, nothing written). `mscr_ldo_basic` output window:
6 of 57 random designs miss it, Scale 1.22 V -> 20 mV. `mscr_ring3` frequency: 63 of 64 miss, by 1.5 GHz -> 1.5 GHz,
with the note. `mscr_vco_lin` centre frequency: 26 of 28 miss -> 430 MHz. No Initial alpha is proposed on any of the
seven (estimates 0.008 to 0.018 against 0.01). Also seen, and not changed: where broken designs miss a row by far more
than its limit the proposal LOOSENS the row - `mscr_ldo1_rgcn` dropout 0.22 V -> 2.2 V, its PSRR rows 6 dB -> up to
26 dB. Read such a proposal before applying it.

**Tests.** `tests/workbench/test_wb_calibration.py` 13 passed (three new: a window most designs are inside and one most designs miss; the Initial alpha by the factor of 2; a result of another rule worked out again when read). The whole suite on the working tree: 1,800 passed, 1 skipped, and one test of the optimizer's benchmark that depends on the machine's load (`tests/test_benchmark_runtime.py::test_benchmark_cli_recording_and_parallel_budget`) failed once while the browser checks and another session's Spectre runs were going, and passed when its file was run again by itself (4 passed). Earlier the same evening, on this record's first state: 1,803 passed, 1 skipped. `tsc --noEmit` clean; a private build of the working tree in a Windows-side copy (the live `frontend/dist` untouched), served by scratch backends running the copy's own code: all 51 scripts, 1,887 checks passed (bundle `index-BagAdDD6.js`) - `calibration_save_check` 22 (new), `calibration_check` 38, `task_save_check` 58, `run_control_check` 74, `reward_block_check` 30 as before.

**For the user.** After a Baseline simulation of a saved task: **Apply and save**, or **Apply only** to look first;
Not now as before. While another session's run holds the Virtuoso session, Save task is refused as it always is; the
applied values stay on the page.

**Limits.** ngspice only for everything measured here. The rule for a window most designs miss is the rule of record
7.197, kept. The six Baseline simulations recorded under the unreleased rule 2 show rule 3's proposals on the page;
their files still say rule 2.

Docs: `docs/workbench.md` (Calibration with the baseline run), `docs/decisions.md` ("What a Scale does to the margin
bonus ..."), the functionality reference's two calibration rows, the index row. 
**Live** since 23:54:23 (user: "go on", to the session "Virtuoso read whole flow"): `frontend/dist` rebuilt from the

### 7.218 Fewer boxes inside boxes: callouts drawn by a rule at their left, without a frame (2026-10-05, same session as 7.158; user: "work on fewer boxes inside boxes", item 5 of the answer to "any other optimization possible?")

**Measured first.** On the live page with a real task's rows, every element was counted that is drawn as a box (a border on
three or four sides, a fill of its own, or a shadow; fields, buttons and tables left out), together with its boxed
ancestors. The page had 10 boxes: the header, the five panels, the footer, and three inside a panel - the "Before every run"
note in the Project column, the reward note in Run configuration, and the formula bar's cell-name field. The windows and
the states that come and go added more. The RL networks window's "values differ" note holds a table and two buttons. The
Connection window frames Add a connection, its test result and its note. Matched devices put a framed review, a table
inside, in its own frame: three levels. All of them came from 16 rules in `styles.css`, `.note` alone used in 19 places.

**Changed** (`styles.css` only, shared by both looks). Inside a panel, the header or a window a callout has no frame: a
rule at its left sets it off, the accent colour for a note (`.note`), a confirmation (`.confirm`), the column help line
(`.guidance`) and a group (`.matched-devices`, `.connection-window .connection-add`, `.calibration-ready`, `.ideas`,
`.selection-info`), the status colour for a status. Only a status that must not be missed keeps its faint fill: `.blocker`,
`.status-line` (a connection test result), `.provisional`, `.virtuoso-window .copy-warning`, `.mock`, `.real`,
`.settings-problem`. A callout inside another one has no rule of its own: the review inside Matched devices sat as a
second rule beside the first, and its heading sets it off alone. The MOCK banner between the header and the columns
(`.banner.mock`) keeps its frame: it is inside nothing. Fields, buttons, tables, the Agent tab's chat and notes areas,
charts and the panels keep their frames. The reward note (`.note.reward-other`, the "RL reward design" session's design)
follows the shared rule like every note; its text and buttons are unchanged.

### 7.219 Performance proposals: functionality review and Claude handoff (2026-10-05, session "Project audit"; user: "check alll the proposed modifications if they will affect functionality or not. wrtie a plan for claude to work on all of them")

**Plan only.** `docs/plan-performance-and-learning-efficiency.md` reviews all 13 proposals from the performance audit:
conditional gamma-zero work removal, replay copies, collection/learning overlap, CPU contention, input caches,
checkpoints/storage, chart preparation, training API polling, deferred waveform transfer, calibration replay reuse,
update ratio, encoder size, and resume/fine-tune workflow. Each has its functionality effects, source locations,
implementation steps and acceptance tests; the handoff gives phased delivery, experiment discipline and rollback.

The review distinguishes equivalent calculations from changed learning behavior. Gamma remains the user's actual
setting; skipping an unused sample must preserve RNG consumption. Public replay samples must remain isolated from
mutation. Any optimizer-package edit changes its source digest and the compatibility of existing checkpoints.
Asynchronous learning and calibration import stay optional; update ratio and model size require controlled trials.
Calibration currently uses a different episode horizon from the starting training preset, so reward recalculation
alone does not make its observations compatible. Existing per-chart caching and identical final-checkpoint reuse
must not be rebuilt as if absent.

**Validation and scope.** Source and documentation inspected; references and all 13 work-package entries checked.
The earlier synthetic profiling artifacts are linked with their limitations, not presented as new measurements.
No application/optimizer code, presets, live settings or run data changed; no tests, training runs, real simulations
or paid calls started. Only the plan and its documentation index/catalog records were added. Concurrent sessions'
existing changes remain in place. No commit, build or deployment.

### 7.211 Calibration on Spectre: it did not help on mscr_ota7t; a Baseline simulation on the six other MScR tasks (2026-10-05, session "Single baseline simulation merge"; user, asked what next: "Spectre test of calibration", then "1 lane, ~85 min", and "Baseline on 6 MScR circuits")

**In plain words.** The calibrated Scales of a Baseline simulation raised the passing designs from 93 to 98 % on the
free ngspice circuit (7.197). On Spectre, on `real:mscr_ota7t`, they did not help: with the Graph Transformer starting
preset the calibrated runs learned as fast or about 250 designs later, passed slightly fewer designs late (93.5 against
97.0 %), and drew the same current. So the calibration stays a proposal - shown in Run status and "Calibration ready",
applied only by a press - and nothing applies it by default. Then each of the six other MScR tasks got its Baseline
simulation: all seven now have a recorded baseline and calibration proposals to look at.

**The test** (2,400 Spectre simulations, 21:03-22:55). Every run exactly as record 7.199's Graph Transformer run (code
`2f66279`, one lane, 800 launches, reward compressed / share 0.5 / no bonuses); that run is the empty-box arm's seed 0.
The calibrated arm: the 18:03 proposals (gain 260 V/V, ugbw 11 MHz, pm 9.6 deg, ibias 0.28 mA) saved into the task of a
settings copy - the page's Apply calibration + Save task - and Initial alpha 0.011 (the same proposal under this reward).

| | empty, seed 0 | empty, seed 1 | calibrated, seed 0 | calibrated, seed 1 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| first passing design | 202 | 146 | 455 | 177 |
| last third passing | 98 % | 96 % | 95 % | 92 % |
| median bias current, last-third passing | 684 uA | 727 uA | 619 uA | 746 uA |
| failing after learning | 3.9 % | 6.3 % | 3.4 % | 8.5 % |

In the late seed the network's choices stayed as spread as at the start for ~3,000 updates while its temperature fell
as in the control, so the Initial alpha was not the cause. Two seeds on one task: not proof that calibration hurts.
"Failing after learning" is "RL reward design"'s measure (from max(300, first pass + 100)).

**The six Baseline simulations** (390 Spectre simulations, 22:55-23:17, through the live backend as the page's button,
one lane, the user's output root): mscr_ota5t, ldo_basic, ldo1_rgcn, ldo2_rgcn (kept its earlier reference), ring3,
vco_lin - every one completed, 28 to 64 scored random designs each, proposals for 3 to 6 Specs rows each, Initial alpha
only for vco_lin (0.02). The table: the evidence README.

**Hygiene.** Before every run and after the last the cell was compared with its record: equal everywhere. The test
ran from scratch settings copies; the live task's working copy kept its 8 setup folders. Other sessions' test suites
loaded the machine during the first run (load 120): only slower, the learner makes 8 updates per design whatever the
clock. Checkpoints of the three new runs packed (1.2 GB -> 422 MB).

with the baseline run": the "Not measured" bullet became the Spectre result), this entry and its index row. Evidence

### 7.221 The six lower/moderate-risk speed items of the 7.220 plan: lent replay samples, a gamma-0 shortcut, cheaper cache keys, shared chart work, cached Training-tab reads, measurement rules (2026-10-06, session "Time consumption analysis"; user, after the audit of the plan: "do all the items. not able to continue is ok")

**Asked.** Build items A-F of `docs/plan-low-moderate-risk-performance.md` (record 7.220), accepting that runs saved
before the optimizer change can no longer be continued. The audit that preceded it (2026-10-05,
`artifacts/local/reviews/plan-low-moderate-risk-audit-2026-10-05/README.md`) had re-measured the plan's evidence on
the recorded `mscr_ota7t` replay: the replay copy costs ~28 ms per learner step there, three times the synthetic
fixture's 8-9 ms, so item E is the largest win; item D's full form can keep the random numbers exact.

**How it was built.** In isolated copies on the Linux disk (`/home/fyworkstation/gator-perf-7221`, rebased from
`c170802` to `3ce9717` when 7.217 landed), never under a running worker; A and B by two helper agents in their own
copies, reviewed and merged here. Baseline of the untouched copy: 1,778 passed, 21 skipped.

**D - gamma 0.** `SACLearner.update` with a discount of exactly 0 leaves out the next states' batch, the next-state
actor forward and both target critics: the target IS the reward (`rewards + 0 * finite` is the reward bit for bit;
only a reward of -0.0 could come out as +0.0, equal in every comparison and loss). The next-state actor's noise is
still drawn - `GroupActor.sample` calls `Normal(mu, std).rsample()`, i.e. one `torch.empty(shape).normal_()` with one
entry per active action, which the transition contract keeps equal between a state and its next state - so the torch
generator and every later action stay exactly as before (`_draw_skipped_next_action_noise`). Every nonzero discount,
however small, keeps the full calculation. `SACLearner.zero_gamma_shortcut` exists only so tests can compare with the
full calculation; nothing changes a configured gamma. Lost: incidental non-finite detection inside the unused branch
(transition validation, loss and gradient checks are unchanged).

**E - lent replay samples.** `ReplayBuffer.update_learner(learner, n)` draws exactly what `sample(n)` draws (same
generator, items, order) and lends the stored transitions to `learner.update` for that call only, without the deep
copy; `sample()` still returns deep copies to every other caller, admission still copies. The engine's two learner
calls (`_maybe_update`, `_initial_update`) use it. The CLI collectors (`runner.py`, `parallel.py`, `benchmark.py`)
keep `sample()`.

**F - cache keys and capacity.** The input cache key is now `sha256(schema digest + observation digest)`; the schema
digest is remembered per (FeatureSchema, SpecSchema) - both deeply immutable - and the replay's lend path works each
stored observation's digest out once and passes it along (`batch(..., digests=...)`), so cache hits no longer pickle
20 kB observations. Other callers (`act()`, `update()` on public samples) still hash every observation in full; equal
content finds the same entry either way. `PdkViewLearner` takes the argument and ignores it: the PDK view makes new
objects, keyed by their own content. `info()` adds evictions, tensor bytes and an optional `max_bytes` bound.
Capacity study (recorded replay repeated 1x/3x/7x/14x into a 10,000 replay, copies tagged in graph metadata, GT
preset, gamma 0): every cached entry holds ~4.6 kB of tensors and a miss costs ~1.3 ms; up to ~2,500 transitions every
capacity hits 100 %, at 5,257 transitions 2,048 entries hit 52 % (52.7 ms per step against 45.5 with all hits), at a
full 10,000 replay 29 % (58.6 against 48.0). So the learner's default became **8,192 entries** (all 7,964 distinct
states of a full replay, ~36 MB) with a 256 MiB byte bound for unexpectedly large graphs.

**C - measurement.** `docs/performance-measurement.md`: the quiet-machine procedure (load checked before EACH timed arm,
resources and load recorded, nobody else's job touched), the existing resource settings (1 torch thread per worker;
`OMP_NUM_THREADS=1` for the hash-pinned `examples/` launchers; `-n 4` and `--lanes 2` while others measure or
simulate), and `tests/perf/learner_update_profile.py`, a repeatable learner-step timing (uninstrumented totals and
phases, synthetic or a recorded save point, threads and load recorded). CLAUDE.md points to it.

**"Can continue" after a digest change** (raised by the "Training tab with live graphs" session): a full save point of
another optimizer source now reads "model only" in the run list and its Details say why; Run status's Continue is off
with the reason (`runs.save_point_code`, status field `save_point_code`; the current digest is kept 30 s because
hashing it takes ~0.4 s on the 9p mount).

**Equivalence (exact, all three encoders).** gamma-0 shortcut vs the full calculation at batch 1/3/6, warm and cold
cache, over four updates: losses, targets, weights, target networks, all three optimizers' moments, temperature,
counters, torch/Python/NumPy and replay generators and the following sampled and deterministic actions identical; the
skipped draw has exactly the next-state batch's active-action count; nonzero gamma (1e-300 to 0.99) batches next states
and bootstraps; save and resume with gamma 0 gives the exact next update. Lent vs copied samples over ring wraparound
and eviction: identical updates and generators, the whole replay fingerprint-identical after every update; changing
admitted originals, public samples or batch tensors never reaches the replay. End to end: a seeded MOCK run records the
same proposals, executed sizes, rewards and learner losses on the new and the old path (gamma 0 and 0.99), and the run's
own gamma decides the shortcut; with the replacement draw removed on purpose that test FAILS (sensitivity checked).
Old save points: HEAD's `src/analog_optimizer` (digest `f83342bc...`) loads the GATv2 run's final save point and two
loads give identical next updates; the new source refuses it.

**Measured.** One learner step as the engine records it, median, load ~2, before -> after:

| Preset | synthetic | recorded `mscr_ota7t` replay |
|---|---|---|
| Graph Transformer 48/4/4 | 53.3 -> 32.0 ms (-40 %) | 83.1 -> 46.5 ms (-44 %) |
| RGCN 32/4 | 71.0 -> 47.5 ms (-33 %) | 118.0 -> 56.6 ms (-52 %) |
| GATv2 192/3/4 | 108.2 -> 82.9 ms (-23 %) | 191.1 -> 122.3 ms (-36 %) |

**Whole run (MOCK, so the learner dominates).** Seeded 200-evaluation MOCK runs with the Graph Transformer starting
preset's training numbers (8 updates per evaluation, gamma 0, batch 8), new and old path alternating, each started on
a quiet machine (load 1.7-2.8): the engine's own recorded `update_seconds` 45.8 / 44.7 s against 93.9 / 90.6 s for the
same 1,224 updates (37 against 75 ms each), wall 71.7 (the first, cold) / 54.4 s against 103.9 / 105.7 s; the two
paths recorded the same learner losses. On a real Spectre run the simulator time is unchanged, so the gain is the
learner's share: about 0.27 s of the 2.23 s per evaluation of 2026-10-05's 1-lane Graph Transformer `mscr_ota7t` run
(-12 %), 0.46 of 3.24 s for RGCN, 0.51 of 2.76 s for GATv2 (-18 %) - estimates from the step timings, not measured on
Spectre (no simulator time was spent).

**A - the Training tab's charts** (helper agent, reviewed): `trainingData.ts` gives each run's store one shared,
incrementally kept chart preparation (a `WeakMap`, released with the store): evaluation categories, pass flags and
counts, learner restarts and abandoned updates, the learner's rows in the order of each x axis (not copied while rows
arrive in order, sorted only when an x goes down), the evaluations a comparison draws, and running means (smoothing, the
mean of the last N evaluations, the share so far) as checkpoints every 4,096 positions, replayed with the same additions
in the same order, so the values are bit-identical. A table notes where its rows changed other than by growing
(`Changes`: a flush cutting the provisional tail, thinning, a spec value joining an earlier page's evaluation), and only
what follows that row is derived again. A chart then works out only its rows in view; key numbers use binary-searched
ranges. `TrainingTab.tsx` (its `builtCache` and every invalidation) and `TrainingChart.tsx` are unchanged. One deliberate
change: an x that is NaN gets its own pixel column in `decimate` (before, the loop never ended; valid data has none).
Changed-frame CPU (node, 8 charts, whole run): 8,000 updates 5.5 -> 3.0 ms (1 run), 50 -> 12.9 ms (4 runs); 200,000
updates 101 -> 23 ms and 997 -> 138 ms; the newest 1,000 at 200,000: 79 -> 0.9 ms and 917 -> 3.4 ms. In the browser (a
live run of 121,712 updates, 8 charts): 109.8 -> 24.5-30.6 ms main thread per changed frame. Memory: the shared part
holds 0.5 MB for a 200,000-update run; heap growth in one 8-chart frame 198 -> 31 MB; the heap returns to 7.3-7.9 MB after
each of four run switches. Evidence: about 2.9 million comparisons with the builders before this record over 800+
random seeds, 0 differences; 4 recorded Spectre runs read in pages by the backend's own reader, 40,980 comparisons, 0
differences; 30 of 33 deliberately broken variants caught (the 3 others cannot change an output). The checks:
`training_data_check.mjs` 92 -> 117 (145 with `--reference <the earlier trainingData.ts> --seeds 40`, exact),
`training_tab_check.mjs` 83 -> 89 (a long live run; memory after run switches and after removing a compared run; a
listener-count race in the check fixed). New `frontend/scripts/training_chart_profile.mjs` (timing; `--verify` checks
the timed frames against an earlier module).

**B - the Training tab's reads** (helper agent, reviewed): `read_training` reads the configuration and the manifest once
each (`_objective`, `_learner`; the public `run_objective`/`learner_record` keep their behaviour) and keeps a bounded
incremental index of the flush log (`_flush_records`): a verified offset and the unacknowledged bytes after it, only
complete lines consumed, each record reduced to the four fields the reader uses; before reading on, the log must be
the same file (device and inode), not shorter, the same size only with the same times, its first 4 kB and the last
record read unchanged - anything else, an unreadable or oversized log, or a corrupt complete line, reads it exactly as
before (`read_jsonl`, the same errors with the line's number). The configuration's objective is kept while its text is
byte-identical (on D: a stat costs about as much as reading the 14 kB file). Never kept: the manifest, the worker's
state and lock, the journal tail, the Parquet parts, a failed read. Bounds: 16 runs / 16 MiB of log, 32 runs / 4 MiB of
configuration; one lock, never held while a file is read. `read_training(..., cached=False)`, `clear_caches()`,
`cache_stats()` for tests and measurement. Interleaved in one process (same load): an unchanged poll of the recorded
800-evaluation run on D: 57.2 -> 29.7 ms, one flush behind 135.9 -> 97.7 ms; a 2,500-record log on 9p 905 -> 18.5 ms
unchanged and 861 -> 36.9 ms with one record appended; system calls per unchanged poll 74 -> 34 (6,009 -> 24 for the
2,500-record log); a whole HTTP request 36.9 -> 25.2 ms. Tests: every poll is compared three ways - the reader before
this record (kept word for word in the test file as the oracle), uncached and cached - across growing runs, tail and
flush replacement, compaction, torn and corrupt records, atomic replacement, cuts, same-size rewrites, a run remade at
the same path, a resumed writer, 8-thread polls with and without eviction, and a scratch server polled by four pages
during a live MOCK run (every row exactly once). `test_wb_training_series.py` 12 -> 23 tests.

**Compatibility.** Every file under `src/analog_optimizer/` that changed changes the source digest:
`f83342bc...` -> `4aebbfab2176605c6e984d3b42ae622335246dfed693c5b9cc39f942c0aaad73`. Save points written before this record (the eight viewing copies of 2026-10-05's
Spectre runs in `outputs/` included) can no longer be continued under the new source - the user accepted that - but
still load for fine-tune and frozen (the digest is a warning there); they resume exactly under their own source, any
commit from `ab34f03` up to the one before 7.221's. Checkpoint format, fields and cadence are unchanged.

**Tests.** On the merged copy (HEAD `3ce9717` + 7.221): the full regression **1,834 passed, 21 skipped** (`-n 4`;
the untouched copy: 1,778 passed, 21 skipped), and the 21 evidence tests, run with `artifacts/` linked in read-only,

`artifacts/local/reviews/plan-low-moderate-risk-audit-2026-10-05/`).

### 7.222 Rows not saved in the task, highlighted in Parameters, Probes and Specs (2026-10-06, session "Parameters, specs, and probes linking in Virtuoso"; user: "In the parameter probe and spec windows, highlight the rows that are unsaved")

**In plain words.** Every row that differs from what Save task stored is coloured now: amber when it changed (the changed
cells a darker amber), green when it is new, each with a bar at its start; the tab of a table with such rows carries a
dot. Hovering says what the task holds.

**Before.** The page knew only as a whole that rows differed: the blocking issue "Parameters differ from the saved task
real:<name> (revision n): ..." under the table and in the header, and the question of Reload saved values. Which rows, the
sentence said; the table did not.

**Backend.** `consistency.unsaved_rows(config, saved)`: per table the IDs of the rows here and not saved (`added`), saved
and not here (`removed`), the rows that differ (`changed`: the saved value of each differing field, and the saved row's
unit), and whether the same rows stand in another order (`reordered`). Rows are read as `row_differences` reads them (by
the schema, so a field given its default equals one left out): a mark is exactly what makes the start check refuse.
`validation._check_saved_task` keeps it in `Report.unsaved_rows` in every run mode (before the early return of a resume) -
an attribute, not in `as_dict`, so the start checklist, the agent's reports and a refused start's report are unchanged -
and `POST /api/config/validate` answers it as `unsaved_rows`: None without a saved task (a cell read without a task, an
ngspice task, a test task).

**Page.** `Editors.tsx` marks rows by ID from the validation answer: `tr.unsaved-changed` / `tr.unsaved-new`, the changed
cells `td.unsaved-cell` (Specs: the objective is the Limit cell's). The tick-box cell's tooltip names each change with its
saved value as the cell writes it ("Changed since the task was saved: Upper (saved: 1.5 um)"), also of a column not in
view (Scale behind More columns, a Spec's unit); a changed cell's tooltip starts "Saved in the task: 1.5 um.". A tab with
such rows gets `has-unsaved` - a dot drawn by CSS, so the tab's text ("Parameters (5)") is unchanged - and a tooltip that
counts them and names the saved rows deleted here and rows in another order ("Not saved in the task: 3 changed, 1 new"); a
tie toggle whose folded rows are not saved takes the mark's colour and says how many. Colours by name in both looks
(`--unsaved-row`, `--unsaved-cell`, `--unsaved-mark`, `--added-mark`, with `--added-bg` of an added diff row); an error
row keeps `--bad-row`, its changed cells still marked; the sticky tick box and ID take the tint.

**Asked again after a save.** Validation was asked only when the configuration's text changed, so after a Save task whose
rows came back as they were nothing asked again: the marks - and, from reading the code, the blocking issue "... differ
from the saved task" - would stay until the next edit. `store.ts` `noteTaskSaved` / `useTaskSaves`: a count raised when
rows of a saved revision are noted (`noteRowsFrom`: Save task, choosing a task, Reload saved values) and when a save the
page followed ends (`TaskSave.tsx`); App's validation request depends on it.

**Also.** The `<datalist id="gator-units">` left by 7.208 went: no input names it since the Probes' Unit became a list.
functionality-reference's 7.208 row named `UnitBox` for `UnitSelect`.

**Checked.** `test_wb_unsaved_rows.py` (new, 5 tests): the saved rows mark nothing; a changed, a new and deleted rows
named with the saved values; a field given its default is no change, another order is said (and still blocks); every run
mode, only a saved task, not in `as_dict`; the route. `unsaved_rows_check.mjs` (new, 21 checks; validation answered from a
fixture's saved rows field by field, as the backend does): rows, cells and tooltips; the tabs' dot and count; a tie
toggle; an error row; the colours of both looks read from their names; typing the saved value back takes the mark away;
Save task asks validation again with the same rows and every mark goes; a save that ended elsewhere, followed after a
reload, brings the mark of the row it left behind; no saved task, no marks. The whole suites on the working tree: Python
1,806 passed, 1 skipped; browser 53 scripts, 1,949 checks passed and one not: crash_panel_check's "Start from an empty
configuration" missed its 15 s while all 53 scripts ran, and passed alone twice after. `tsc --noEmit` clean.

bundle `index-DyPXgTWt.js` from the working tree with its record 7.223. 8765 still runs the backend started at 23:54,
which sends no `unsaved_rows`, so nothing is marked until it is restarted; validation is already asked again after a

### 7.223 Switching task asked to discard edits nobody made (2026-10-06, session "Unsaved changes blocking task switch"; user: "I cant switch from mscr ota7t to other registered tasks as it says unsaved changes but I cant see where it is")

**In plain words.** There was no edit. Three tasks - real:mscr_ota7t, mscr_ring3 and mscr_vco_lin - were saved before the
Specs tolerance column retired (7.172, 2026-10-03), so their task files still carry `tolerance: 0.0` on every spec. The
page drops that field when it reloads (`store.ts` `migrateTarget`), while choosing another task compared the page's rows
with the saved ones as JSON text (`Left.tsx` `sameRows`): after any reload the rows never matched again, and the switch
- and Reload saved values - asked "This replaces your unsaved Parameters, Probes and Specs edits". OK lost nothing.

**Found.** The user's page rows (read from a copy of the browser's storage) against `GET /api/tasks/starter`: parameters
and probes identical, the four specs differing only by the missing `tolerance`. 7.222's `unsaved_rows` and the start
check's `row_differences` find nothing on the same rows (both read rows through the schema), so 7.222 live would have
marked nothing either.

**Fix.** `Left.tsx` compares the three tables as a reload leaves them, on BOTH sides: `tablesOf` = the rows through
`store.ts` `migrateConfig`, used by `sameRows` (choosing a task, Reload saved values, the "now show this task's rows" line)
and by the testbench-probe follow's two comparisons. What a choice puts on the page is unchanged (the saved rows as served;
a reload tidies them, as before). A first version tidied only the saved side (`readStarter`, live at 00:55): rows on the
page that still name a retired field - virtuoso_source_check's saved task brings `target_kind`/`relation`/`name` - then
never matched, and choosing that task asked to discard edits (found by the session committing 7.221/7.222: 60 passed, 2
failed). The task files are unchanged (a Save task rewrites them without the field).

**Checked.** `task_rows_check.mjs` gained a task saved with `tolerance: 0` (real:old_saved): it loads, a page reload drops
the field, and switching away and Reload saved values then ask nothing - 46/50 on the build before 7.223 (the reported
dialog, reproduced). On this tree through run_checks.sh: task_rows 50, virtuoso_source 88, testbench_probes 40, task_save

### 7.225 Saved RL models get names of their own, and can be renamed (2026-10-06, session "Random seed explanation"; user: "for saved rl models with same names add numbering to them by default and add a functionality of rename saved model")

**Before.** A run's model was named by the RL model label (7.159) - the task's run label unless one is typed - so the
saved-model list held eight cards named `mscr_ota7t` and two `mscr_ldo2_rgcn_gt`, told apart by their save times only.

**Names of their own.** No two models under one output root carry the same name:
- At a start, a label a model carries already gets the first free number: `mscr_ota7t`, `mscr_ota7t (2)`,
  `mscr_ota7t (3)` (`checkpoints.free_model_label`; counted from the name without its own number, so a taken `ota (2)`
  becomes `ota (3)`, never `ota (2) (2)`). The run manifest's `model_label` holds the name given. The label itself stays
  the key the run is remembered under (`settings.json` `last_run`), so resume / fine_tune / frozen still start from the
  newest model saved under the label. Every run that saves a model takes a name, with or without a save point yet; a
  Baseline simulation or an LLM-only run takes none (`prepare_run`; `runs.model_label_rows` reads the manifests and
  `model_label.json` under the root, nothing else).
- Models from before - and any two runs that got one name, e.g. copied folders - are numbered the same way when they
  are listed, oldest first (`checkpoints.unique_model_labels`, applied in `runs.list_runs`: renamed models keep their
  names, then the others oldest first). A run from before the label (no `model_label` in its manifest) is named by its
  task when it holds a save point. Every run row carries `model_label` (the unique name) and `renamed`; `saved_models`
  takes the row's name.
- The Runs window has a **Model** column (its filter finds the name); the saved-model cards show the name as before.

**Rename.** Under the chosen card in "RL model to load" and in "Saved models (look only)": a **Name** box with the
model's name and **Rename** (Enter in the box does the same), disabled while the name is unchanged or empty.
`POST /api/runs/model/rename {run_dir, model_label}` writes `model_label.json` in the run folder (the name, the previous
one, the name given at the start, when, a history): the manifest keeps the name given at the start, and the engine
rewrites the manifest from memory while a run lives but never this file, so a live run can be renamed too. A name
another model under the same output root carries is refused (409 `name_taken`, said in plain words under the box;
nothing is numbered on a rename - the user chose the name); an empty name or one over 80 characters: 422; not a run:
404. The list is read again once after a rename; the configuration's chosen model and save point are untouched. The
default model (`GET /api/runs/default-model?label=`) also answers with the model that CARRIES the label when no run
was started under it, so a renamed model is loaded by typing its name into the RL model label box; a run started under
that name is then numbered (`gold (2)`).

**Unchanged.** Nothing is renamed by itself; the names given at a start stay. A model from before keeps its listed
number unless an older run folder with the same name is deleted. `model_label.json` is a run file (`layout.PURPOSES`).
The label box's tooltip says the numbering.

**Tests.** `tests/workbench/test_wb_model_label.py` 6 -> 10: numbering at a start (typed label, the task's own name, a
baseline takes none, the key remembered, `output_root_of`); numbering of listed rows (renamed first, then oldest first,
a given `ota (2)` on a taken number, a baseline left out, `free_model_label`); a run from before the label (named by its
task when it saved a model, else none; a rename file wins); the rename route (the file, the manifest kept, the list and
the run rows, 409 / 422 / 404, the same name again, the default model by the new name, a later start numbered, a
numbered name is a name like any other). `model_chooser_check.mjs` 65 -> 73 (a rename section with a canned
`/api/runs/model/rename`: the Name box and the disabled button, a taken name refused and said, a free name renames -
one re-read, the card, the note, the box, the configuration untouched -, renaming back). Through `run_checks.sh`:
model_chooser 73, run_list 62 (the Model column), network_panel 42 passed. Python suite (`-n 12 --dist loadgroup`):
1,862 passed, 1 skipped; a second full run after the last change (the pre-7.159 naming), while the page was being
built: 1,862 passed, 1 skipped, 1 failed - `test_wb_training_series.py::test_a_killed_and_resumed_run_reads_whole_in_order_and_once`
("worker ended before it could be killed": a MOCK worker outran the test under that load; nothing of this record
is in it); its file passed alone, 23 tests. `tsc --noEmit` clean.

**Live**: page rebuilt 05:06 (`frontend/dist`, with 7.224; rebuilt again by the session "Task button and fine-tuning
comparison plan" with its 7.227, full browser suite green on it). 8765 restarted 05:49 on the user's word (by that session;
no run was live): seen on it read-only at 05:5x - 18 saved models, every name unique (`mscr_ota7t` ... `mscr_ota7t (8)`
oldest first, `mscr_ldo2_rgcn_gt`, `mscr_ldo2_rgcn_gt (2)`), 18 of 33 run rows with a model name, the rename route
answering (422 on an empty body); nothing renamed. Not committed.

right end, **What's missing?**; a headline and one closed "What to ask it" line. A press opens the CLI in a terminal
window of its own, in the repository, as the user starts it: no permission option, settings file, model or prompt from
