ships for its WSL agent, started with the environment the app gives that agent (read from its running app-server):
`CODEX_HOME` = the Windows user's `.codex`, `CODEX_SQLITE_HOME` = `~/.codex/sqlite`, the app's build folders first on the
PATH - so the session is in the app's history. The window: a start script in `~/.local/state/gator/agent-cli/<cli>.sh`
(written whole, then put in place), run by `wt.exe -w new new-tab --title ... wsl.exe -d <distro> -- bash -l <script>`,
else a console through PowerShell, else a Linux desktop terminal; the script ends in the user's own login shell, so the
window stays open after the CLI. The process owns nothing of the backend (own session, no streams, `WSLENV` emptied; on a
Linux desktop the Claude Code session markers are taken out). A CLI not installed is a disabled button with the reason;
a window that cannot open says why and gives the command to type (Copy). `GATOR_AGENT_CLI_LAUNCH=off`: every launch a dry
run that writes nothing (`tests/conftest.py`, `run_checks.sh`). **What's missing?** opens a window of its own (popupWindow)
with the verdict and the checklist - each item's mark, whose step ("yours" / "an agent can help") and what to do, the
three tables' actions naming Claude Code or Codex; worked out on the press only, again on the next press. The checklist
module moved to `readiness.py` and lost what only the in-tab agent read (`changeable_rows`, `required_parameter_rows`,

**Six start-B tasks** (`real:mscr_{ota5t,ota7t_w,ring3,vco_lin_c,ldo1_rgcn,ldo2_rgcn}_b`, cells `MScR/mscr_<name>_b` +
`tb_<name>_b`): the same circuit, rows, ranges and analyses, in a cell of its own, sized to a random design OF RECORD -
among the A task's Baseline simulation's random designs (ota7t_w: its run's 8 warm-ups) the valid one with the median

rows reach in 65 of 329 / 37 of 282 cases; -5 dB was never reached in 1,500. Found by the compatibility check and fixed:
a model's spec registry takes a Specs row's Scale as the metric's reference, so ota5t_b, ldo1_rgcn_b and ldo2_rgcn_b

### 7.228 GraphGPS and parameter-group cross-attention (2026-10-06; user: "Add the two encoders with all corresponding integration")

**Scope.** Added `graph_gps` and `group_cross_attention`; default stays `graph_transformer`.
GraphGPS combines parallel local terminal-aware TransformerConv messages and within-circuit global attention per
block. Parameter-group cross-attention keeps the local blocks and adds a query per group, derived from group
features, bound devices and goals. The readout attends to all device/net nodes in its circuit. Critics still bind
proposed actions to their devices before node processing. Actor/Q networks remain independent; parser, SAC,
reward and action semantics are unchanged. No novelty or transfer-superiority claim.

**Inputs.** Both new encoders optionally project random-walk return probabilities at 2, 4, 8, 16 steps (GraphGPS
starts on; group attention off). Raw structural inputs only are cached, bounded to 64 topologies / 32 MiB. The
cache keys include ordered edges, node count and the structural definition version; no learned state is cached.
Dense attention explicitly packs interleaved device/net batches by graph ID, masks keys and restores row order.
Full node attention is quadratic; group attention adds O(GN). No new dependencies.

**Integration.** Schema and validation, per-encoder settings and provenance, learner construction, all four run
modes, checkpoint architecture checks, saved-model summaries, structure roles and graph-view scopes. Frontend
selects both encoders and structural options, saves them separately, restores the complete loaded architecture,
and distinguishes physical-edge attention from global/group node weights. New presets are labelled experimental
at 48/4/4 with inherited SAC settings; architecture references are separate from hyperparameter evidence.
`examples/benchmark_encoders.py` previews an explicitly selected synthetic matrix; `--execute` writes under
`<output>/mock/<id>`. Frozen M5/M6 scripts/configuration/evidence files were not edited; their historical budgets
require explicit original encoder selections under current code. Documentation: `docs/global-encoders.md`,
README, decisions, encoder/preset guides, workbench and functionality index.

**Compatibility.** New model contracts include structural encoding and architecture version 1. Existing encoders'
contract fields, tensor names/shapes and legacy configuration hashes are preserved. The source digest is now
`061768e0806ac6c631dcb1c3ca8cfa70b4e5caaeb4a4d753b661710d1d3b26d5`; old exact resumes require historical
source, while compatible model inspection/fine_tune/frozen retain the existing checks. A full regression initially
caught extra null metadata fields in the old model chooser; those fields are now included only when present in
the saved contract, and the six chooser tests pass.

**Validation.** Initial optimizer checks: 128 passed. Focused integration: 110 passed, including mixed
topology isolation, gradients, permutations, raw cache equivalence, all run modes and attention inspection.
TypeScript and a separate Vite build passed. Browser network controls: 48 checks; graph view: 62 checks, including
new global/group modes; actual saved-model loading/structure/attention: 14 checks. The latter initially refused
its own read-only POST requests; the harness call was corrected to allow reads, then all checks passed. The current comparison CLI completed 44 synthetic evaluations, zero real simulator calls,
with both new encoders across scratch/frozen/adaptation. Final full regression: **1,957 passed, 1 skipped**, zero
failures (4 workers, `--dist loadgroup`, 289.74 s). Existing torch/Starlette deprecation warnings remain.
Browser checks total **124 passed**. `git diff --check` passed. Final regression log:
`artifacts/local/encoder-integration-preview/regression.log`. The learner-step profiler also accepts both new
encoders with the experimental starting presets; its default selection is unchanged. No timing comparison
was collected on this shared, busy machine.

**Live.** Preview built under `artifacts/local/encoder-integration-preview/dist`, tested on a separate backend with
isolated settings. The live `frontend/dist` and backend on 8765 were not changed by this task. No real circuit
simulation, paid agent call, commit or push. Pre-existing work in the shared checkout was preserved.

**Commit.** `40d1249` (10-06 08:32, pushed), by the session "Virtuoso read whole flow" from a private index, on the user's "commit and push": what this record left in the tree after 7.226, 7.225 and 7.227 - with no session to ask, its files and hunks were told apart by content (encoders, structural inputs, presets, graph-view scopes, their checks and docs) -, from the snapshot of 08:02; the final tree equals the working tree but for the user's untracked spec file. Optimizer source digest `0b0808bc...` computed on it (`061768e0...`, this record alone on `5560c01`, was never committed). ### 7.229 Fine-tuning a trained model on a new starting point: the twelve runs and what they say (2026-10-06, session "Task button and fine-tuning comparison plan"; user: "All twelve (Recommended)", then "I dont think first passing design is a good metric for showing transfer learning here" -> "Yes, adopt them", then "go on")

**The runs.** `queue.sh` of record 7.227, started 06:58 after its first start had been refused (`confirm_transfer`, see 7.227),
ran the twelve runs one after the other and ended 12:03: for each of ota5t, ota7t_w, ring3, vco_lin_c, ldo1_rgcn and
ldo2_rgcn the fine-tune arm (Mode `fine_tune` from the A run's last save point, `--confirm-transfer`) and the scratch arm,
both on the start-B task `real:mscr_<name>_b`, seed 1, 800 launches, the A run's lanes. Every run completed its 800
launches; Check now passed before and after every run (the cell equalled its task); every `run_manifest.json` names
optimizer source `4aebbfab` (the runs import a `git archive` of 5560c01, so the uncommitted 7.226 / 7.228 package edits
of that morning never reached them), seed 1, and each fine-tune run names its A run's save point (`ckpt-00000800`, the
LDOs `ckpt-00001500`). The save points of these twelve runs resume only under `4aebbfab`; fine-tune and frozen load them
under today's `0b0808bc`. Folder `artifacts/local/reviews/finetune-vs-scratch-2026-10-06/` (queue.log, run logs,
results.md / results.json from `compare.py`, the page's charts from `results_page.py`).

**The criteria were revised while the runs ran, before any verdict** (user: "I dont think first passing design is a good
metric for showing transfer learning here"): the first passing design is a single event dominated by chance (every run's
first 8 designs are random warm-ups whatever the mode; on ota5t it read 13 against 19 while the passing share right after
the warm-ups read 90 % against 6 %). Now C1 = the fine-tune arm's passing share of designs 9-108 at least 30 points above
the scratch arm's; C2 = last-third share within 5 points; C3 (LDOs) = passes at all; beside them cumulative passes by
100 / 200 / 400, "50 % by" (the end of the first 100-design window with at least half passing), mean reward early and late;
first pass a footnote. Plan section 5 and the page carry the revision (committed in dc1a9fd).

**Results** (passing share of designs 9-108; passes by 100 / 200 / 400; 50 % by; last third; reward early -> late):

| Task | Arm | Evaluations | Passing, designs 9-108 | Passes by 100 / 200 / 400 | 50 % by | Passing, last third | Reward early -> late | Working time | First pass (footnote) |

- ota5t: C1 early passing share at least 30 points above scratch: yes (90 % vs 11 %); C2 last third within 5 points: yes (96 % vs 95 %); 50 % by 100 vs 281
- ota7t_w: C1 early passing share at least 30 points above scratch: yes (93 % vs 1 %); C2 last third within 5 points: yes (95 % vs 95 %); 50 % by 100 vs 311
- ring3: C1 early passing share at least 30 points above scratch: no (16 % vs 2 %); C2 last third within 5 points: yes (32 % vs 33 %); 50 % by never vs never
- vco_lin_c: C1 early passing share at least 30 points above scratch: yes (89 % vs 5 %); C2 last third within 5 points: yes (95 % vs 80 %); 50 % by 100 vs 525
- ldo1_rgcn: C1 early passing share at least 30 points above scratch: no (27 % vs 0 %); C2 last third within 5 points: yes (7 % vs 0 %); C3 passes at the relaxed target: yes (89 designs); 50 % by never vs never
- ldo2_rgcn: C1 early passing share at least 30 points above scratch: no (4 % vs 0 %); C2 last third within 5 points: yes (12 % vs 0 %); C3 passes at the relaxed target: yes (58 designs); 50 % by never vs never

**Verdict.** By the rule as written, C1 and C2 hold together on three of six (ota5t, ota7t_w, vco_lin_c): the rule says "no
transfer". What fails on the other three is the rule's bar, not the data: C1 asks for 30 points of early passing share,
an absolute bar a hard circuit cannot reach. On ring3 the loaded model is still ahead early (16 % against 2 %); on both
LDOs it is the only arm that passes at all (89 and 58 passing designs against none in 800 of the fresh model, and none in
1,500 of the A run at -5 dB). Read without that bar, the loaded model is ahead in the first 100 own proposals on all six
circuits (by eight times or more on five) and ends as well or better on all six (vco_lin_c: 95 % against 80 %). Reported
as it stands; the rule was fixed before the runs. Not a GT or transfer-advantage claim beyond these six circuits, one seed
per arm.

**Two findings beyond the verdict.** (1) ldo1_rgcn: the loaded model transfers, then training on loses part of it - its
first 100 own proposals pass 27 % (26 designs), the last third 7 %; the relaxed HF PSRR row fails 63 % of designs in the
first 200 and 82-88 % after, while the scratch arm fails it on 98-100 % throughout. The loaded critics were trained at the
old -5 dB target; trained on, the policy drifts out of the region the relaxed target had opened. A follow-up to test:
fine-tune with `reset_critic`, or a lower learning rate. (2) ring3: both B arms end at a third of designs passing, twice
the A run's 15 % from start A; the frequency window fails about 70 % of designs throughout in all three runs - the hardest
of the six for this preset, whatever the start. Also: on vco_lin_c the scratch arm needs 525 designs to reach half passing
(the A run 291, the loaded model 100), the fine-tuned arm 95 % at the end against 80 %.

**Offered, not ordered:** 50 frozen designs of each A model on its B task (300 launches) to separate pure zero-shot
transfer from the first updates; a second seed of both arms on ring3 and ldo1_rgcn (3,200 launches); fine-tune with
`reset_critic` on ldo1_rgcn (800). The SKY130 benchmark stays parked.

**Checked.** Provenance of the twelve runs read from their manifests and status files (table above); the comparison
numbers recomputed by `compare.py` from the Parquet tables; the page's charts drawn from the same tables, their three
colours validated with the data-visualization palette check for both themes (one contrast warning in the light theme,
relieved by the direct labels and the table). No code of the workbench changed in this record; the 7.227 index row's
"no run started" is corrected here.

### 7.230 Plan: one general network from all saved experience, the six circuits from a third start, and policy drift (2026-10-06, session "Task button and fine-tuning comparison plan"; user: "next write the plan is it possible to train one general network with all the previous saved experience and then compare the six cases with different starting design parameter and starting seed from scratch and load the general network. how to avoid policy drift?")

**What.** `docs/plan-general-network.md`, plan only: nothing built, no task registered, no simulation started. Its answers,
read from the source at `2cba8c6` and the recorded runs:

- **Possible, without new simulation.** Every run archives every transition with both states (`data/transitions`,
  `data/states`); `TrajectoryLoader.transitions()` rebuilds optimizer `Transition` objects from them, and
  `SACLearner.update` accepts a batch of different circuits (it checks only reward settings, condition policy and
  origin per transition; the graph batch handles the topologies). All 30 recorded models share one feature schema,
  reward version, action version, normalizer and condition policy; they differ only in the spec schema (one per task,
  4-14 slots) and, for 8 old ota7t runs, the reward settings. Pool of record: 22 runs, 17,124 Spectre evaluations, 15
  tasks of 10 circuits under today's reward settings; the 8 ota7t runs (4,326) join only after re-scoring.
- **One shared spec registry:** the union of the 15 task registries is 34 metrics; 26 agree everywhere, 8 differ in the
  reference only (iq_a, loopgain_db, ugb_hz, psrr_10k/1m/hf_db, noise_vrms, power_w; the plan proposes one value each).
  The reference normalizes the goal input only, never the reward, so the old data re-contexts consistently; the context
  grows to 725 columns. Set per run through `run.advanced.spec_registry`; both arms of the comparison set it, so they
  differ only in the loaded weights.
- **Method of record:** offline SAC updates through the unchanged learner on the pooled transitions (batch 8, balanced
  per task, 10-20 passes, 35-70 min CPU at 7.221's 0.1 s per update), held-out checks per task (critic reward error
  against a constant and against the task's own A critic; actor proposals at recorded states; a per-task forgetting
  curve), saved as a run-shaped folder the chooser lists ("general", mode `scratch` with a `pretraining` block,
  `source_pretraining_cost_evaluations` 17,124). Optional hindsight relabelling of the 3,000 LDO -5 dB designs at the
  relaxed targets. No change to `src/analog_optimizer/`; the tool would be a `pretrain` CLI command of the workbench.
- **Comparison:** six start-C tasks (the start-B rule with the 25th-percentile random design of record, seed 2, the
  relaxed LDO rows), arms scratch and load-general at 800 launches (9,600, about 5 h), optional arm "own-circuit offline
  model" (+4,800) that separates pooling from offline training; zero-shot frozen probes first (6 x 300 on the B tasks).
  Criteria: 7.229's C1-C3 plus C4 "no drift" (last third not more than 10 points under the early share; the weakest row's
  failure share not up by more than 15 points) and C5 "general within 10 points of own". Verdict rule fixed before any run.
- **Policy drift:** defined (while adapting, and while training the general network), explained (fine_tune starts an
  empty replay and updates 8 times per new transition at 1e-3; the critic, a reward model at gamma 0, loses its shape
  elsewhere and the actor follows), and seven levers ranked: rehearsal replay (half of every batch from the pooled
  experience; a workbench engine change that revises the documented "no source replay" rule, no optimizer change),
  learning rate / update budget, `freeze_encoder`, keep the loaded critics, keep-best save point by a rule, a drift meter
  over save points (read-only), and the optimizer-side regularizers (KL / L2-SP / EWC / CQL) listed and not proposed.
- Ten decisions for the user (D1-D10) and the parked SKY130 line.

**Checks.** Read-only analysis: the registry union and its 8 conflicts computed from the 15 profiles; the contract values
counted over the 30 save points; the evaluations per task summed from the run folders. No code of the workbench
changed; the previous plan's status line says 08:09 now (it said "08:1x").

**Live.** Nothing to build or restart. Not committed when written.

paper's method, not these encoders'). Twelve registered tasks `real:graphgps_<circuit>` and `real:xatten_<circuit>`
